# Top 10 video ideas

23 Nov 2026. These are the ten videos to make next, ranked by expected impact. Each comes with a posting plan and a full production plan.

**How they were chosen**
- 8 generators proposed 48 ideas, each working from a different lens. One lens was built on the new trend research.
- The 48 ideas were merged into 21 candidates, and 5 judges scored them.
- 12 candidates were developed into full plans, and a reviewer attacked each one. Each plan was then revised to answer the review. The reviewer scores quoted below were given before those revisions.
- This file keeps the best 10 and ranks them [inference]. The order follows four things:
  - the evidence for the room type and the premise;
  - the judges' mean;
  - how well the wow reads with no text;
  - the risks each plan admits.
  Variety decided the lower places.
- It builds on `research/report.md` (the Haven Haus study) and `research/trends_nov2026.md` (what is working now, 23 Nov 2026). It replaces nothing in `research/next_videos.md`.
- Three are already being built by their own render sessions, because their evidence is strongest or their posting window is near: #1 (C2), #2 (C4) and #4 (C5).

**Labels**
- [measured]: computed from our data.
- [fact]: read from a source.
- [snapshot]: taken from a search engine's copy of a page. Indicative only.
- [inference]: a judgement.

**Names inside the plans**
- The plans refer to each other by rank and name (for example "#4 Advent Stair"). A bare "#n" always means this file's rank. The table below also gives each candidate id.
- C10 and C16 are the runners-up. C3 and C8 were scored but not developed.
- "Trends #n" is the numbered list in `research/trends_nov2026.md`. "next_videos #n" is the list in `research/next_videos.md`.

- **Seasonal posts stay inside their windows.**
  - #4 lands in the advent window (27 Nov-3 Dec).
  - #8 and #9's holiday cut land before mid-December.
  - #2 opens the "fix this layout" slot (27 Dec-10 Jan).
  - #6 and #7 sit in the January reset window. "Declutter" searches spike in early January [fact, secondary].
- **Evergreen ideas fill the gaps and can slip.** If you want more space around #1's long cut, move it to 7 Dec and push #3 and #5 back 3-4 days. If you do, skip #9's holiday cut (post only its evergreen long cut on 30 Dec), and move #2 to Fri 1 Jan (15 s) and Tue 5 Jan (long), so it stays at least 5 days after #5's long cut.
- **Cadence**: 20 posts over 9 weeks, 1-4 a week. Buffer puts the sweet spot at 3-5 a week on Instagram and 2-5 on TikTok [fact]. Add ideas from `research/next_videos.md` if output allows.
- **Post natively on each platform.** Never post a re-cut of an earlier video. The 19 s re-cut of the 19.3M kitchen got 24K views and no comments [measured].

**Clashes to watch**
- **Drawer stairs appear in five plans** (#1, #2, #3, #4, #9).
  - They are a proven beat: the 12.8M loft and the 5.5M flat both fill stair drawers [measured].
  - #4 makes them the hero; in the others they are a supporting beat.
  - Where two land within 5 days, trim the riser fill, as #3's plan already allows.
  - #9's long cut (30 Dec) sits 2-3 days from both #2 cuts (28 Dec and 2 Jan): trim #9's riser-drawer fill (Zone 2, 6.8-8.4 s) to the top drawer only.
- **#4's plan asks #1 to swap its stair drawers or post further apart.**
  - #1's 15 s cut goes out 4-5 days before #4.
  - #1's long cut, where a child climbs the stair drawers, follows #4 by 1-3 days. The fix above (long cut on 7 Dec) widens the gap.
- **#1 and #9 are both two-kids rooms.**
  - #1's plan asks for 3 weeks between kids'-room cousins; #9's asks for 1 week.
  - The calendar gives 15 days from #1's first post (8 from its long cut), so that #9's holiday cut lands before mid-December.
  - If that is too close, skip #9's holiday cut and post only its evergreen long cut in January.
- **#3 and #9 both had celery on a stair-drawer front.** #9's top stair front is now slate, its one accent.
- **#5 and #2 are bedrooms where the bed moves**, posted 5 days apart (#5's long cut on 23 Dec, #2's 15 s cut on 28 Dec). The mechanisms differ: a ceiling descent and a tile-puzzle shuffle.
- **#8 and #10 are both kitchens**, five weeks apart, with different mechanisms.

**The 15 s vs long-cut test**
- **Both cuts for every idea**: posted natively 4-7 days apart, with the same caption, except for the two weak pairs and the one caption change below.
- **Two weak pairs**:
  - #4's long cut ships only if it is ready by Wed 2 Dec.
  - #9's two cuts differ in caption and season (holiday, then evergreen).
- **One caption change**: #7's 15 s caption drops "Count the things that open", because that cut doesn't show every opening.
- **Order**:
  - The 15 s cut goes first for #1-#6, #8 and #9: it renders first, and the seasonal ideas need it.
  - For #7 and #10 the long cut goes first, to check whether the order changes the result.
- **What to compare after 7 days**:
  - Shorts: engaged views, average percentage viewed, and viewed vs swiped away.
  - TikTok: completion and rewatches.
  - Instagram: sends, saves and skip rate per reach.
- **Why test it** [measured]:
  - On Haven Haus, 8 of 11 videos of 40-60 s passed 1M views, against 0 of 11 under 30 s.
  - Two short Shorts did well because they never stopped moving: a 29 s double-desk study (1.04M) and a 24 s two-beds room (991K).
  - [re-checked today] The two-beds room has the highest motion of all 59 Shorts (15.5) and the highest camera movement of the 29 hits (11.88). One flop, a 6,836-view dimension guide, scores slightly higher on camera (12.1).
- **Read the result across all ten pairs.** One pair on its own is noise [inference].
- **Two caption tests from the trends file**:
  - Do household-premise captions (#1, #2, #3, #9) lift sends?
  - Do captions with dimensions and a save prompt lift saves?

**Measuring**
- **Before posting**: run `python3 research/hh_metrics.py <video>` on the low-res preview, then again on the final. The targets are the hits' ranges:
  - motion 7.5 or more;
  - share of clear changes 0.39 or more;
  - longest still 1.8 s or less;
  - camera movement 1.8 or more;
  - first movement by 0.4 s;
  - brightness 138-178;
  - saturation 0.17-0.38;
  - warmth 22-57;
  - length 34-46 s (long cut only).
- **Aim to miss at most one target.**
  - [measured, re-checked 23 Nov] Leaving out length, only 9 of 29 hits pass every target (6 of 29 with length).
  - The median hit misses 1 target and the median flop 5. So the targets give direction; they are not a gate.
  - The long cuts of #1 (49.1 s) and #5 (49.5 s) will miss the 46 s length target. They are still inside the top 12's 34-53 s [measured] and the owner's 40-50 s.
- **After 7 days**: add a row for each post to `research/videos.csv`.
  - Set `set` to `ours`, and fill in platform, url, date, duration_s, views, likes, comments, saves, shares, caption and room_type.
  - Put sends, average percentage viewed and skip rate in `notes`.
  - Then run `python3 research/youtube_inventory.py stats research/videos.csv --where set=ours`. Add `--metric likes`, `saves` or `shares` to rank by those.
- **Answer room requests that come with dimensions.** Commenters post them [measured], and they make the next series.

## Rules for all 10

**Originality**
- Original work only. Never copy Haven Haus's or anyone's titles, on-screen text, watermarks, end cards, music or exact designs.
- No on-screen text: no captions, labels, arrows, readable letters or numbers on props, and no end card. Captions go in the post.
- No copyrighted characters, logos or recognisable IP. Keep book spines plain, jars and boxes unlabelled, and devices and shoes free of logos.
- A different layout every video. Never repeat a whole look: change the shell, camera path, music track and accent each time, against the rest of the slate and against `rooms/bedroom` and `rooms/kitchen`.

**Structure**
- Frame 1 is the empty room, and something lands by 0.3 s [measured: 22 of 29 hits open empty]. The one exception is #10, which opens on its closed studio with the wall moving at 0.10 s.
- Build the whole room, and build it around the star feature [measured: 28 of 29 hits against 12 of 30 flops].
- 6-12 storage moments (a few 15 s cuts show fewer), with colourful contents on screen within the first 2-3 s.
- A faceless stand-in (`pipeline/figure.py`) uses the cleverest feature, at least in the long cut. No figure is ever under, on or inside the arc of a moving part.
- The camera never stops [measured: motion 8.4 against 5.1, longest still 1.4 s against 2.6 s]:
  - a glide in every station (`pipeline/camkit.py`), joined by 0.3 s swings;
    - Exceptions: #7 hard-cuts into and out of the walk-in (no swing through a wall); #10 straight-cuts into a11 and a13 (turns over 120°).
  - no still stretch over about 1.5 s;
  - a final wide that drifts into the frame-1 pose, so the video loops.
- Two cuts:
  - a 15 s cut on its own camera path, rendered first;
  - a 40-50 s long cut with a new zone every 3-8 s, as in the top 5, and the wow at about 60-70% of the runtime.

**Look**
- **Shell**: neutral white for all ten (about sRGB 242, 241, 238).
- **Wood**: light oak or birch, never walnut or dark wood.
- **Colour**: one muted accent (one per child where two children share, as in #1), plus the colour from filled storage.
- **Avoid**: cream or orange casts, saturated walls, busy patterns, and pink or red-and-gold themes.
- **Clean and bright**: brightness 138-178 the whole way through. Lights-on adds lamps; daylight may ease to no less than 85%. Use fill lights in recesses and enough samples, so nothing is grainy.

**Sound**
- A sound on every landing, plus one or two playful effects.
- Original music at about 118-120 BPM. No licensed or recognisable tracks.
- Export an SFX-only mix as well.

**Believability**
- Real dimensions; hinges, posts, castors and runners you can see; things land with weight. Nothing assembles itself or morphs: viewers are tired of AI-looking content [fact, trends file].
- Pin a plan with dimensions and "Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app."
- Keep away from what the pipeline renders badly: water, hair, faces, draped fabric, crowds and clear glass.

**Captions**
- Lead with the size and who lives there, and end with a debate question and a prompt to send or save.
- Keep every claim exact. A bedroom is a bedroom, never an apartment.
- Leave out "secret", "hidden" and "magic": titles with them run 0.82, against 1.00 without [measured, 11 titles].

**Process**
- Before any Blender time: probe stills, then a low-res preview, then `hh_metrics.py`.
- Keep look-alike videos at least 5-7 days apart (see Clashes to watch).
- Lean work: one project file per room, with a switch for the cut, reusing the bedroom and kitchen helpers.

## 1. Tape Line, Turning Wall

- **At a glance**:
  - Candidate C2. Judges' mean 8.4; reviewer score 6.5, given before this revision.
  - Being built now by the two_kids_9m2 render session.
  - Posting: the 15 s cut on Thu 26 Nov, the long cut on Thu 3 Dec.
- **Why it ranks first**:
  - It has the highest judges' mean and the strongest topic evidence [measured]: kids' rooms 1.35, "two kids" titles 1.36, and split titles up to 9.71× in 2025.
  - Its wow is one rigid move that reads with no text [inference].
  - The main risk is the 2026 slump for two-kids rooms, so the pivot has to carry the video.

### Pitch

A two-colour tape zips down the middle of an empty kids' room. Two different halves build at the same time around one shared playroom: a loft with a cave desk on the left, a mid-sleeper with a window corner on the right. At bedtime a wardrobe, already full of clothes on one side, swings 90° off the far wall onto the tape, and each child gets their own corner. In the long cut it swings back in the morning.

### Post caption (in the post, never on screen)

"This wardrobe swings 90° and gives each kid their own corner. 2 kids, 12 m² (3.6 × 3.4 m): one playroom by day, two corners at night. Which side would you pick, the loft or the window corner? Send this to your sibling."

- Pinned comment: a top-view plan with dimensions, the pivot post, casters, foot brake, swing arc and clearances, plus "Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app."
- The caption leads with the mechanism. The size and "2 kids" back it up.
- It says "own corner", not "split in two": the divider stops 0.9 m short of the door wall, so "full privacy" would draw "that's fake" replies.

### Why it should work

- [measured] **The topic has the best record in the data, but mostly from 2025.**
  - Kids' rooms run at 1.35× normal, and "two kids, one room" titles at 1.36×.
  - The split-and-privacy titles are all from 2025:
    - "Turning One Bedroom Into Two": 602K (9.71×)
    - "Divided Bed & Study Area": 813,838 (8.47×)
    - "Second Child Bedroom… Full Privacy": 653,937 (4.99×)
    - "Turn 1 Room into 2 Kids' Bedrooms": 536K (3.39×)
  - "Genius Two-Kids Room Design" (Aug 2025) has 4,256,830 views.
  - In 2026 most two-kids Shorts flopped (see Risks). So the pivot wardrobe has to carry the video, not the topic.
- [measured] **Both beds are raised: a loft at 1.4 m and a mid-sleeper at 0.9 m.**
  - Every 2026 hit was a bedroom or kids' room with a raised or loft bed.
  - The 5 worst 2026 uploads were a bay window, a wardrobe wall, two dimension guides and a two-kids room (17,591 views).
- [measured] **Hits never stop moving.**
  - Motion per 0.2 s is 8.4 for hits against 5.1 for flops. The longest still stretch is 1.4 s against 2.6 s.
  - The split rooms moved most. "Divided Bed" scored 12.6, with a 0.6 s longest still, and "Second Child" scored 13.6.
  - The 24 s "Two Beds in One Small Room" (991,555 views) scored 15.5, the highest of all 59.
  - [inference] Landing both halves at once doubles what moves in each beat.
- [snapshot, indicative] **"Two kids, one room" still stands out in 2026 on TikTok** (trends file).
  - HomeCraft's "Two kids, one room" post (22 May 2026) shows 22.5K likes. Its single-bedroom posts from Jan 2025 to May 2026 show 260-924. Its best post overall is older: 351.8K likes ("bed in the middle", Apr 2024).
  - [fact, trends file] Sends drive reach to people who don't follow you. That is why the caption ends "send this to your sibling".
- [inference] **The premise reads without words.** A line is drawn at 0.3 s, and later a whole wardrobe turns onto it.
  - Premise videos have a median of 307,650 likes against 634 for plain showcases [measured, confounded by account size].
  - The long cut plays the wow twice (the evening swing and the morning swing back), from two different angles. YouTube counts every replay as a view [fact, trends file].

### The room

- **Size and shape**: 12.2 m², a rectangle 3.6 m wide × 3.4 m deep, with a 2.7 m ceiling.
  - **Near wall (south, camera side)**: a 0.8 m pocket sliding door in the centre, so nothing swings into the room. This wall is hidden from the camera (Build notes), and no station looks at it.
  - **Left wall (west, Kid A)**:
    - a loft bed along the far 2 m, deck at 1.4 m;
    - under it, a cave desk behind an east-facing oak bookcase;
    - a vertical ladder at the loft's near end;
    - a window between the loft and the near wall, with a lift-lid window-seat toy bench under it.
  - **Far wall (north)**:
    - a floor-to-ceiling steel pivot post 0.25 m right of centre;
    - the double-sided wardrobe (1.25 m long × 0.5 m deep × 2.2 m tall, on casters, with a foot brake) stands closed along the right half;
    - behind it, hidden until the swing: B's fold-down desk and a shallow niche in the wall.
  - **Right wall (east, Kid B)**:
    - a mid-sleeper (deck at 0.9 m) along the near 2 m, with a solid raised guard at its north (wardrobe) end and the pillow at the door end;
    - five stair drawers beside it, rising toward the door;
    - the den bay under its far end holds a roll-out play table;
    - a window beyond the bed, in the corner the wardrobe frees.
  - **Floor**: a 6 cm tape inlay on the centre line, one sage strip and one blue strip. A flush brass arc track (radius about 1.18 m) runs from the wardrobe's far end and ends exactly on the tape.
  - **Plan check (my top-view block-out)**:
    - The pivot sits at the wardrobe's wall-side corner, 0.25 m right of the tape, so the locked wardrobe is centred on the tape. Swept radius: 1.35 m (√(1.25² + 0.5²)).
    - Clearances: B's bed about 16 cm (its north-west corner is 1.50 m from the pivot), and B's stair about 0.81 m. The parked play table sits further back than the bed. A's half is never swept.
    - Zero clearance would come at a wardrobe about 1.42 m long. At 1.25 m there is room for the guard rail and the bedding.
    - The slatted panel telescopes out of the wardrobe's end in two stages, 1.2 m in all, to 0.9 m from the door wall. A shared 0.9 m strip stays open at the door.
    - After the lock, the space is unequal on purpose. A gets the loft, the cave desk and a 0.55 m aisle beside the wardrobe. B gets a corner about 1.55 × 1.4 m with the window and the desk. That is the caption's question.
- **Palette**:
  - **Shell**: soft, clean white walls (no grey tint, no cream or "warm white") and a white ceiling. The bare floor is pale grey until the oak lands at 0.4 s.
  - **Wood**: light oak (the bedroom project's oak, srgb 208, 172, 130; no walnut) on the floor, bed frames, ladder, bookcase, stair-drawer fronts, hatch doors, face A's doors and the slatted panel. The big unit in the middle of the frame is oak, not white.
  - **Accent**: one muted colour per child, on small surfaces only: sage for A and muted blue for B, on each child's bedding and tape strip and nowhere else. Of the 7 paint-brand 2027 colours of the year found, 4 are greens and 2 are muted blues (trends file). No pink, no yellow theme.
  - **Colour from filled storage, warm first**:
    - terracotta, ochre, rust and green books on A's bookcase;
    - warm-toned toys in the drawers, the bench and the play table;
    - folded clothes behind face A;
    - terracotta, ochre and sage fabric bins in face B's cubbies.
  - **Timing**: the tape at 0.3 s, the oak floor at 0.4-0.8 s, then the bedding at about 2.1-2.6 s. The bookcase is full by 2.9 s in the 15 s cut and by 5.0 s in the long cut.
  - **No readable marks on props**: plain book spines and covers, no alphabet or number blocks, no printed toys, logos or lettered rugs.
- **Light**:
  - **Daylight**: soft, slightly warm light through both side windows. A gentle sun patch from B's window falls on the floor where the wardrobe swings, so the brass arc catches the light.
  - **Lights-on**: A's loft lamp clicks on, then B's wall lamp, then warm LED strips under both decks (the den bay glows). Daylight dims only to about 85%, aiming to keep brightness at 138-178.
  - **Morning (long cut only)**: the day sky returns and the lamps switch off.

### Frame 1 and the hook

- **At 0.0 s**: the empty room, seen on the centre line from about 1.6 m behind the near wall, which the camera cannot see. The camera is at about (1.8, -1.6, 2.3), tilted about 32° down (85° vertical field of view).
  - White walls and a bare pale-grey floor. B's bright window sits near the right edge; A's window is out of frame.
  - The camera is already pushing in.
- **By 0.3 s**: the sage-and-blue tape zips along the floor from the far wall to the bottom of the frame, with a zip sound. It splits the 9:16 frame top to bottom.
  - [inference] The image means "sibling territory" in any language, with no text.
- **What follows**:
  - 0.4 s: oak planks slap down on both sides at once, stopping at the tape's edges.
  - 0.9 s: the pivot post and the arc track clank down.
  - About 1.5 s: the wardrobe slams onto the far wall.
  - About 1.8-2.4 s: a big tease. The wardrobe swings about 30° off the wall on its post and clunks back. On my block-out projection the whole unit stays inside the frame.

### 15 s cut

Its own camera path, not trimmed from the long cut; it ends split and lit, with no swing back.

- **0.0-2.9 s · S1 doorway push-in**:
  - Camera: from about (1.8, -1.6, 2.3) to (1.85, -0.7, 2.15), about 0.9 m forward, turning about 6° right toward the wardrobe.
  - What lands:
    - 0.0-0.3: the tape zips.
    - 0.4-0.8: planks slap down on both sides, far to near.
    - 0.9: the post and arc track clank.
    - 0.9-1.2: the loft posts drop in whole on the left while B's frame and stair carcass slide in on the right.
    - 1.3: both decks drop together (one double thud).
    - 1.5: the wardrobe carcass with its oak doors slams onto the far wall.
    - 1.8-2.4: the tease. It swings about 30° on its post and clunks back.
    - 2.1-2.5: mattresses plus sage and blue bedding drop.
    - 2.4-2.9: the bookcase under the loft fills in one sweep of warm-coloured spines.
- **3.2-6.9 s · S2 slide along B's side**:
  - Camera: low (about 1.0 m) on A's side of the tape, looking east at B's stair and den. It slides about 0.8 m toward the door while turning about 10° right, framed on the drawers, not the wall.
  - What lands:
    - 3.3-4.0: five stair drawers open in a wave, bottom to top.
    - 3.6-4.6: toys and clothes drop in.
    - 4.7-5.2: the drawers close top to bottom.
    - 5.3-5.7: the play table rolls out of the den. 5.8: its lid lifts.
    - 5.9-6.3: toy bins drop in.
    - 6.4-6.8: the lid closes and the table rolls home. The floor is clear.
- **7.2-9.6 s · S3 orbit at the closed wardrobe**:
  - Camera: about 1.3 m high, orbiting left to right about 12° with about 0.7 m of travel, framed on face A.
  - What lands:
    - 7.3: face A's oak doors open.
    - 7.5-8.2: folded clothes drop onto the shelves, shelf by shelf.
    - 8.3-8.9: two drawers open, socks and toys drop in, and the drawers close.
    - 9.1: the doors close. 9.4: the foot brake lifts and the latch clicks.
- **9.9-12.9 s · S4 high corner in B's half**:
  - Camera: over the door end of B's bed, rising from about 2.2 m to about (3.35, 0.2, 2.5) and sliding about 0.4 m, looking north-west. The whole arc and the freed corner are in frame.
  - What happens:
    - **10.0-11.8: wow.** The wardrobe, full on face A, swings 90° on its post, its casters tracing the brass arc. The hidden face B turns toward the camera, and the wardrobe clank-locks on the tape.
    - 11.9-12.3: the panel telescopes out along the tape toward the door.
    - 12.0-12.6: coloured bins drop into face B's cubbies.
    - 12.5-12.9: B's desk flaps down off the bare wall, revealing a niche of crayons.
- **13.2-15.0 s · S5 final wide**:
  - Camera: slides about 0.5 m left and eases into the exact frame-1 pose by 15.0 s, so the loop doesn't jump. If both kids don't fit, move the frame-1 camera itself 0.2 m back, in both cuts.
  - What happens:
    - 13.3: both kids pop in sitting up in bed (static).
    - 13.4: A's lamp and the LED under the loft click on.
    - 13.6: B's lamp clicks on (chime). 13.8: the LED under B's deck.
    - 14.3: a teddy lands on B's bed, so the last second is never still.
    - 15.0: hard cut back to the empty room.

### Long cut (49.1 s)

7 zones; a new angle every 2.0-4.0 s; the wow at 29.5-31.3 s (60-64% of the runtime); the lights come on at 41 s, then a short morning coda swings the room open again; a 2 s drifting wide at the end. No end card. 10 of the 15 stations are sideways slides or orbits framed on filled storage.

- **Zone 1 · 0.0-6.2 s · Both halves land**
  - S1 0.0-3.0, doorway push-in (as in the 15 s cut, about 0.9 m):
    - tape 0.0-0.3; planks 0.4-0.8;
    - post and arc track clank 0.9; loft posts drop in and B's frame slides in 0.9-1.2;
    - double-thud decks 1.3; wardrobe slam 1.6;
    - the tease (about 30°) 1.9-2.5; mattresses and both sets of bedding 2.4-2.9.
  - S2 3.3-6.2, sideways slide north along A's loft (about 0.9 m, 8° of turn), looking left: the bookcase fills shelf by shelf, left to right, 3.5-4.9 (full by 5.0). The ladder lands at 5.2 and the pillows at 5.5.
- **Zone 2 · 6.5-12.8 s · Kid A's corner**
  - S3 6.5-9.4, push-in into the under-loft cave from its open end, with a 10° orbit:
    - the desk top lands at 6.6;
    - two drawers open at 7.1, fill with plain sketchbooks and pencil pots 7.3-8.0, and close at 8.3;
    - a desk lamp and stool land 8.6-9.0.
  - S4 9.7-12.8, low orbit around the window seat under A's window:
    - the bench lands at 9.8 and its lid lifts at 10.1;
    - toys drop in 10.3-11.1 and the lid closes at 11.4;
    - a roller blind drops at 11.8 and a backpack lands on a hook beside the window at 12.2.
- **Zone 3 · 13.1-20.0 s · Kid B's corner**
  - S5 13.1-16.2, across the tape at B's bed, sliding toward the door:
    - the five stair drawers open bottom to top 13.4-14.2;
    - they fill 13.8-15.0 and close top to bottom 15.2-15.8;
    - B's wall lamp lands at 16.0.
  - S6 16.5-20.0, low push-in at the den bay with a 10° orbit:
    - cushions and picture books drop into the den 16.6-17.3;
    - the play table rolls out 17.7-18.3 and its lid lifts at 18.5;
    - toy bins drop in 18.7-19.6.
- **Zone 4 · 20.3-28.1 s · Shared playroom**
  - S7 20.3-23.8, slow left-to-right orbit in front of the closed wardrobe:
    - face A's doors open at 20.5;
    - shelves fill with folded clothes 20.8-22.0;
    - two drawers open, fill and close 22.1-23.1;
    - the doors close at 23.5. The small hatch door stays shut.
  - S8 24.1-28.1, sideways slide (about 0.7 m, camera about 1.7 m high), framed on the play table:
    - the two kids pop in kneeling at the table at 24.2 and play until 25.7;
    - they pack the toys into it and close the lid, 25.7-26.5;
    - the table rolls home 26.5-26.9;
    - they stand and walk to their own sides, 26.7-27.6, and stop outside the arc: A at about (1.1, 1.0), B at about (2.3, 1.6).
- **Zone 5 · 28.4-35.0 s · The swing**
  - S9 28.4-32.3, high corner in B's half (rising from about 2.2 to 2.5 m, sliding about 0.4 m, looking north-west), with B standing in frame outside the arc:
    - the foot brake lifts and the latch clicks at 29.3;
    - **wow 29.5-31.3**: the wardrobe swings 90°, face B turns toward the camera, and it clank-locks on the tape;
    - the panel telescopes out along the tape in two stages, 31.4-32.1.
  - S10 32.6-35.0, slide in B's freed corner (about 0.6 m, turning about 10° left), framed on face B:
    - bins drop into face B's cubbies 32.7-33.5;
    - the fold-down desk flaps down off the bare wall 33.6-34.1, revealing a niche of crayons;
    - a desk lamp lands at 34.4;
    - B walks from (2.3, 1.6) to the hatch, 33.6-34.9 (about 1.5 m), while A walks into the aisle off screen, 33.0-35.0.
- **Zone 6 · 35.3-43.0 s · Two rooms, one hatch**
  - S11 35.3-37.6, from B's side at child eye height, orbiting toward the hatch:
    - both hatch doors open at 35.4;
    - A's book slides through into B's hands 35.6-36.2, with A visible through the opening;
    - the doors close at 36.6, and B turns and walks to the foot of the stair, 36.7-38.3 (about 1.75 m).
  - S12 37.9-40.6, side-on from A's side of the tape (looking through the open strip and the slats), slow crane up while sliding toward the door:
    - B climbs the five stair drawers, 38.4-39.9;
    - B lies down with the head at the door end, 40.0-40.5;
    - A climbs the ladder off screen.
  - S13 40.9-43.0, night wide from the frame-1 position, sliding about 0.5 m sideways:
    - the wardrobe and panel split the frame down the middle, with A on the left and B on the right, both lying in bed;
    - A's lamp clicks on at 41.1, B's at 41.4, and the deck LEDs at 41.6;
    - B's desk folds up at 42.2; daylight eases to about 85%.
- **Zone 7 · 43.3-49.1 s · Morning, back to one room**
  - S14 43.3-46.8, high corner in A's half (about (0.5, 0.4, 2.55), rising slide, looking north-east), a new angle for the second wow:
    - the day sky returns and the lamps switch off at 43.4; both kids sit up at 43.5;
    - B steps down the stair drawers and A comes down the ladder, 43.6-44.6. They stand at the foot of their steps, outside the arc: A at about (0.7, 1.1), B at about (2.45, 1.35);
    - the panel retracts 44.7-45.1; the brake and latch click at 45.2;
    - the wardrobe swings back 45.2-46.7 and clunks home. The wow plays a second time.
  - S15 47.1-49.1, final 2 s drifting wide: the camera eases into the frame-1 pose with the tape down the middle. A teddy lands on B's bed at 48.0. Then a hard cut back to the empty room.

### Storage moments (10, numbered)

1. **A's bookcase under the loft**: fills left to right with warm-coloured books.
2. **A's desk**: two drawers open, fill with plain sketchbooks and pencil pots, and close.
3. **A's window-seat toy bench**: the lid lifts, toys drop in, and the lid closes.
4. **B's stair drawers**: five drawers open in a wave bottom to top, fill with clothes and toys, and close top to bottom.
5. **B's den bay under the mid-sleeper**: cushions and picture books land, then the play table rolls out.
6. **The play table**:
   - Its lid lifts and toy bins drop in.
   - Before the swing the toys go back, the lid closes, and the table rolls home, clearing the floor.
7. **Wardrobe face A** (facing the room by day, A's aisle by night): two 0.4 m oak doors open, shelves fill with folded clothes, two drawers fill, and the doors close. It is full before the wardrobe swings.
8. **Wardrobe face B** (hidden against the wall until the swing): open cubbies fill with coloured fabric bins.
9. **B's fold-down desk**: on the wall the wardrobe leaves, a flap folds down into a desk and reveals a shallow niche. It folds up again before the swing back.
10. **The hatch**: a small cubby through the wardrobe at child height (0.8-1.1 m), with a door on each face. Both doors open, a book passes through, and they close.

- The 15 s cut shows six: 1, 4, 6, 7, 8 and 9.
- The telescoping slatted panel is the transformation, not a storage moment.

### The person moment

- **The figures**: two faceless children made with figure.py (`'child'`, about 1.15 m), using its existing `fig_sage` colour for A and `fig_blue` for B.
- **Shared (24.2-27.6 s)**:
  - Both kneel at the play table (`crouch`) and play.
  - They pack the toys away and the table rolls home. Then they walk to their own sides and stop outside the arc. B's mark, (2.3, 1.6), is 1.77 m from the pivot, against a 1.35 m arc.
- **The cleverest feature in use (33.6-36.6 s)**:
  - B walks to the hatch at a child's pace (about 1.5 m in 1.3 s) while A walks into the aisle.
  - Both doors open, and A's hand pushes a book through to B, who takes it with a custom reach pose (arm forward about 80°).
  - Hand contacts are approximate (handoff), so frame it at medium distance and check it with a probe still.
  - [inference] This answers "own corner, but still together", and it is what people send to siblings.
  - If time runs short, cut this first: the open strip by the door means some will say "just walk around".
- **The step drawers in use (38.4-40.5 s)**:
  - B climbs the five 0.15 m treads to bed. A child's `step_up` raises the foot only about 0.13 m (handoff), so use a high-knee version (hip about 75°, knee about 95°). Alternate it with `mirror()` and raise `floor` by 0.15 m per tread. Check foot clearance with a probe still.
  - figure.py has no lying pose, so place the lying figures with a root transform (`M`).
  - The second comment under the 19.3M kitchen calls its step drawer "genius" (report section 5).
- **Safety rules**:
  - No figure is inside the arc while the wardrobe moves.
  - Nobody is in a bed during either swing. The swing back happens in the morning, after both kids are out of bed and standing outside the arc.
- **15 s cut**: both kids pop in sitting up in bed at lights-on (static and cheap). Use a long-sit pose (hips about 85°, knees straight) with `seat` at the mattress top. B sits mid-bed with a book so both fit the final wide.

### Sound

- **Landing sounds**:
  - `tick` for books, toys and bins;
  - `thud` for decks and carcasses (the two decks land as one double thud);
  - `swish` for drawers, doors, lids and the panel;
  - `chime` for the lamps.
- **Metal, for believability**: a short clank when the post and track land (0.9 s), a click at the brake and latch, and a clank at each lock. Use a high-pitched `thud` for the clank.
- **Playful effects (two)**:
  - the tape zip at 0.0-0.3 s (a `swish`);
  - a long rising whoosh under each swing.
  - An optional third: a tiny "boop" as the book pops through the hatch.
- **Music**: original, 120 BPM. The pipeline default is 126, so set `music.json` to `{"bpm": 120}`.
  - One bar is 2.0 s, close to the 1.8 s swing. Landing the lock on a downbeat is optional: the hits' cuts don't follow the beat [measured].
- **What needs a code change**:
  - Today music.py plays a fixed 0.3 s swish, sets tick pitch by event order (980 + 140 × (i mod 6) Hz) and has no duck window. Its loader also drops any per-event settings in frames.json.
  - So the long whoosh under each swing, a 3 dB music duck under the swing and ticks that rise row by row need one small change: pass an optional per-event `sfx` dict through from frames.json (`build()` already honours a `whoosh` length and a `freq`), and add an optional duck window to music.json.
  - music.py is shared by every session, so coordinate the change. If it is not agreed, drop these three effects and use the stock swish and thud.
- No licensed audio. Also export the SFX-only mix for adding a platform sound.

### Risks and fixes

- **The topic is hit-or-miss in 2026.** [measured, dates estimated]
  - 5 of 8 two-kids or second-child Shorts from 2026 ran at 0.49-0.71×: 17,521; 17,591; 19K; 23K; and 17,879 (27 Oct 2026, 44 s). The other 3 ran at 1.36-3.14×.
  - In 2026 H2, 4 of those 5 flopped (median about 0.51×), and kids' rooms overall fell to 0.71× (n=9).
  - The 17,521 flop (Sep 2026) already followed the formula: a whole room, neutral, motion 7.5, camera 3.18. Its first 12 s stayed on one bed. The 17,591 flop was pink, opened on old furniture, and had saturation 0.12.
  - 12 m² is not visibly tiny. The size-titled bedroom and kids'-room hits named 6-9 m² (8 m² kids' room 5,673,214; 9 m² study 1.04M); the one larger size title that hit, the "24 m² apartment" (5.5M), drew a title fight.
  - Fix: let the pivot carry the video from the first seconds. The big tease at about 2 s stays fully in frame, the caption leads with the mechanism, both halves land from 0.4 s, colour arrives early, and there is no pink.
- **"Fake or unsafe" comments** (pinched fingers, tipping, weight, "it will crush the kid").
  - The wardrobe is 1.25 m long, leaving about 16 cm to B's bed. A solid guard sits at the bed's north end, and the pillow is at the door end.
  - Land the post and arc track with a clank by 0.9 s, and keep the casters, the foot brake and the latch visible.
  - Swing slowly (1.8 s out, 1.5 s back) with a soft settle, only after the floor is cleared and nobody is in a bed or inside the arc.
  - Pin the plan with the arc and the clearances.
- **"That's not privacy"**: the divider covers only the far 2.45 m, and A could see over a low top.
  - Lower A's deck to 1.4 m, so A's eyes, sitting up, stay below the 2.2 m wardrobe top. Make the panel as tall as the wardrobe and run it to 0.9 m from the door wall.
  - The caption says "own corner". The leftover gap and the unequal floor space become the argument people enjoy.
- **Collisions**: probe mid-swing frames: long cut 29.9, 30.4, 30.9 and 45.9 s; 15 s cut 10.4, 10.9 and 11.4 s.
  - Probe the panel at full reach next to B's stair drawers pulled out (about 10 cm gap).
  - Fold B's desk up before the swing back (42.2 s).
- **The colour risk is grey and cool, not too much colour.** Kitchen v2 measured saturation 0.11 and warmth 14.5.
  - Face A's doors and the panel are oak, storage fills warm first, and blue stays on B's bedding and tape strip.
  - Run `hh_metrics.py` on the first preview (saturation 0.17-0.38, warmth 22-57). If it is still low, apply `--grade 1.35,0.03` in `mkvideo_png.py`, as the handoff does.
- **The camera score**: `hh_metrics.py` scores the quietest tiles, and a push-in toward a blank wall barely changes them.
  - Most stations are sideways slides or orbits framed on filled storage: 10 of 15 in the long cut and 4 of 5 in the 15 s cut. The S1 push is about 0.9 m, not 0.4 m.
  - A teddy lands inside each final wide so the end never sits still for more than 1.8 s.
- **Readable marks on props**: books, sketchbooks, crayons, toys and the backpack stay plain (Palette). Check every close storage station at full resolution.
- **Freshness**: the loft, stair drawers, fold-down desk and a child climbing stair drawers repeat the kit of Haven Haus's 12.8M loft video.
  - The pivot wardrobe and the tape are new. Keep the post, the brass arc or the wardrobe in every station where the framing allows (all but A's cave and window seat). Never copy that video's layout.
- **Slate overlap**: this is a close cousin of C3, a candidate that was scored but not developed (and of #9 Count the Beds and #6 Pull One Book). Ship only one split-kids room, with the others at least 3 weeks apart.
- **Review's sample caption not used**: "One wardrobe turns one room into two" is too close to Haven Haus's title "Turning One Bedroom Into Two", which breaks the owner's no-copying rule. The caption leads with the mechanism in different words.
- **Panel: two review fixes combined**: one fix asked for a 1.0 m panel sliding 0.9 m (ending 1.2 m from the door wall), another for a panel reaching 0.9 m from the door wall. A two-stage panel (two 0.65 m leaves, 1.2 m reach) does both inside the 1.25 m wardrobe.
- **Sixth storage moment in the 15 s cut**: the review suggested A's desk drawers or the toy bench, but neither can be seen from the 15 s stations. B's fold-down desk, which is in the wow shot, is the sixth instead.
- **No overhead cutaway**: a camera above the room would need the ceiling hidden, which also blanks the top of frame 1. High corners do the job instead: B's half for the first swing, A's half for the second. On my block-out projection each one frames the whole arc.

### Build notes

- **Where to build it**: the two_kids_9m2 render session has switched to this concept (production/sessions/two_kids_9m2_update1.md). Build it in that session's room folder, and reuse its sliding slatted partition for the two panel leaves.
- **Plan coordinates** (metres; x east, y north):
  - Room: x 0-3.6, y 0-3.4. Door (pocket sliding): x 1.4-2.2.
  - Tape: x 1.77-1.83, a 13 mm inlay (z -0.012 to +0.001). It sits right on the bare subfloor at 0.3 s and is flush once the planks land.
  - Wardrobe:
    - pivot at (2.05, 3.35);
    - closed: x 2.05-3.30, y 2.85-3.35;
    - locked: x 1.55-2.05, y 2.10-3.35;
    - inside: face A 0.23 m deep, a 4 cm panel slot, face B 0.23 m deep;
    - face A, from the pivot end: a 0.45 m column (hatch 0.12-0.40 m from the pivot end, 0.8-1.1 m high, with two drawers below), then two 0.4 m doors over shelves. The doors fit the 0.55 m aisle when locked.
  - Panel: two 0.65 m leaves about 2.15 m tall, each with a caster at its outer end on the tape. 1.2 m reach in all, to y 0.90.
  - Caster arc: radius about 1.18 m, from (3.20, 3.10) to (1.80, 2.20).
  - A's side:
    - loft: x 0-1.0, y 1.35-3.35, deck at 1.4 m;
    - bookcase: x 0.75-1.0, y 1.9-3.35;
    - ladder at the loft's near end, x about 0.5-0.95;
    - window: y 0.3-1.2, with the window-seat bench at x 0-0.45, y 0.3-1.2.
  - B's side:
    - bed frame: x 2.67-3.6, y 0.03-1.98, deck at 0.9 m, solid guard at the north end;
    - stair: x 2.22-2.67, y 0.2-1.2, five treads at 0.15, 0.30, 0.45, 0.60 and 0.75 m, rising toward the door; the drawers pull 0.3 m toward the tape (to x 1.92);
    - den bay: y 1.2-1.98;
    - window: y 2.1-2.75 (clear of the closed wardrobe);
    - fold-down desk: x 2.5-3.25.
  - The wardrobe's back stands 5 cm off the wall so the folded desk fits behind it.
  - Figure marks: parked A (1.1, 1.0), B (2.3, 1.6); at the hatch A (1.3, 3.1), B (2.25, 3.1); morning A (0.7, 1.1), B (2.45, 1.35). All are outside the arc.
- **Reuse from `rooms/bedroom/project.py`**:
  - the shell, windows and materials (its wall `srgb(238, 233, 225)` is a warm white, so use a soft, clean white instead), plus oak `wood_mats` (srgb 208, 172, 130) and `sage`;
  - `cloth()` for the duvets, `pillow()`, `stack()` for books and folded clothes, `plant()`;
  - `drawer_fn(..., base=...)` for drawers that ride a moving parent;
  - the `door_z` hinge pattern (`about(hinge, R(...))`) for the wardrobe swing and the desk flap;
  - the `lights()` slot pattern for the lamps and LED strips;
  - `camkit.hold_camera` for the stations.
- **Reuse from `rooms/kitchen/project.py`**: the pull-out pantry slide with its contents riding along (for the play table and the panel leaves), and the floor-row slap-down.
- **New pieces**:
  - the two-strip tape inlay;
  - the steel post with hinge collars, the flush brass arc track and the foot brake;
  - the double-sided wardrobe (doors, shelves, drawers, cubbies, bins, the hatch and its two doors);
  - the two-stage slatted panel;
  - the 1.4 m loft with its ladder, bookcase and desk;
  - the mid-sleeper with its five stair drawers, guard and den bay;
  - the play table, window-seat bench, fold-down desk and niche, and the lamps;
  - the two figures.
- **Key technique**: one parent transform for the swing. Compose every wardrobe child's own animation with it (doors, drawers, bins, clothes, hatch doors, panel leaves), the way drawer contents ride their drawer. Add `Timeline.settle` (`tl.settle`) at each lock.
  - Build B's pieces from their own coordinates. If you ever mirror a mesh with negative scale, flip its faces back the right way (re-wind its triangles).
- **Camera**:
  - Hide the south wall from the camera but keep its bounce light. That is a few lines in `blender_render.py`, the way lights already get `visible_camera = False`. The file is shared, so coordinate. Fallback: switch the wall off with `appear` only in S1, S13 and the final wides, and check the light change at those swings.
  - The ceiling stays visible. Both wow stations are high corners at 2.55 m or lower: B's half at about (3.35, 0.2, 2.5) looking north-west, and A's half at about (0.5, 0.4, 2.55) looking north-east. The wardrobe top is at 2.2 m; never fly through geometry.
  - `camkit` uses one field of view for every station. Keep 85°.
  - A 9:16 frame at 85° sees only about 54.5° across. Probe frame 1, the tease, both swings and the 15 s final wide with `ROOM_REST=1` before animating.
- **Hard to render, and avoided**: no faces or hair (faceless figures); duvets as simple puffed cloth; roller blinds instead of curtains; no water; no crowds.
- **Effort: medium-high.**
  - It is much bigger than the bedroom project (6 stations, no figures). It has two figures (walk, crouch, climb, handover, lying), a double-sided wardrobe with nested doors, drawers, bins, a hatch and a two-stage panel, a fold-down desk, and 20 camera stations over two timelines.
  - Use one project file with a cut switch (`ROOM_CUT=long`), each timeline with its own `HOLDS`.
  - Render time, from the README's figure of about 35 s per frame on 4 CPU cores at 720x1280. The camera never stops, so no frames are copied and these are the real figures:
    - long cut: about 1,228 frames, about 11.9 h;
    - 15 s cut: about 375 frames, about 3.6 h.
  - If behind schedule, cut the hatch handover first, then the play-table kneel.
- **Before posting**: run `python3 research/hh_metrics.py <video>`. Targets: motion 7.5 or more, longest still 1.8 s or less, camera movement 1.8 or more, saturation 0.17-0.38, warmth 22-57, brightness 138-178. Treat them as direction, not a gate: only 9 of 29 hits pass every target.
- **Posting**: it is evergreen.
  - The session builds the 15 s cut first, so post it first, about 26-27 Nov, on a different day from the advent post (27 Nov to 3 Dec).
  - Post the long cut about a week later, then compare the two after 7 days.
  - Keep it at least 3 weeks from any other split-kids concept.

## 2. Same Bed, Better Room

- **At a glance**:
  - Candidate C4. Judges' mean 8.0; reviewer score 6, given before this revision.
  - Being built now by the same_bed_better_room render session.
  - Posting: the 15 s cut on Mon 28 Dec, the long cut on Sat 2 Jan.
- **Why it ranks here**:
  - Its own plan calls it a test, because the topic is crowded: the newest bed-direction title ran 0.82 [measured].
  - It still ranks second because it is the only idea that combines all of these:
    - the strongest room type (bedrooms 1.51) [measured];
    - a measured title family (bed-placement or tatami titles: median 1.74, 13 of 15 above normal) [measured];
    - the 2026 raised-bed pattern [measured];
    - a loop that is its own before-and-after [inference].

### Pitch

Seen from above, an empty 9.3 m² bedroom fills in about a second with the layout most people have. The bed sits dead centre, the wardrobe at its foot bonks it every time the doors open, and the desk is wedged at the bed's corner. Then the same pieces slide like a tile puzzle into a better plan, and the bed rises on one storage platform. The room is for a couple: two pillows, two sets of clothes. The last frame uses the first frame's camera, so every loop is a before-and-after.

### Post caption (in the post, never on screen)

"Send this to whoever gets the wall side. Same 3 × 3.1 m bedroom (9.3 m²), same bed, same wardrobe, same desk. With the bed in the middle, the wardrobe doors hit the end of the bed and the desk chair can't pull out. Move the bed to the corner and raise it 1 m: the wardrobe goes full height, the desk gets the window, and a 1.4 m shirt rail slides out from under the bed. The big addition is one platform; the full list is pinned. Middle or corner? Save this before you move your bed."

- Pinned comment:
  - before and after plans with dimensions, including the room door hinged on its west jamb;
  - what stayed (bed, mattress, wardrobe, desk, chair) and what was added (one platform with stairs, a deep drawer, the pull-out rail and a headboard ledge; a top cupboard on the wardrobe; one shelf over the window);
  - "The rail and the wardrobe doors are used one at a time.";
  - "Changing the sheets: the mattress slides 30 cm toward the aisle.";
  - "In real life the bed's legs come off and the frame sits in a recess in the deck.";
  - "Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app."

### Why it should work

- [measured, videos.csv] Bed placement is a strong but crowded title family.
  - 15 bed-placement or tatami titles: era-adjusted 0.43 to 17.54, median 1.74, 13 of 15 above 1.00. With the three raise-the-bed titles (10.39, 7.81, 2.53): 18 titles, median 2.72.
  - Two are below the norm: "Small Bedroom? Flip the Bed, Double the Space!" (0.43) and the newest, "If your bedroom layout isn't enough, try changing the direction of your bed." (0.82, est. 16 Nov 2026).
  - The closest match shows a normal layout first, then rebuilds it: "The small bedroom felt twice as spacious after the bed wasn't placed in the middle!" (Aug 2026), 536,285 views, era-adjusted 16.75, 49 s, motion 7.5, longest still 1.2 s, camera 4.26.
- [measured] It is a whole bedroom that ends on a raised bed:
  - bedrooms run at 1.51 times normal;
  - 28 of 29 hits build or transform a whole room, against 12 of 30 flops;
  - every 2026 hit was a bedroom or kids' room with a raised or loft bed. "Be bold! Raise the bed by 90 centimeters in a small bedroom!" got 343,222 views (era-adjusted 10.39).
- [measured, weak] Problem-first openings are thin evidence. 3 of the 59 open on a problem: 2 hits and 1 flop (50,604 views).
  - The hits are the 1,590,288-view kitchen with red X marks (6.47) and the 982,327-view "bathroom door facing the bed" bedroom (6.42).
  - That bedroom has the lowest motion (3.9) and camera score (0.77) of any hit, so copy its opening, not its camera.
  - The only overhead plan-view opening among the 59 was a hit (695,010 views, 4.54), but it measured grey and cool (saturation 0.13, warmth 16.1). So the made-up ochre bed lands at 0.3 s.
- [fact, search snapshots: indicative] The debate works outside the channel, but the peak is old.
  - HomeCraft's "Who still puts the bed in the middle of a small bedroom?" is its best post (351.8K likes, 759 comments), from Apr 2024. Its single-bedroom posts from Jan 2025 to May 2026 got 260-924 likes (its "Two kids, one room" post got 22.5K) [snapshot], and there is no figure for its 1 Jun 2026 "Stop centering the bed".
  - An architect fixing a viewer's tiny bedroom layout passed 2.1M views.
  - The trends file names "two kids, one room" as the topic still breaking out. Treat this video as the Dec-Jan "fix this layout" test.
- [fact, trends file] Sends drive reach to non-followers (Mosseri). [inference, trends file] Premises that name a household and "send this to…" captions should drive sends. [inference] Two pillows, two sets of clothes and "who gets the wall side" turn a layout debate into a send.
- [fact] YouTube counts every replay as a view. [inference] Ending on the frame-1 camera makes each replay a before-and-after.
- [measured] No text is needed. Comments are practicality debates in many languages, and viewers notice playful sound ("the Scooby-Doo sound effects", 214 likes), so a bonk can show the problem. [inference] "Be Bold — Stop Putting the Bed in the Center of a Small Bedroom!" ran at only 1.015, the 3rd weakest of the 15, so the puzzle motion has to carry the video, not the slogan.

### The room

- **Size and shape**: 9.3 m², 3.0 m east-west × 3.1 m north-south, 2.7 m ceiling, 0.12 m walls. Room coordinates: x runs east from the west wall (0-3.0 m), y runs north from the door wall (0-3.1 m).
  - **The pieces**: a double bed with a 1.5 × 2.1 m frame and a 1.4 × 2.0 × 0.2 m mattress; a 1.0 × 0.55 m wardrobe, 2.0 m tall, with two 0.5 m doors; a 0.8 × 0.5 m desk and a low-back chair that tucks fully under it.
  - **South (door) wall**: 0.8 m door at the west end (x 0.15-0.95), hinged on the west jamb and opening inward. Its swing (x 0.15-0.95, y 0-0.8) stays clear of everything.
  - **North (window) wall**: 1.2 m window at the west end (x 0.15-1.35, sill 0.85 m, top 2.25 m). After: the desk centred under it (x 0.35-1.15, y 2.6-3.1) and a shelf above at 2.35 m.
  - **East side (after)**:
    - the platform bed: x 1.5-3.0, y 1.0-3.1, deck at 1.0 m. The frame sits in a recess, so the mattress lies on the deck and its top is at 1.2 m;
    - a headboard ledge, part of the platform, along the north wall over the bed head;
    - stairs at its foot: x 1.5-2.25, treads y 0.25-1.0, steps at 0.25, 0.5 and 0.75 m. Their drawers pull out west;
    - a deep drawer in the platform's south face beside the stairs: x 2.3-3.0, pulling out 0.8 m;
    - the pull-out rail unit inside the platform's west side: 1.4 m long (y 1.05-2.45), sliding out 0.55 m.
  - **West wall (after)**: the old wardrobe at y 1.0-2.0, raised to 2.7 m with an oak top cupboard.
  - **Result**: a clear aisle 0.95 m wide (wardrobe front at x 0.55 to the platform at x 1.5), running 1.55 m from the wardrobe's south end to the tucked chair. 1.5 m of sitting headroom above the mattress. The climb is 0.25 m from the top step to the deck, then 0.2 m onto the mattress.
  - **Clearances in the finished room** (worked out from these sizes; re-check in the collision test):
    - wardrobe doors at 90° reach x 1.05 and stay 0.55 m from the chair;
    - the open rail reaches x 0.95 and stops 10 cm short of the chair;
    - the open rail and an open wardrobe door would overlap, so they are used one at a time (pinned);
    - stair drawers reach about x 1.0, clear of the door swing; the open deep drawer clears the stairs by 5 cm.
  - **Before (the same pieces)**:
    - the bed truly centred on the window wall: x 0.75-2.25, y 1.0-3.1, 0.75 m on each side. Its headboard covers half the window;
    - the wardrobe on the door wall, just east of the door (x 1.05-2.05, front at y 0.55). The bed's foot is 0.45 m away, so its 0.5 m doors stop at about 64°;
    - the desk on the east wall (x 2.5-3.0, y 0.75-1.55), right at the bed's foot corner. The half-pulled-out chair sits 5 cm from the bed and can't move.
- **Palette**:
  - Shell: clean white. Not warm white or cream: those sit at the edge of the cream-cast zone.
  - Wood: light oak for the floor, platform, steps, desk, wardrobe doors and top cupboard. No walnut.
  - Blue is moved off the furniture: one muted blue pillow and a blue throw on the bed.
  - Colour from filled storage, two sets for the couple: rust, mustard, terracotta and white for one; sage, navy, denim and light grey for the other. Keep the warm set on the side most in frame.
  - An ochre duvet with one white and one blue pillow, on screen from 0.3 s; plain white and tan shoes; solid-colour book spines.
  - No patterns. A plain jute-coloured runner, long cut only.
- **Light**:
  - No Blender sun for this project: the bridge's sun is static and cannot fade.
  - The window beam is a roomkit spot light outside the window. The open-top "sky" in the overhead shots is a set of roomkit area lights above the room. The Blender export animates both lights every frame.
  - Around the ceiling switch, cross-fade the overhead area lights against hidden interior fills over about 0.5 s, so the descent never darkens. Target brightness 138-178.
  - The window backdrop plane is hidden while the camera is above 2.7 m.
  - Lights on: warm lamps are added on top of the daylight, never replacing it. They are a lamp on the headboard ledge, LED strips under the platform lip and stair edges, a desk lamp and a wardrobe interior strip.

### Frame 1 and the hook

- **0.0 s**: the empty room from a high three-quarter angle, looking about 68° down from the south (never straight down), with a 48° field of view. The room fills about 85-90% of the frame width. The camera is already circling slowly. Three full-height white walls, a south wall cut down to 0.9 m that shows the door gap, an oak floor with a patch of window light, and pale textured ground beyond.
- **0.3 s**: the double bed drops dead centre against the window wall with a heavy thud, already made up: ochre duvet, one white and one blue pillow.
- **0.6 s**: the oak wardrobe slides onto the door wall beside the door.
- **0.9 s**: its doors swing, bonk the foot of the bed at about 64° and wobble.
- **1.1 s**: the desk and chair land on the east wall at the bed's foot corner. The chair tries to pull out and knocks the bed.
- **1.3 s**: the shuffle starts.
- No lines, labels or red X.

### 15 s cut

- **0.0-4.5 s · S1 overhead, one continuous orbit**:
  - 0.3-1.1 s: the bad layout lands.
  - 1.3-1.7 s: the chair tucks in and the desk slides 0.67 m south (clack).
  - 1.8-2.5 s: the bed slides 0.75 m east into the corner (scrape, then a heavy clack).
  - 2.6-3.3 s: the wardrobe glides into the empty west half, turns a quarter and lands against the west wall (two clacks).
  - 3.4-4.4 s: the desk slides west along the door wall and turns a quarter in the open floor inside the door (clack). It runs up the new 0.95 m aisle with 7.5 cm to spare on each side, then slides under the window (clack at 4.4 s).
  - Camera: circles the room centre at about 5° per second, 68° down, 48° field of view. No stop and no swing.
- **4.5-7.0 s · S2 drop, rise, descent**:
  - 4.5-5.0 s: the camera drops fast to about 35° down, just above wall-top height, south of the room.
  - 5.0-6.4 s: a low, slow sideways glide. The full-size oak platform rises as one lift under the bed, carrying it to 1.0 m (5.1-5.9 s), and the bed's legs fold up like landing gear. Three steps stack at its foot (5.9-6.4 s).
  - 6.4-7.0 s: the camera finishes the descent to eye level just inside the door as the field of view widens to 85°. The stair drawers pop out full of knitwear and shoes, then close (6.5-7.0 s).
- **7.3-10.7 s · S3 the aisle, seen from the window end looking south**:
  - 7.4 s: an oak top cupboard drops onto the wardrobe with a thud, taking it to the ceiling.
  - 8.0 s: both wardrobe doors swing wide open with no bonk.
  - 8.2-8.6 s: the wardrobe's hanging bar fills with both sets of clothes.
  - 8.7 s: the doors close.
  - **8.9-9.8 s, wow**: the 1.4 m rail glides out of the platform side, loaded with shirts, across the aisle.
  - 10.3 s: the rail glides back.
  - Camera: slow push-in down the aisle, leaning left to follow the rail.
- **11.0-15.0 s · S4 wide, from the door corner looking north-east**:
  - 11.0-11.6 s: the headboard ledge fills with books, a plant and a lamp.
  - 12.2 s: lights on.
  - Slow drift to 13.2 s.
  - Then the camera tilts down and rises out of the room, and the field of view narrows back to 48°. It arrives at the exact frame-1 position at 15.0 s, circling at the same speed, so the loop cuts to the empty room and the bad layout lands again.

### Long cut (45 s)

The same opening. The bed stays on the floor after the shuffle until 13 s, so viewers wait to see where the storage goes.

- **0.0-5.0 s · overhead, one continuous orbit**: the bad layout lands (0.3-1.1 s); the same shuffle, a little slower, 1.3-4.8 s. From 3.0 s the orbit eases from 68° to about 58° down.
- **5.0-7.0 s · the descent** to eye level by the door, with the field of view widening from 48° to 85°. The bed sits low in its corner.
- **7.0-13.0 s · window desk**:
  - A, 7.0-9.8 s, push-in from behind the chair: the desk drawers slide out and fill, and the shelf above the window fills with books and boxes.
  - B, 10.1-13.0 s, slide along the window: the chair rolls out and the lamp lands.
- **13.3-19.0 s · bed corner**:
  - C, 13.3-16.0 s, low circle: the full-size platform rises as one lift under the bed to 1.0 m (13.5-14.5 s) as its legs fold up; then the steps stack (14.6-15.4 s).
  - D, 16.3-19.0 s, slide along the stairs: the stair drawers fill and close (16.4-17.6 s); the deep drawer slides out with a suitcase and a spare duvet (17.8-18.9 s).
- **19.3-24.0 s · wardrobe wall**:
  - E, 19.3-21.4 s, push-in facing west: the top cupboard drops onto the wardrobe with a thud (19.4-20.0 s). The doors swing wide with no bonk (20.1 s), and the wardrobe's hanging bar fills with both sets of clothes (20.3-21.2 s).
  - F, 21.7-24.0 s, slide along the wardrobe toward the desk: two drawers fill with folded T-shirts (21.8-22.8 s); the top cupboard opens on boxed spare bedding; all doors close at 23.4 s.
- **24.3-31.0 s · headboard, sheets and wow**:
  - G, 24.3-27.3 s, rising glide up the stairs: the headboard ledge fills (24.5-25.5 s). The mattress slides 30 cm toward the aisle and back (25.8-26.9 s), showing how the sheets get changed.
  - H, 27.6-31.0 s, low in the aisle: **the 1.4 m rail glides out fully loaded (27.8-29.4 s, 62-65% of the runtime)**, with the camera travelling alongside. It glides back at 30.4 s.
- **31.3-38.6 s · the person**:
  - J1, 31.3-34.6 s: a tracking dolly at hip height follows the figure in from the door.
  - J2, 34.9-38.6 s: a slow push-in from the window end down the aisle, leaning toward the rail.
- **38.9-43.0 s · K, wide from the high south-east corner, looking north-west**: slow drift; lights on at 39.4 s; from 41.0 s the camera rises out of the room.
- **43.0-45.0 s · drifting overhead wide**, arriving at the frame-1 position at 45.0 s with the field of view back at 48°. No end card.

### Storage moments

1. Window desk: two drawers slide out and fill with plain notebooks and pens; the shelf above the window fills with books and boxes.
2. Stair drawers: three steps stack at the platform foot. Each one pulls out sideways full of knitwear and shoes, then closes.
3. Deep drawer beside the stairs: slides out 0.8 m with a suitcase and a spare duvet.
4. The old wardrobe goes full height: an oak top cupboard drops onto it with a thud, taking it from 2.0 to 2.7 m. The doors open wide (no bonk), the wardrobe's hanging bar fills with clothes and two drawers fill with folded T-shirts.
5. Top cupboard: its doors open on boxed spare bedding (long cut, F, 21.7-24.0 s).
6. Headboard ledge: fills with books, a small plant and a lamp.
7. The 1.4 m pull-out hanging rail: the platform side glides out 0.55 m with about 12 shirts and blouses, no jackets (the wow).

- The platform is a build, not a storage moment: one full-size oak lift rises under the same bed to 1.0 m, the bed's legs fold up and the frame settles into the deck.

### The person moment (long cut only, 31.3-38.6 s)

A faceless stand-in adult (`fg.figure`, default light grey).

- 31.5-32.7 s: walks in through the door, along the path that used to be jammed.
- 32.8 s: opens the wardrobe all the way with no bonk, the payoff of the opening gag; closes it at 33.8 s.
- 35.2-35.8 s: stands in the 0.95 m aisle and stretches both arms overhead (the `reach_both` preset). Arms out wide would hit the furniture.
- 36.0-36.6 s: steps back to the south end of the aisle.
- 37.0-38.5 s: pulls the rail out by its end handle and lifts a shirt off the near end. The rail glides back at 38.6 s.
- The figure stays at the south end of the aisle, shirt in hand, through lights-on and the final overhead view, so the last frame shows the room in use.
- The 15 s cut has no figure. People appear in 4 of the top 5, but also in three sampled flops, so on their own they do not separate hits from flops [measured].

### Sound

- Landings:
  - a heavy thud for the bed; thuds for the wardrobe and desk;
  - a dry wooden clack for each shuffle move, like puzzle tiles, and a long scrape for the bed's slide;
  - three rising thunks as the steps stack; ticks for small items;
  - a swish and a soft click for the rail.
- Playful effects (two):
  - a dull bonk with a springy wobble at 0.9 s, then the chair's knock at 1.1 s;
  - at the no-bonk moment (8.0 s in the 15 s cut, 20.1 s in the long cut), a bright two-note chime where the bonk used to be, and a softer echo when the figure opens the wardrobe (32.8 s).
- All effects are synthesised in `pipeline/music.py`: no sampled cartoon sound library, and no melody or sound that echoes a famous puzzle game.
- Music: original, 120 BPM (hits lean to 111-125 BPM, weak evidence). A light plucked lead and a soft kick, with a short fill under the descent. No licensed tracks.

### Risks and fixes

- **It reads as a floor plan or "dimension guide"**: both dimension-guide videos flopped (13,770 and 6,836 views) [measured]. Fixes:
  - a 68° view, never straight down; three full-height walls; the camera circling from frame 1; no lines, labels or arrows; the made-up bed by 0.3 s;
  - a 48° field of view, so the room fills most of the frame and the bonk reads on a phone;
  - pale, textured ground just past the frame edge, so the overhead frames neither look like a still camera nor show the near-black world.
- **A slow start**: two flops sat nearly empty for 6-8 s, and the hits' longest still is 1.4 s against 2.6 s for flops [measured]. Fix: four landings by 1.1 s, the shuffle from 1.3 s, and no gap longer than 0.3 s between moves.
- **Impossible moves look fake or AI-made.** Every move is a slide, or a slide and a turn in open floor:
  - The bed slides; it never turns. A 1.5 × 2.1 m bed needs a clear circle 2.58 m across to turn, which this room cannot give.
  - The wardrobe turns (1.14 m circle) in the free west half, 1.5 × 2.1 m, once the bed has moved.
  - The desk turns (0.97 m circle) inside the door, at least 6 cm from everything, then runs up the aisle with 7.5 cm on each side, a satisfying near-miss.
  - These are worked out from the plan's sizes. Run the top-view collision test on the full move path, then again with every door and pull-out open one at a time.
- **The "before" looks staged.** Fix: the bed truly centred, the wardrobe at its foot (doors stop at about 64°) and the desk wedged at the bed's corner. These are clashes people live with.
- **The raised bed doesn't add up.** Fix: the frame sits in the deck, so the mattress top is at 1.2 m with 1.5 m of headroom. The long cut shows the mattress sliding out for sheet changes, and the pinned comment explains it.
- **The rail could look fake.** There is only about 0.9 m clear under a 1.0 m deck. Fix: rail at 0.88 m, shirts and blouses only, hanger plus garment modelled at 0.78 m. The hems stop at 0.10 m, at least 5 cm above the unit's 0.05 m base.
- **A "you added half a room" pile-on.** Title fights drew comments with 2.3K likes [measured]. Fix: the vanity and the mirror tower are cut. The platform holds the stairs, deep drawer, rail and ledge. The caption says the platform is the big addition, and the pin lists every added piece.
- **A grey or cool look.** The plan-view hit measured saturation 0.13 [measured], and kitchen v2 measured warmth 14.5 against a 22 target. Fixes:
  - blue is only on a pillow and a throw; oak wardrobe doors and top cupboard; the ochre duvet or knitwear in frame at every station;
  - run `hh_metrics.py` on every station, not only the first 5 s (targets: saturation 0.17-0.38, warmth 22-57, brightness 138-178).
- **The two colour fixes pull in different directions.** One review fix said "lighter blue on the wardrobe door panels"; another said "move the blue off the wardrobe". I followed the second. It cuts the cool area further and stops the room echoing top-5 #3.
- **The light could jump mid-descent** when the ceiling switches on. Fix: keyed area lights cross-faded over 0.5 s (see Light). Render test frames either side of the switch and compare brightness.
- **A crowded topic.** The newest bed-placement title ran at 0.82, and HomeCraft's big number is from 2024. Fix: the couple premise and the send line lead the caption. Post it as the Dec-Jan "fix this layout" test, not as a top bet.
- **Text or IP on props.** Fix: blank, solid-colour spines, notebooks and box faces; a suitcase with no logo; shoes with no stripes or logos.
- **It isn't very fresh.** The finished room uses the familiar kit: a raised platform, storage stairs (top-5 #2 and #5) and a window desk. Fix: the overhead tile-puzzle shuffle and the bonk-then-chime are the signature; the side pull-out rail is the hero; the blue-door wardrobe is gone.

### Build notes

- **Reuse from `rooms/bedroom/project.py`**:
  - the materials pattern (oak floor, `wood_mats`), `garment()`, `stack()`, `cloth()` and `pillow()` for the made bed, and `plant()`;
  - the `door_z` and `drawer_fn` timeline helpers;
  - its lamp lighting and BLENDER settings as starting values, minus the sun.
  - Build a fresh shell, though: that project is its own post with a different layout.
- **Reuse from `rooms/kitchen/project.py`**: the pull-out pantry slide-and-fill pattern for the rail and the deep drawer.
- **One timeline, two cuts**: write the pieces, contents and beat functions once, and drive each cut from its own beat list.
- **Camera**: use `pipeline/camkit.py` `hold_camera` for the eye-level stations, wrapped in a custom `camera(t)` for the orbit, the low glide, the descent and the rise.
  - Patch `blender_render.py` to let the lens width change per frame (keyframe `cam.angle_y` from `camera(t)['vfov']`; `pt.cpp` already reads it per frame; a few lines). Today it reads the field of view once, from `camera(0)`. Keep the old behaviour when `vfov` never changes.
  - 48° overhead, widening to 85° inside.
  - Starting values: about 7.4 m from the room centre along the 68° line (about 6.9 m up and 2.8 m south), circling at about 5° per second. Check with ROOM_REST stills and adjust.
  - While the camera is above 2.7 m, keep the frame's top edge below the horizon. The world is hard-coded near-black (0.05).
  - J1, J2 and every other station must clear the 1.8 camera-movement target.
- **Person**: `pipeline/figure.py`.
  - `fg.walk` for the entrance and the step back.
  - `fg.track` for the poses: a custom "open" pose with the right arm forward, `reach_both`, and a two-arm "pull".
  - The shirt follows the right forearm through `fig.transforms(...)['<inst>_farm_r']` in a `Timeline.custom`.
- **Sound**: add bonk, clack and scrape events to `pipeline/music.py`, beside its thud, tick, swish and chime.
- **New pieces**:
  - the 3.0 × 3.1 × 2.7 m shell. The south wall comes in two parts: a 0.9 m base that is always there, and an upper part shown only while the camera is inside the room below 2.7 m. Switch it while it is behind the camera: as the camera passes through its plane on the way in, and at the 2.7 m crossing on the way out.
  - a ceiling that switches in the frame where the camera crosses 2.7 m, when it is seen edge-on;
  - pale, textured ground (pale oak deck or warm concrete), sized from stills to pass the frame edge in every overhead frame;
  - roomkit area lights above the open top, a spot for the window beam, and hidden interior fills;
  - the bonk doors: swing to 64°, stop, wobble to rest, close;
  - the platform (one rigid, full-size lift that rises from the floor), with the bed riding up on it, folding legs and a recess for the frame;
  - the mattress slide (long cut);
  - stairs with side-pull drawers, and the deep drawer;
  - the rail unit: frame, tube rail at 0.88 m and about 12 garments;
  - the top cupboard (drops onto the wardrobe at 2.0 m as one piece), the headboard ledge, the desk drawers and the window shelf.
  - The desk and the tucked chair move as one piece during the shuffle.
- **Effort**: medium-high. About 20 animated pieces plus their contents, a new camera function and a small bridge patch.
  - The camera never stops, so no frames can be copied: 375 + 1,125 = 1,500 frames at the README's ~35 s per frame (720x1280, 4 cores) is about 14.6 CPU-hours.
  - Render the 15 s cut first, as the test. Before any full render, do low-sample test renders of the ceiling switch, the descent and the rail.
- **Hard to render, and how to avoid it**:
  - No see-through "ghost" of the old layout: the Blender bridge only supports glass and translucent mixes.
  - No cloth simulation: the duvet is a rigid `cloth()` mesh that moves with the bed.
  - Garments are simple hanging panels at their real length; the figure is faceless with no hair.
- **Variety**: no toe-kick step drawer (that beat belongs to #3 Three of Us; #8 Dinner for Eight now uses a dish lift). No sliding mirror panel or floating desk, which are in `rooms/bedroom`.
- **Posting**: evergreen, but best as the new-year "fix this layout" post: the trends file's slot is 27 Dec-10 Jan. Storyboard episode 2 ("Undo": a galley kitchen with the fridge in the way), and make it only if #2's 15 s cut beats the slate median after 7 days.

### Corrections to the candidate notes

- **The 536,285-view video is not the 2nd-best 2026 upload.** In `videos.csv` it is the 4th most-viewed, after 853K, 689K and 547K. I cite it by its era-adjusted 16.75 instead.
- **The 24 s two-beds Short (991,555 views) does not have the highest camera movement of the 59.** It has the highest motion (15.5). Its camera score (11.88) is second to a 6,836-view dimension guide (12.1).
- **The bed slides, it does not spin.** The candidate had the bed pivot 90° into the corner, which can't be done without passing through the wardrobe or desk.
- **Review corrections applied to this plan:**
  - The bed family is 15 titles, median 1.74, 13 of 15 above the norm. It is not "all 12 beat the norm, median 3.04". The "11 titles give 3.17" figure is not in any file: 3.17 is one title's value.
  - "Be Bold — Stop Putting the Bed..." is 1.015, the 3rd weakest of 15. The weakest is "Flip the Bed" at 0.43.
  - The old "centred" bed (x 0.85-2.35) was 0.10 m off centre. It is now truly centred at x 0.75-2.25.
  - The 1.5 m headroom only held with the mattress on the deck; it now is.
  - The 7 cm clearances had no desk size behind them. The desk is now 0.8 × 0.5 m, with 7.5 cm each side in the aisle.
  - The rail was 1.9 m and would have hit the desk. It is now 1.4 m.
  - Problem-first openings are 2 hits and 1 flop, not 2 hits.
  - HomeCraft's 351.8K likes date from Apr 2024.

## 3. Three of Us in 3 × 2 m

- **At a glance**:
  - Candidate C1. Judges' mean 7.8; reviewer score 6.5, given before this revision.
  - Plan ready, not started.
  - Posting: the 15 s cut on Wed 9 Dec, the long cut on Mon 14 Dec.
- **Why it ranks here**:
  - A bedroom with a raised bed and a size the caption can lead with (6 m²) [measured].
  - It answers a real request that came with dimensions [fact].
  - The household is untested on the channel: 0 of 298 titles mention a baby [measured]. So it is also the slate's test of a household premise [inference].

### Pitch

Two parents and a newborn share a 3 × 2 m bedroom with a 2.9 m ceiling. The double bed goes up wall to wall on a 1.2 m deck. The crib always stays at floor level: it parks under the deck by day and rolls out beside the stair at night. And the baby gets two-thirds of the wardrobe.

### Post caption (in the post, never on screen)

"3 × 2 m bedroom, 2.9 m ceiling. Two parents and a newborn.
The crib parks under the bed by day, and yes, the baby got two-thirds of the wardrobe.
Crib at floor level, bed 1.2 m up. 3 a.m. feed: genius or nightmare? Send me your room size and who lives there."
- Pinned comment: the plan with dimensions, plus "Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app." The dimensions:
  - the crib is always at floor level;
  - deck top 1.20 m; mattress 1.35 × 1.90 m (a standard double), 1.50 m below the ceiling;
  - guard rail 0.3 m above the mattress;
  - stair: 0.24 m rises, 0.25 m treads, a 10 cm landing at the top;
  - changing top 0.5 × 0.9 m at 0.9 m high;
  - top cabinets start at 2.05 m, reached from a 0.2 m step drawer.
- Lead with the size and call it a bedroom, never an apartment.
- Never say "you asked". Never quote, name or link the channel where the request was posted.

### Why it should work

- **A bedroom with a raised bed, the pattern that held up in 2026** [measured]:
  - Bedrooms run 1.51 era-adjusted (47 Shorts).
  - The 4 extra "best of 2026" videos in the in-depth sample (290K-547K) are all bedrooms or kids' rooms. The report codes all 4 as raised or loft beds. The CSV frame notes confirm 3; the 536K one is noted "small bedroom; conventional layout first".
  - The two biggest 2026 uploads (853K kids' room; 689K secondary bedroom) are in the sample as matched-pair hits, but their frame notes ("kids room; zone labels ~6 s", "small bedroom") don't record the bed type.
  - One of the measured four, "Be bold! Raise the bed by 90 centimeters in a small bedroom!", got 343,222 views (10.39 era-adjusted, 43 s, motion 7.5, longest still 1.0 s).
- **A size the caption can lead with** [measured]: titles naming a size run 1.34, against 1.00 without (15 Shorts). The four "6㎡" titles ran 4.83 (396K), 2.20 (286K), 1.34 (230K) and 0.63 (82K).
- **A real request with real numbers** [fact]: report section 6 records a comment asking for "a compact bedroom for a husband, wife and newborn: 3 m x 2 m, ceiling 2.9 m". The checklist says to answer requests like this as a series.
  - [snapshot, indicative] Household premises still break out in 2026. HomeCraft's "Two kids, one room" (22 May 2026) shows 22.5K likes, against 260-924 for its single-bedroom posts from Jan 2025 to May 2026.
  - [snapshot, indicative] An answer-a-request series on Bilibili (real footage, not 3D) reached about 202.6K plays (Sep 2026).
- **The whole formula fits** [measured]:
  - It builds a whole room: 28 of 29 hits did, against 12 of 30 flops.
  - It has 10 storage moments in the long cut and 7 in the 15 s cut; the top 5 have 6-12.
  - A person uses a feature, as in 4 of the top 5.
  - That feature is a pull-out step drawer, the same kind of mechanism as the 19.3M kitchen's wow (29-32 s), which the second comment calls "genius". The staging here is different (see Risks and fixes).
- **A built-in argument and a reason to send it**:
  - [measured] Comments argue about practicality: the fridge vent (167 likes) and "how do you make your bed?" (32). A 1.2 m bed above a crib's parking bay invites that argument, and the design answers it (the crib at floor level, a seat on step 2 beside it).
  - [measured] The most-liked comments on record are title fights (2.3K, 2.2K, 1.2K), not practicality. So the caption's claims must be exact.
  - [fact] Sends per reach is one of Instagram's three main ranking signals.
  - [inference] "The baby got two-thirds of the wardrobe" is a joke parents send each other. The trends file lists "a couple with a baby" as a premise that drives sends, but that is its own inference, not a measured figure.
- **Believable beats AI-looking** [snapshot, indicative]: AI "empty room to furnished" timelapses are spreading (trends file). Real dimensions and mechanisms that visibly work are the contrast.

### The room

- **Size and shape**: 6 m², 3.0 m long (north-south) by 2.0 m wide, with a 2.9 m ceiling. Coordinates: x runs east 0-2.0, y runs north 0-3.0. Block it out in a grey-box probe before modelling, and check every camera station there.
  - **North end (window wall)**:
    - Window 1.0 m wide (x 0.5-1.5), from 1.55 to 2.6 m high, just above the mattress.
    - The deck fills the north 1.5 m wall to wall (y 1.5-3.0). Its top is at 1.20 m. It sits on ledgers on three walls and a slim front beam, carried by two pale-grey posts: one at the stair's top corner (x 0.5) and one at x 1.54.
    - Along y, from the front: barred guard rail (3 cm), a 10 cm landing strip, then the mattress, then a 2 cm gap at the north wall.
    - Mattress 1.35 × 1.90 m (a standard double), head to the east, x 0.05-1.95, top at 1.40 m. That leaves 1.50 m to the ceiling.
    - The guard rail runs along the deck front, about 0.3 m above the mattress, and is open only above the stair.
    - Headboard niche: recessed 0.1 m into the east wall above the deck, open (no door).
  - **Under the deck** (1.10 m clear), west to east:
    - x 0-0.5: behind the stair. The riser drawers run back into it (up to 1.0 m long).
    - x 0.52-1.15: the crib's day bay. The compact crib is 0.56 × 0.92 m and 0.85 m tall, on castors, and parks at y 1.55-2.47. Behind it is an open shelf, 0.4 m deep (y 2.55-2.95), with an LED. It holds nappy packs, wipes and spare sleepsuits.
    - x 1.17-1.53: the baby pull-out, 1.35 m long, with an end-on rail of tiny outfits above two bins. It slides out along the wardrobe front.
    - x 1.55-2.0: closed. It sits behind the wardrobe's north end and carries the light cables. Keep it out of shot.
  - **West wall**:
    - A 4-step drawer stair, 0.5 m wide, at y 0.5-1.5.
    - Steps at 0.24, 0.48, 0.72 and 0.96 m, then the landing strip at 1.20 m (0.24 m rises, 0.25 m treads).
    - Celery riser-drawer fronts. Each drawer is at most 1.0 m long and pulls out as far as the room allows (0.45-0.75 m; the south wall stops the lower ones).
    - A pale-grey handrail on two posts on the open side (no balusters), an LED strip under each tread, and nothing above the stair.
  - **East wall**: a floor-to-ceiling wardrobe at y 0-1.5, 0.45 m deep (x 1.55-2.0), with three 0.5 m bays and light birch fronts.
    - A (by the door, y 0-0.5) and B are the baby's. C is the parents': one end-on pull-out rail, because adult hangers need more than 0.45 m of depth.
    - Inside bay A, a flap at 0.9 m folds down, at most 0.5 m deep. With the shelf behind it, it makes a 0.5 × 0.9 m changing top. A nappy drawer sits under it.
    - A 0.2 m toe-kick step drawer sits under bay A, clear of the stair.
    - Top cabinets run from 2.05 to 2.9 m. The one over bay A has a lift-up flap.
  - **South wall**:
    - A 0.9 m doorway (x 0.55-1.45). The door opens outward and its leaf is never in shot.
    - A bridge cabinet over the doorway (x 0-1.55): 2.15-2.9 m high, 0.35 m deep, with lift-up flaps. It meets the wardrobe's top cabinets in an L.
    - A white hall stub behind it, 2.0 m wide and 1.6 m deep. Glimpses through the door then look real, and its pale-grey floor is the foreground floor in the hall shots.
