  - **Free floor**: by day, 1.05 × 1.5 m between the stair and the wardrobe, plus the 0.5 m strip at the foot of the stair. At night the crib stands beside the stair (x 0.52-1.15, y 0.5-1.45). That leaves 0.4 m in front of the wardrobe: its doors open about 50° past the crib.
- **Camera rule**: no doorway keyhole. In the hall shots the south wall, the bridge cabinet and the hall stub's walls and ceiling are hidden from the camera, so the room reads as a cut-away box. In the west shots the west wall is hidden. Both are set per hold.
- **Palette**:
  - **Shell**: neutral white (about sRGB 242, 241, 238) on walls and ceiling, not the bedroom project's warm white (238, 233, 225), which sits at the edge of the cream zone. Pale-grey posts, rails and handrail. The warmth comes from the birch, the mustard throw and the dusk backdrop.
  - **Wood**: light birch on the floor, deck fascia, stair treads, crib and wardrobe fronts. No walnut.
  - **One accent**: celery green on the riser-drawer fronts, echoed by a sage duvet. Sherwin-Williams Celery is one of the 2027 colour picks [snapshot, trends file].
  - **Colour from filled storage, early**:
    - The crib's sky-blue sheet at 0.45 s.
    - The crib-bay shelf fills with sky, coral and teal packs by 1.7 s (15 s cut; 2.55 s in the long cut).
    - Then mid-tone baby clothes (coral, teal, mustard, sky; not washed-out pastels), the parents' navy, denim and grey, and books in the niche.
    - A mustard throw folded at the foot of the bed for the final wide (never in the crib).
  - **Keep in view**: the crib-bay shelf and the niche have no doors, so their contents stay in frame to the end.
  - **Avoid**: a pink theme, cream, saturated walls and patterns.
  - **Targets** (the hits' band) [measured]: saturation 0.17-0.38, warmth 22-57, brightness 138-178. Our kitchen came in on the cool, grey side: saturation 0.11, warmth 14.5-18.3.
- **Light**:
  - **Day**: soft daylight through the north window, plus the two fill slots used in the bedroom project, and a weak fill under the deck so the bays never go black.
  - **Lights on** (from 12.2 s in the 15 s cut, from 40.4 s in the long cut): evening, not night.
    - The window turns a soft lavender-peach dusk: a new backdrop, swapped in the way the bedroom project swaps in `bd_night`.
    - Keep the fills at 85% or more, the slate's rule for lights-on. The bedroom project drops them to 6-7%, which is far too dark here.
    - The lights come on one after another (see the cuts): reading light, tread LEDs bottom to top, the crib-bay shelf LED, the niche night light.
    - On the preview, check warmth 22 or more and 57 or less, saturation 0.17 or more, and brightness 138 or more.

### Frame 1 and the hook

- **0.0 s**: the empty, tall, narrow white room, seen as a cut-away box from the hall. Bare pale-grey floor, the window at the far end, daylight.
  - The camera is already craning up. Pose P0: x 1.0, y -2.8, z 1.4 m, looking north, 1° down.
  - Lens: 62° vertical (about 37° horizontal). From here the full 2 m width and the floor-to-ceiling height are in frame, with a thin white ceiling edge at the top and the hall floor at the bottom. The 6 m² reads at once. Confirm in the probe.
- **0.12-0.30 s**: an empty light-birch crib drops onto the bare floor, just left of centre, in line with its bay. It lands at 0.30 s with a soft thud and a small wobble, so the first question is "a crib, in here?"
- **1.20 s**: the deck slams in wall to wall, and the room's width visibly equals one bed length.

### 15 s cut

- **0.0-3.2 s · S1 hall (the frame-1 pose)**:
  - The crib lands at 0.30 s, and its mattress with the sky sheet drops in at 0.45 s.
  - Light-birch planks slap down far to near, 0.40-0.80 s; the crib hops as its row lands.
  - Two posts drop, 0.85-1.0 s.
  - **The deck slams in wall to wall at 1.20 s**: the hardest thud of the video.
  - The crib-bay shelf drops in at 1.30 s and fills with pips, 1.35-1.70 s.
  - The crib rolls back into its bay, 1.75-2.10 s, with a soft roll.
  - Guard rail 2.15 s, mattress 2.30 s, sage duvet spreads 2.45 s, two pillows 2.60 and 2.70 s, mustard throw 2.80 s.
  - The niche fills, 2.90-3.05 s (night light, two books), and the reading light clips onto the rail at 3.15 s.
  - Camera: crane up 0.46 m with a 0.16 m push-in while tilting down 4.8° (about 0.15 m/s and 1.5°/s).
- **3.5-7.0 s · S2 stair and pull-out (high west cut-away, about x -0.9, z 2.2, looking east and 30° down)**:
  - Stair blocks drop bottom to top, 3.55-3.95 s, with thuds rising in pitch; handrail at 4.05 s.
  - The riser drawers slide out across the frame and fill in turn, 4.15-5.75 s: navy and denim jumpers, white sheets, teal and mustard sleep sacks. Then step 4's 1.0 m drawer keeps coming, 0.75 m out over the lower treads, full of coral and sky towels and spare crib sheets. Each drawer shuts with a thud.
  - The baby pull-out glides out across the frame at 5.85 s. Tiny coral, sky and mustard outfits tick onto its rail, 6.0-6.5 s. It glides back, 6.6-6.9 s, with a deep thud.
  - Camera: slide south 0.5 m with a 5° pan right, following the drawers. The handrail and treads give foreground parallax.
- **7.3-10.7 s · S3 wardrobe (high west cut-away, about x -1.25, z 1.9, 10° down; the whole wardrobe fills the portrait frame above the stair)**:
  - The carcass and its top cabinets slide out of the east wall with the doors shut, 7.35-7.7 s (thud).
  - **Wow, 7.9-9.0 s**: the three doors swing open on one sparse parents' bay (bay C's door opens with a "bwomp"; its skinny rail stays in), while bays A and B fill in a cascade of tiny outfits, nappy stacks and sock baskets (pip-pip-pip).
  - Bay A's flap folds down into the changing top and the mat drops on, 9.1-9.6 s.
  - The nappy drawer under it pulls out, fills (nappies, wipes, cream) and shuts, 9.7-10.5 s.

- **11.0-15.0 s · S4 hall wide (crane up into P0)**:
  - The flap folds up and the doors close, 11.0-11.3 s.
  - The window turns dusk at 11.1 s.
  - The crib rolls out of its bay toward the camera, 11.3-11.9 s, and stops beside the stair with a soft roll.
  - Parent 2 pops in seated on the deck at 11.7 s, so the headroom reads.
  - Parent 1 pops in seated on step 2 at 11.95 s, facing down the stair with the crib at the left hand: the floor-level answer to the 3 a.m. question.
  - **Lights on, staggered**: reading light 12.2 s; tread LEDs 12.5, 12.7, 12.9 and 13.1 s; crib-bay shelf LED 13.4 s; niche night light 13.7 s.
  - **Late beats**: parent 1 rests a hand on the crib and it rocks about 1 cm, 13.9-14.3 s. Parent 2 lies back on the pillows, 14.0-14.6 s, and the duvet settles, 14.4-14.8 s.
  - Camera: from (1.0, -3.0, 0.83 m, 5° up) to P0, crane up 0.57 m with a 0.2 m push-in, tilting down 6°. That is about 0.15 m/s and 1.5°/s, the same speed and tilt rate as S1, so the loop is seamless.

44.0 s in 6 zones (6.7-7.3 s each) and 12 camera holds: 11 of 3.3-3.5 s and a final 3.1 s crane, joined by 0.3 s swings. The wow is at 26.6 s (60%). The lights stagger from 40.4 to 42.15 s and late beats run to the end. No end card.

### Storage moments (6-12, numbered)

1. **Crib drawer** (long cut 1.05-1.5 s): the drawer under the crib pulls out and coral and teal muslins drop in.
2. **Crib-bay shelf** (15 s 1.30-1.70 s; long 2.05-2.55 s): the open shelf drops in behind the crib's bay and pip-fills with nappy packs, wipes and spare sleepsuits before the crib parks. At night the crib rolls out and the shelf glows (15 s 13.4 s; long 41.85 s).
3. **Headboard niche** (15 s 2.90-3.05 s; long 4.7-6.0 s): a night light and books land in the recessed niche; in the long cut, a monitor and a phone too.
4. **Stair riser drawers** (15 s 4.15-5.75 s; long 8.1-10.6 s): four drawers slide out across the frame in turn and fill with the parents' jumpers, white sheets, and teal and mustard sleep sacks. Then step 4's 1.0 m drawer keeps coming, 0.75 m out, with towels and spare crib sheets.
5. **Baby pull-out** (15 s 5.85-6.9 s; long 11.2-14.1 s): it glides out from under the deck, tiny outfits tick onto its end-on rail, and bibs and toys fill two bins. It glides back with a deep thud.
6. **Bridge cabinet over the door** (long 15.0-17.0 s): the flaps open, a rolled winter duvet, a suitcase and a sky-blue box slide in, and the flaps swing shut.
7. **Top cabinet via the toe-kick step drawer** (long 23.3-25.2 s): parent 1 hooks the step drawer out with a foot, steps up and slides three colour-coded next-size boxes into the top cabinet over bay A.
8. **Wow: the baby gets two-thirds** (15 s 7.9-9.0 s; long 26.6-28.2 s):
   - The three doors swing open. Bay C, the parents', is sparse: its one skinny end-on rail stays in.
   - At the same time, bays A and B fill in a cascade of tiny coral, teal, mustard and sky outfits, nappy stacks, and sock and bib baskets.
   - Long cut only: parent 1 then pulls the skinny rail out of bay C, five garments in navy, denim and grey, and shrugs (28.7-29.3 s). In the 15 s cut it stays in.
9. **Changing table** (15 s 9.1-9.6 s; long 29.9-30.5 s): bay A's flap folds down on two brackets into a 0.5 × 0.9 m changing top at 0.9 m, and the padded mat drops on.
10. **Nappy drawer** (15 s 9.7-10.5 s; long 30.8-31.8 s): it pulls out under the changing top, fills with nappies, wipes and cream, and shuts.

The 15 s cut has 7 of these (#2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 9, 10); the long cut has all 10.

### The person moment

- **Figures**: parent 1 is `fig_sage` and parent 2 is `fig_blue` (both `fig_adult` proportions, about 1.68 m), so they read against the white walls and birch fronts. Both are faceless.
- **Long cut, 22.4-29.3 s**: parent 1.
  - Walks in from the door using `walk()`, 22.4-23.2 s.
  - Hooks the 0.2 m step drawer out from under bay A with a foot (23.3 s), steps up (`step_up` pose) and reaches up (`reach_up`). The top cabinet's flap lifts, and three next-size boxes go in one by one, in size order, colour-coded (sky, teal, coral; no numbers), 23.8-25.0 s.
  - Reach check: `reach_up` reaches about 2.05-2.1 m, so about 2.25-2.3 m from the step, against a cabinet floor at 2.05 m. Probe the pose.
  - Steps down and back, about 0.75 m from the fronts, so the doors can open (25.2-25.6 s). The drawer slides home at 26.1 s.
  - When the doors open, parent 1 turns the head to the baby's two bays, then pulls the skinny rail out of bay C and shrugs: forearms forward and out, palms up, plus a 3 cm bob of the pelvis. A custom pose. It tells the "baby wins" joke with no text.
- **Long cut, evening, 32.2-39.9 s**:
  - Parent 1 walks out (32.2-33.2 s) and comes back cradling the swaddled bundle (35.0-36.6 s): a faceless soft ellipsoid, about 0.5 m long, that follows the left forearm.
  - Parent 2 pops in already seated on the deck at 37.4 s (`figure.py`'s pop-in). No climb and no swing-round: they cost the most and would read as still stretches.
  - Parent 1 sits on step 2 (0.48 m, a normal seat height), facing down the stair with the feet on step 1, the crib at the left hand. That is the floor-level answer to the 3 a.m. debate.
  - Parent 1 stands and lays the bundle on its back in the bare crib, then later rests a hand on the crib (42.9-43.4 s). Parent 2 lies back (42.4-43.0 s).
- **15 s cut**: parent 2 pops in seated on the deck at 11.7 s and parent 1 pops in seated on step 2 beside the crib at 11.95 s, both with the pop-in option. Late beats: the crib rock and parent 2 lying back.

### Sound

- **Landings**:
  - Thud: the deck (the loudest hit, at 1.20 s / 1.95 s), the posts, the carcasses and every drawer as it shuts.
  - Tick: planks, clothes, books and boxes.
  - Swish: drawers, doors and flaps opening.
  - Chime: each light coming on, rising in pitch through the stagger.
- **Playful effect 1**: every baby-sized item (crib sheet, tiny hangers, socks, nappies) lands with a higher, softer "pip", about twice the tick's pitch, while adult items land with a deep thunk. Viewers hear the size difference.
- **Playful effect 2**: a two-note falling "bwomp" as bay C's door opens on the sparse parents' bay (about 8.0 s in the 15 s cut; 26.7 s in the long cut), and again under the shrug (28.9 s).
- The crib's roll when it parks (2.1 s / 3.0 s) and when it stops at night (11.9 s / 34.8 s) is a plain soft roll, not a third effect.
- **Music**:
  - An original cue from `music.py` at 120 BPM (set `{"bpm": 120}` in `music.json`), with a music-box-like pluck line. Original melody only: no known lullaby, nothing licensed.
  - Hits lean to 111-125 BPM, but that evidence is weak. There is no need to cut on the beat.

### Risks and fixes

- **Baby-safety pile-on.**
  - The crib is always at floor level and parks under the deck only when empty. The caption says so first ("Crib at floor level, bed 1.2 m up"), so it never implies feeding on the loft.
  - The crib stays bare: no pillow, toy or loose blanket. The throw stays on the parents' bed.
  - There is no tiny pillow on the parents' bed. It would read as co-sleeping, and as a pillow for a newborn. The crib and its sheet are the baby cue.
  - Other safety details: a barred guard rail about 0.3 m above the mattress, a handrail, lit treads, a 10 cm landing at the top, and nothing above the crib at night.
  - Pin the plan with these heights. Make no co-sleeping or safety-standard claims.
- **Untested household, and the source.**
  - [measured] 0 of 298 titles mention a baby, nursery, crib or newborn. The premise is either open ground or a dud.
  - [measured, my count from `videos.csv`] The 6 "master bedroom" titles have a median of 0.91 era-adjusted. The 0.89 (8 titles) in the judges' notes includes a "masterpiece" studio title.
  - Fix: run it as a test. Post the 15 s and long cuts a few days apart, and compare sends and saves per reach with the two-kids video.
  - Lead with the size. Call it a bedroom, not a master bedroom and never an apartment (the "24 m² apartment" pile-on comment got 2.3K likes).
  - Paraphrase the request; never credit or link it.
  - Have a second "who lives here?" episode ready, so a hit turns into a series.
- **The camera stalls in a 2 m-wide room (our biggest measured gap).**
  - Use one 62° vertical lens for the whole clip: `blender_render.py` reads the field of view once, at t = 0. The narrower lens also keeps proportions honest.
  - Favour lateral slides, orbits and cranes over push-ins toward blank walls. Every hold moves at least 0.3 m or 5°.
  - Put texture or foreground parallax in every part of the frame: guard-rail bars, the handrail, a wall edge, planks, filled open shelves. `hh_metrics.py` scores the 3rd-quietest of 12 tiles, and bare white tiles barely change however the camera moves.
  - Before any detail modelling, render a 720p grey-box preview with the planks and shelf fills in, and run `hh_metrics.py`. Targets: camera movement 1.8 or more, longest still 1.8 s or less, motion 7.5 or more, plus the colour targets.
  - [measured, re-checked today] Only 9 of 29 hits pass every visual target in `hh_metrics.py`; the median hit misses 1 and the median flop misses 5. Treat a miss as direction, not a gate.
  - The 15 s cut works only if it never stops. The two short hits did not: the 29 s study (1,035,401 views) and the 24 s two-beds room (991,555 views). The two-beds room has the highest camera movement of the 29 hits (11.88); the highest of all 59 is a 6,836-view flop at 12.1.
- **Stations blocked by the stair.** The stair stands between the west camera and the wardrobe and crib.
  - The wardrobe is shot from a high west camera (z 1.9 m or more, about 10° down) so the stair stays below the action.
  - The step drawer moved under bay A, clear of the stair.
  - The night crib is shot from the hall or the deck, never from the west.
  - Probe every station in the grey box first.
- **Kept from the original: the stair stays visible in every hold.** The review also offered hiding it from the camera as a dollhouse section. I didn't, because it would vanish and reappear across the 0.3 s swings, and parent 1 sits on it. The high west camera does the job.
- **Colour drifts cool, not warm.** Our renders miss the band on the cool side (kitchen warmth 14.5-18.3, saturation 0.11).
  - Fixes: neutral white walls, with the warmth from birch fronts and fascia, open shelves and niche, a mustard throw, and a lavender-peach dusk instead of blue.
  - Today's extra check put "warm white" at the edge of the cream zone by eye, so the walls stay neutral, as in the rest of the slate. The preview's warmth number decides: 22-57.
- **The last stretch reads as still.** Kitchen v2's longest still was 2.4 s, against a 1.8 s target (the hits' median is 1.4 s).
  - Fix: lights staggered over 1.5-1.75 s, then late figure beats, and a final crane at about 0.15 m/s instead of a slow push-in.
- **Things that light up must land first.** The crib-bay shelf lands and fills before the crib parks, and the niche props and reading light land in S1. Otherwise it reads as the AI "room fills itself" look.
- **Practicality attacks the design must survive.**
  - The night crib leaves 0.4 m in front of the wardrobe, and the doors open about 50° past it. The changing flap is no more than 0.5 m deep.
  - The riser drawers are capped at 1.0 m (step 4's gag included). The deep space under the deck goes to the crib bay and the pull-out.
  - The bed geometry closes: 3 cm rail + 10 cm landing + 1.35 m mattress + 2 cm gap = 1.5 m deck. The niche is recessed, so a standard 1.90 m mattress fits.
- **The step-drawer beat echoes the 19.3M kitchen's wow** (a woman on a pull-out step drawer reaching a top cabinet). The mechanism is generic: the trends file lists toe-kick step drawers as a copyable trick. Stage it differently: a wardrobe, not a kitchen, a foot hooking the drawer out, and colour-coded boxes in size order.
- **Readable text on props.** Real nappy packs, wipes and size boxes carry logos and numbers. Use plain colour blocks (sizes by colour), a soft blank glow on the monitor, a dark phone screen, no clock digits, blank book spines, and plain clothes with no prints, characters or logos.
- **Repeats across the slate.**
  - Riser drawers that fill in turn also appear in #4 Advent Stair (30 Nov, 2 Dec) and #1 Tape Line (26 Nov, 3 Dec). #5 Bed on the Ceiling (19 Dec) is another bed-moves bedroom.
  - #8 Dinner for Eight now uses a dish lift instead of a step drawer, so the step-drawer beat is this plan's alone.
  - Post #3 on Wed 9 Dec (15 s) and Mon 14 Dec (long): at least 5 days after #1 and #4, and 5 days before #5. It is evergreen, so it can slip.
  - If a stair-drawer video posts within 5 days anyway, cut the riser fill to step 4's long drawer and give the time to the pull-out.

### Build notes

- **Reuse from `rooms/bedroom/project.py`**:
  - Materials: the oak and birch woods, clothing and book colours, linen and mattress. Swap the warm-white `wall` for neutral white.
  - Helpers: `garment()` (at about half size for tiny outfits), `stack()` for nappies, muslins and jumpers, `pillow()`, and `cloth()` for the duvet.
  - `drawer_fn()` for every drawer: risers, nappy drawer, crib drawer and step drawer.
  - `door_z()` for pieces that land open and swing shut: the long cut's wardrobe doors (20.6-21.6 s) and the bridge flaps (`axis='x'`).
  - The hinged `lift()` pattern for the changing flap.
  - `appear()` plus chime for the light stagger, the day/dusk window backdrop swap, and the `BLENDER` look settings.
- **Reuse from `rooms/kitchen/project.py`**: the pull-out pantry's slide out and back, for the baby pull-out and the parents' end-on rail.
- **Shared modules**: `camkit.hold_camera` for both cuts' holds, and `figure.py` for two adults (`walk`, `track`, `sit_pose`, pop-in), plus custom cradle and shrug poses.
- **New pieces**:
  - The 2.0 × 3.0 × 2.9 m shell with the doorway, the north window and the recessed niche; the 2.0 × 1.6 m hall stub.
  - The deck, front beam, two posts, landing strip and barred guard rail.
  - The 4-step stair: riser drawers up to 1.0 m long, handrail on two posts, tread LEDs.
  - The compact crib on castors: thin-box slats, a drawer and a fitted mattress.
  - The baby pull-out, and the crib bay's open shelf with its LED.
  - The wardrobe: 3 bays with birch fronts, top cabinets from 2.05 m, step drawer under bay A, end-on rail, changing flap with brackets, nappy drawer.
  - The bridge cabinet.
  - Niche props: a monitor (rounded box with a blank glow), night light, phone (dark screen) and books (blank spines); plus the reading light.
  - Tiny hangers, nappy packs, wipes, baskets, colour-coded next-size boxes, the mustard throw, and the bundle (an ellipsoid).
  - A lavender-peach dusk backdrop.
- **Pipeline changes**:
  1. Hide pieces from the camera per hold.
     - Hall holds: the south wall, the bridge cabinet, and the hall stub's walls, ceiling and back wall. West holds: the west wall.
     - Keyframe `obj.visible_camera` at the hold boundaries, next to the existing `hide_render` keyframes in `key_transform()`, so the hidden walls still bounce light and cast shadows.
     - A static flag won't do: the west wall is in shot in the hall holds.
     - Fallback that works today: toggle those pieces' visibility per hold in `timeline()`. Their bounce light goes too, but the swings hide the change.
  2. A custom hinge for doors that go closed → open → closed: the wardrobe wow in both cuts, the 15 s cut's door close, and the top-cabinet flap. `door_z()` only pops in open and swings shut.
  3. Add two sound kinds to `music.py`, "pip" and "bwomp", using the existing tick and pluck synths.
  4. Use one `project.py` with `CUT=15` or `CUT=44` switching between the holds and timelines.
- **Effort: medium-high.**
  - Every mechanism is a rigid slide, swing or fold the kit already does, apart from the new hinge.
  - It has about 200 pieces, two timelines, and two figures with a walk, foot hook, step up, reach, shrug, cradle, stair sit and lay-down. Popping parent 2 in seated saves the climb.
  - Render time [inference from the README's ~35 s per frame at 720x1280 on 4 cores]: about 3.6 h for the 15 s cut (375 frames) and about 10.7 h for the long cut (1,100 frames), rendered at 720x1280 and scaled to 1080x1920 as in the README.
  - A native 1080x1920 render is about 2.5x slower (the README's figure): about 9.1 h and 26.7 h.
  - No frames get copied, because the camera never stops. Preview with the code renderer first.
- **Hard to render, and how to avoid it**:
  - Faces and hair: faceless mannequins and a plain swaddle.
  - Draped fabric: use `cloth()` for the duvet only. Clothes and the throw are rounded boxes, and there is no loose blanket in the crib.
  - Figure moves: keep each one short and overlap it with other motion, so no stretch goes still.
  - Under-deck darkness: an under-deck fill light and the bay LED.
  - Mattress or crib meeting the walls: leave 2-5 cm gaps so there are no visible intersections.

## 4. The Advent Stair

- **At a glance**:
  - Candidate C5. Judges' mean 7.3; reviewer score 6, given before this revision.
  - Being built now by the advent_drawer_wall render session. This plan is its upgrade list.
  - Posting: the 15 s cut on Mon 30 Nov or Tue 1 Dec at the latest. The long cut on Wed 2 Dec, only if it is ready by then (never on #1's long-cut day, 3 Dec).
- **Why it ranks here**:
  - It has the nearest posting window, and it is built on the 2026 pattern, a kids' loft [measured].
  - The stair-drawer beat is proven [measured], and the 24-drawer cascade is a natural 15 s hero [inference].
  - The holiday theme is untested (0 of 298 titles) [measured], and the video fades after 25 Dec.

This is the upgrade list for the advent_drawer_wall session (rooms/advent_drawer_wall, branch claude/video-advent-wall, commit d33a0d1). That session has already built the Advent Stair, and its layout is locked, so do not rebuild it. Everything below that is not marked **Change** already exists in its PLAN.md and project.py: keep it.

### Pitch

An 8 m² kids' room builds around a loft bed. The six-step stair on the east wall hides 24 drawers: 21 in the stepped side face and 3 in the top risers. They open bottom to top, each on the next note of a rising F major scale. Each tray slides out, its front drops into a little ledge, and a tiny colourful treasure hops onto it. Then they all snap shut. The child climbs, opens the last drawer, and its star flies to the tree on the loft as the lights come on.

### Post caption (in the post, never on screen)

- **Change** (lead with the premise and the real dimensions): "One stair, 24 drawers: one for every day until Christmas. A 2.6 × 3.1 m kids' room (8 m², 2.8 m ceiling): the loft bed left no wall for an advent calendar, so the stair became one. Would your kid wait 24 days, or open them all on day one? Save it for next December."
- **Pinned comment**: "The plan: a 0.50 m wide stair, six 230 mm rises, 220 mm treads, loft deck at 1.38 m. Oak guard on the open side, fixed glazing, soft-close drawers. It's a loft-bed stair, not a house stair. Would you trust drawers built into a stair? Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app."
- **Timing**: post the 15 s cut on Mon 30 Nov (Tue 1 Dec at the latest). Post the long cut on Wed 2 Dec, only if it is ready by then.

### Why it should work

- [measured] **Kids' rooms with raised beds are the 2026 pattern.**
  - The best 2026 upload is a kids' room: "No wonder so many people have chosen this children's room!" (853,468 views, 27 Aug 2026).
  - Second is a small secondary bedroom (689,706 views, 30 Apr 2026). The files don't record the bed type for either.
  - Third is a small kids' room with a loft (547,264 views, 6 Jan 2026).
  - All 4 "recent_top" 2026 videos in the sample (290K-547K) are bedrooms or kids' rooms that report.md codes as raised or loft beds; the CSV frame notes confirm 3 of the 4.
  - 4 of the 5 worst 2026 uploads were single features or dimension guides.
  - Kids' rooms run at 1.35 era-adjusted, and titles naming a size at 1.34.
  - 28 of 29 hits build a whole room, against 12 of 30 flops.
- [measured] **Stair drawers are a proven hit moment.**
  - The 12.8M loft (12,761,012 views, 50 s) has stairs whose drawers pull out and fill, then a boy walks in.
  - The 5.5M "24 m²" flat fills storage-stair drawers too.
  - The top 5 have 6-12 storage reveals, and a person uses a feature in 4 of the 5.
  - This plan has 9 storage moments (7 in the 15 s cut), and the child uses the stair.
- [fact, platform-reported snapshots, indicative] **Christmas demand is high right now.**
  - TikTok says US searches for "Christmas decorations" rose more than 40x from October to November 2026.
  - #diychristmas is up 80%+ and #cozychristmas 170%+.
  - The trends file puts advent-calendar interest in early December. Its only figure for that is a beauty-unboxing tag, so treat the advent peak as weak evidence.
  - [measured] 0 of 298 Haven Haus titles mention Christmas, holiday or winter. That means the theme is untested on this channel, so treat the seasonal layer as a test.
- [measured] **The hero never stops moving.**
  - In hits, motion per 0.2 s is 8.4 against 5.1 for flops, and the longest still is 1.4 s against 2.6 s. The hit's camera moved more in 21 of 25 pairs.
  - The cascade opens a drawer every 0.12 s while the camera glides.
  - [inference, from the session's camera poses] Station C keeps 23-24 of the 24 drawers in frame all the way through, and the stair fills 79-100% of the frame width. One drawer front is 13-16% of the width, about 140-170 px at 1080.
  - A short video works only if it never stops: the 24 s two-beds room (991,555 views) has the highest camera movement of any hit (11.88).
- [inference] **Built for replays and debate.**
  - 24 treasures are too many to catch in one viewing.
  - Falling snow hides the loop join, and YouTube counts every replay as a view [fact].
  - "Would you trust it?" feeds the practicality debates that fill these comment sections, in many languages [measured].

### The room

- **Size and shape**: 2.6 × 3.1 m (8.06 m²), 2.8 m ceiling. x is east, y is north.
  - **North: the loft and the den.**
    - The deck runs the full width from y 2.15 to 3.1, top at 1.38 m (underside 1.30 m), with an oak fascia, two oak posts and an oak slat rail.
    - Mattress 1.70 × 0.85 × 0.15 m, top at about 1.53 m.
    - Headboard cubby at the west end (x 0-0.28) with books and a torch.
    - Tree on the east landing.
    - Window x 0.95-2.00, z 1.62-2.55, with a central mullion. Through it: snow, pines and a neighbour's roof.
    - **Change**: the window is fixed glazing, with no handle or opening sash, because the sill sits only about 0.09 m above the mattress. State it in the pinned plan.
    - Den underneath, 1.30 m clear: a desk with a drawer, a kid's chair, a cubby with four fabric bins, a two-shelf book wall, a lamp and an LED strip.
  - **East: the Advent Stair.**
    - x 2.10-2.60 (0.50 m wide). Six 0.23 m rises and 0.22 m treads from y 0.83 to the deck at 2.15; the top tread is flush with the deck.
    - 21 drawers in the stepped side face, which faces the room (fronts about 0.22 × 0.23 m). 3 more in the top three risers, facing south.
    - **Change**: add a slim oak guard on the open (west) side of treads 4-6: two thin balusters, with a top rail that joins the loft rail and doubles as the handrail. It stands on the treads, above the side face, so it never covers a drawer.
    - Under the landing: a closed cupboard, which the long cut opens as the wardrobe.
  - **South-west corner**: the doorway, where the frame-1 camera stands.
- **Palette**:
  - **Shell**: white walls and ceiling, a pale grey subfloor at frame 1, and a light oak floor, fascia, posts and treads.
  - **Change (stair fronts)**: make the outer face of all 24 fronts the light-oak material ('oakl') and the inner face white, with white knobs. A closed drawer then reads as oak, and an open one as a white ledge with a coloured treasure on it. (Trends file: "light oak fronts, not more white".)
  - **Accent**: forest green on the tree and the throw. Gold only on the star; baubles in the treasures' muted colours; red on two or three small treasures at most, never next to gold.
  - **Change (duvet)**: make the duvet a plain matte ochre (not metallic gold) instead of white linen. It lands at 2.32 s and is a large warm patch in the first view.
  - **Colour from filled storage**:
    - the 24 treasures;
    - the cubby books (2.84 s), the fabric bins (3.8-4.1 s) and the book wall (4.8-5.1 s).
  - **Targets**: brightness 138-178, saturation 0.17-0.38, warmth 22-57.
    - [measured] Our white kitchen v2 measured saturation 0.11 and warmth 14.5.
    - [measured] The three best 2026 uploads sit at saturation 0.22-0.24 and warmth 24-32.
- **Light**:
  - Bright snowy daylight with a sunbeam, plus a fill light and a ceiling fill.
  - **Change**: make the window sky light neutral. It is now slightly blue (12.0, 12.4, 13.2); use equal channels. The view outside can stay cool, because it lights only the camera.
  - **Lights on** at 12.20 s (15 s cut) and 39.00 s (long cut):
    - first the tree and the star; then the rail lights at +0.12 s, the den strip at +0.26 s, the lamp at +0.34 s and the downlights at +0.44 s;
    - daylight stays at full exposure, so the room gets brighter, never darker;
    - check that brightness stays at 178 or under and warmth at 57 or under.

### Frame 1 and the hook

- **0.0 s**: the empty white room from the high doorway corner, in bright snow light.
  - Camera: (0.22, 0.18, 1.98), yaw 36°, pitch −17°, vfov 85. It cranes down and pushes in to (0.36, 0.42, 1.68) by 2.9 s.
  - [inference, computed] The whole window is in frame on the left, with snow already moving. The stair will fill about 42% of the width on the right.
- **0.14-0.39 s**: the floor planks slap down, so the first landing comes by 0.14 s.
- **0.50-0.85 s**: the six stair blocks thud in, bottom up.
- **1.02-1.38 s**: the tease. One bottom-row drawer drops its front on the scale's top note, with a glint, then shuts.
  - **Change**: the drawer front is only about 6% of the frame width, so make the glint about 0.16 m across (it is now 0.07 m) and brighter. Keep the 0.3 s scale-up-and-out.
  - This asks a question without words. The cascade and the star answer it.

### 15 s cut

  - **Change**: the stair guard lands at about 2.02 s;

  - **Change**: hold all 24 open for 1.5 s, then start the close at 9.96 s with drawers 0.02 s apart. The last drawer shuts at about 10.63 s and the final thud plays at about 10.64 s.
    - At present the close starts at 10.16 s, 0.035 s apart, so the last drawer shuts at about 11.18 s, after the swing into station D. The session's own PLAN says 9.9-10.7 s.

- **Pace**:
  - A new zone every 3-8 s, in 7 zones: the loft (S1-S2, 0.0-5.7 s), the stair foot and den desk (S3-S4, 6.0-11.3 s), the den cubby and the under-landing wardrobe (S5-S7, 11.6-19.7 s), the book wall and play corner (S8-S9, 20.0-25.4 s), the cascade (S10, 25.7-31.1 s), the child and the star (S11-S12, 31.4-36.9 s), and the climb, lights and loop (S13-S15, 37.2-44.0 s).
  - [inference, from the schedule] Shots, counted as hold plus swing, have a median of about 2.8 s, the hits' figure. [measured] Shot length barely separates hits (2.8 s) from flops (3.0 s), so treat it as a guide.

### Storage moments

1. **The tease**: a bottom-row drawer drops its front with a glint, then shuts. (Both cuts; **Change**: a bigger glint.)
2. **The 24-drawer cascade** (the hero): drawers open bottom to top, 0.12 s apart, each tray sliding out and its front dropping into a ledge. All 24 are held open for 1.5 s, then they close in a fast descending ripple. (Both.)
   - The treasures carry no letters or logos: a wooden car, baubles, a duck, a candy cane, blocks, a bell, a rocket, a mitten, a teddy, gifts, a yo-yo, a mini tree, a sock, a drum, a sweet, a train, a wooden star, a top, a bunny and a gingerbread-style house. The last drawer holds the gold star.
3. **The top drawer** (the person moment): the child opens it and the star flies to the tree. (Both.)
4. **Toy bins**: four fabric bins drop into the den cubby with toys peeking out. (Both.)
5. **Desk drawer**: it slides out, fills with pencils and paper, and shuts. (Both.)
6. **Book wall**: books fill both shelves in a sweep of colour. (Both.)
7. **Headboard cubby**: books and a torch. (Both.)
8. **Under-landing wardrobe**: the door opens, small clothes fill the rail, two drawers fill, and the door shuts. (Long.)
9. **Pull-out bottom step**: it slides out as a half-height first step, with slippers inside, then slides back. (Long.)

### The person moment

- A faceless child figure (figure.py 'child', about 1.15 m) uses the stair as a stair.
- **15 s cut**: the child pops onto tread 3, steps up to tread 4 and bend-reaches to the top riser drawer, which sits 0.23-0.46 m above its feet and about 0.33 m ahead. The star flies to the tree as the lights come on.
  - **Change**: build the reach as a new bend-and-reach pose dict: more spine bend than reach_fwd's 18°, with the arm angled down. Check it with kid.fk so the mitten meets the knob. Lock each foot to the centre of a tread.
- **Long cut**: the child walks in, climbs treads 1-4 at 0.5 s a step, opens the top drawer and sends the star to the tree. Once the drawer has shut, the child climbs to the landing and crouches beside the tree as it lights up.
- The child never stands on a tread under an open drawer. That makes "would you trust it?" winnable on screen.

### Sound

- **Keep** rooms/advent_drawer_wall/music_advent.py:
  - 120 BPM in F major: pad, e-piano and bass from the shared synth;
  - synthesised sleigh bells on the 8ths;
  - a glockenspiel figure in the intro and at lights-on.
- **The cascade scale**:
  - drawer n plays the n-th note of F major, from F4 (349 Hz) up to A7 (3,520 Hz) on the last drawer;
  - the close ripple plays the scale back down, more quietly;
  - the tease plays the top note.
- **Landings**: a thud for every big piece, ticks for small items, and a swish on drawer opens.
- **Check**: listen on a phone speaker. If the top octave sounds shrill, lower its gain; keep the notes as they are.
- At 0.02 s spacing the close ripple becomes a fast run, which suits a snap-shut.
- [measured] Sound did not separate hits from flops (3.5 against 3.8 distinct sounds per second), but commenters single out playful effects (214 likes). The picture must work on mute.

### Risks and fixes

- **Deadline.** [inference] The README gives about 35 s per frame on 4 cores: about 3.6 h for the 15 s cut (375 frames) and 10.7 h for the long cut (1,100 frames). The production HANDOFF.md gives 17-26 s per frame at 720x1280 and 16 samples per pixel (render quality): 1.8-2.7 h and 5.2-7.9 h.
  - Lock and render the 15 s cut first.
  - Release the long cut only if it is ready by Wed 2 Dec.
  - If time runs short, drop the snow first.
  - Any second Christmas cut must post by Sun 13 Dec; otherwise keep the festive props for a fresh evergreen render in January (see Seasonal reach).
- **Safety debate.** The stair is 1.38 m high and open on the room side. Its 230 mm rises are steeper than UK and US house-stair rules allow (about 220 mm and 196 mm) [fact, building rules; not in the research files]. The three riser drawers open over the tread below.
  - Add the oak guard and the fixed window.
  - Keep the child off any tread under an open drawer.
  - Pin the plan and call it a loft-bed stair.
- **Colour drift.** [measured] Off-band colour marked 13 of 25 flops against 2 of 25 hits, and white-heavy renders fall low (our kitchen v2: saturation 0.11).
  - Use light-oak fronts, neutral window light and the ochre duvet.
  - Run hh_metrics on the low-res preview before any Blender time.
  - If warmth is still under 22, recolour the rug a soft mustard. If it tips over 57, or the frame looks yellow, go back to the linen duvet.
- **Noise after lights-on and grainy snow.**
  - List the glowing materials (bulb_on, star_glow, led, dl_emis and lamp_bulb) as camera-only, in the room's BLENDER['separate'] list. They then show only to the camera and in reflections, and the existing light slots do the lighting.
  - [inference, from the flake sizes] Far-layer flakes are about 2-4 px at 720x1280. Make near flakes at least 13 mm in radius and far flakes at least 17 mm, or drop the far layer.
  - Render 3 test frames after lights-on at final settings before the full render.
- **Seasonal reach.** "Advent" reads only where the custom is known, and the video fades after 25 Dec.
  - The caption carries the 24 days; the scale and the star work without it.
  - The festive props are separate pieces, so an evergreen version can be rendered fresh in January, never re-cut.
- **Freshness.** The concept echoes Haven Haus #4 ("8-Square Meters Children's Room…", 5,673,214 views) and Haven Haus #2's loft, stair drawers and walking boy.
  - The stepped side-face grid with drop-down ledges, the scale and the countdown make it our own.
  - The caption no longer opens with "8 m² kids' room".
- **Kept: the push-in through the hold.** The review asked for a still framing of the whole stair until the close. The session's glide already keeps 23-24 of 24 drawers in frame, so stopping it would only add a still stretch.
- **Kept: the build order of S1 and S2.** The review asked to move the mattress, duvet, tree and desk into 3.4-5.2 s. The session already lands them in A and B, and moving them would empty frame 1's build.
- **Kept: the window size.** Raising the sill to 2.0 m would shrink the snowy view that sets frame 1 and hides the loop join, so fixed glazing answers the safety point instead.
- **Kept: planks first.** The first landing is at 0.14 s, and the stair lands on a floor from 0.50 s. Landing the stair by 0.3 s would add nothing.

### Build notes

- **Coordination**:
  - This list is for the running session. Edit only rooms/advent_drawer_wall/project.py and PLAN.md.
  - No shared pipeline change is needed. music_advent.py sets each tick's freq after load_cfg, so music.py and Timeline.ev stay as they are.
  - Coordinator action: the two-kids session (#1 Tape Line, Turning Wall) also has five stair drawers that fill bottom to top while a child climbs, in the same posting week. Ask it to swap them for a lift-up step or toe-kick drawers, or post the two videos further apart.
- **Changes in project.py**:
  - build_stair(): swap the front materials (light oak outside, white inside) and use white knobs.
  - A new stair_guard piece on treads 4-6, landing at about 2.02 s.
  - The duvet material: matte ochre (not metallic gold).
  - lights(): make the sky light pure white (LSKY with equal channels).
  - The glint: about 0.16 m across and brighter.
  - SEPARATE: add the five small emitter materials.
  - The snow flake radii.
  - timeline_short(): T_CL = 9.96, close spacing 0.02, and the child timings and bend-and-reach pose above.
  - timeline_long(): the 15-station schedule above.
- **New pieces for the long cut only**: the pull-out bottom step with slippers, a floor cushion and a toy basket. Reuse the wardrobe, rug and bins already built.
- **Effort**: small to medium. Most of it is parameters; the new work is the guard, the long timeline and the walk-and-climb.
- **Hard to render, and how to avoid it**:
  - the tree: keep the session's smooth lathe cones;
  - snow: larger, fewer flakes, nothing settling;
  - glints: no bloom, a small emissive sparkle;
  - the figure: the faceless mannequin, with the star flying rather than gripped;
  - text: no numbers or letters on drawers, blank book spines, no logos on toys.
- **Checks**:
  - ROOM_REST probes of every station (check that the guard clears the star's flight and the camera).
  - A 360×640 phone-size preview.
  - hh_metrics on the preview.
  - 3 Blender test frames after lights-on.
  - Aim to miss at most one target. [measured] Against all nine hh_metrics targets, 6 of 29 hits pass every one and the median flop misses 5.5. Leaving out the length target, which a 15 s cut can't meet, 9 of 29 hits pass and the median flop misses 5. The median hit misses 1 either way.

## 5. The Bed on the Ceiling

- **At a glance**:
  - Candidate C9. Judges' mean 6.6; reviewer score 7 (the highest of the 12), given before this revision.
  - Plan ready, not started.
  - Posting: the 15 s cut on Sat 19 Dec, the long cut on Wed 23 Dec.
- **Why it ranks here**:
  - The biggest single picture in the slate, a made bed coming down from the ceiling, on the strongest room type [inference; measured].
  - It could read as a flip-bed gimmick: "Flip the Bed" ran 0.43 [measured].
  - Ceiling beds are not new, so the caption must never claim invention.

### Pitch

A 9 m² bedroom builds as a bright room with a sofa and a desk. In second 1 a fully made double bed rises off the new floor and docks on the ceiling. Later a stand-in presses a button on a steel post, and the bed glides 2.06 m down onto the sofa. In the long cut it goes back up at the end.

### Post caption (in the post, never on screen)

"9 m² (3 × 3 m, about 10 × 10 ft) bedroom that also fits a sofa and a desk: the bed lives on the ceiling until bedtime. Would you sit under it? Send this to the friend whose bed eats their room."

- Pin three things: the plan with dimensions; "Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app."; and the power-cut answer (see Risks and fixes).

### Why it should work

- [measured] Bedrooms are the strongest room type at 1.51 times normal (47 Shorts). Living rooms run 0.70, and titles naming a size run 1.34.
- [measured] report.md says all 4 best 2026 uploads (290K-547K) were bedrooms or kids' rooms with a raised or loft bed. The frame notes in deep_analysis.csv confirm the raised or loft bed for 3 of them. The 4th (536K, era-adjusted 16.75, the best of the four "recent_top" videos; the best of 2026 is the 853K kids' room at 26.66) is a small-bedroom layout fix: its title says the bed was moved out of the middle. Three raise-the-bed titles ran 10.39 (343K), 7.81 (289K) and 2.53 (149K). [inference] A bed that leaves the floor completely is the far end of the raised-bed premise and is a layout fix too. So the caption calls the room a bedroom and gives its size.
- [measured] 28 of 29 hits build or transform a whole room, against 12 of 30 flops. Add a neutral palette and it is 27 of 29 against 4 of 30. [inference] All four walls build here, and the descent changes the whole room, not one piece.
- [measured] The hit's camera moved more than its flop's in 21 of 25 pairs (2.9 against 1.0, p = 0.0009). report.md calls this the biggest gap between our kitchen render and the hits. Between hits and flops, whole-room scope, length and motion separate even more strongly. The 24 s two-beds Short (991K) has the highest camera score of the 29 hits (11.88). It is 2nd of all 59, behind a 6,836-view kitchen dimension guide (12.1), so camera movement helps but does not make a hit on its own. [inference] The ride camera lowers with the bed and never stops drifting.
- [measured] The top comments argue about practicality: a fridge with no room to vent (167 likes), "how do you make your bed?" (32). [inference] People will ask "what about a power cut?" without being prompted. The picture already answers it: a made bed held by straps, and a crank socket at hand height. That keeps the argument friendly.
- [measured, confounded by account size] Premise videos have a median of 307,650 likes, against 634 for plain showcases. [fact, trends file; indicative] Instagram ranks on watch time, likes per reach and sends per reach. Content that inspires awe is shared more (Berger & Milkman, 2012). [inference] A made bed docking on the ceiling at 1.2 s raises the question "when does it come down?". The descent supplies the awe, and the caption asks for a send.

### The room

- **Size and shape**: 9 m², 3.0 × 3.0 m, 2.85 m ceiling, a plain box (x east, y north). The plan is rotated from the 11 m² bedroom project: bed bay on the west wall, window and desk on the north wall, wardrobe on the east wall, door in the south-east corner.
  - **West (bed bay)**:
    - A 2.0 × 0.75 m blue-grey sofa (x 0.06-0.81, y 0.50-2.50, seat 0.42 m), with three padded back pads fixed to the wall, each 6 cm thick.
    - Above it, the 1.4 × 2.0 m bed parks flat under the ceiling (x 0.08-1.48, y 0.50-2.50, head to the north). It is 0.30 m thick with the bedding, and its underside sits at 2.50 m, so there is 2.50 m clear below. A visible 2 cm gap separates the bed's back edge from the pads.
    - Two book towers, 0.40 × 0.35 m and floor to ceiling, in the south-west and north-west corners.
    - Four 65 mm steel guide posts at the bed's corners, each with a floor plate and a ceiling plate. The back two stand beside the towers' inner faces, and the front two stand free about 1.5 m out from the wall (x about 1.51). A slim steel rail along the ceiling ties the four post tops together, and the drive belts are hidden inside the posts.
    - A motor box hidden in the top of the north-west tower. A crank socket at 1.1 m on the front-south post, and an unlabelled button pad at 1.1 m on the front-north post.
    - The bed travels 2.06 m and stops on the posts 2 cm above the sofa cushions (mattress top 0.68 m), so the sofa carries no weight. There are no shelves over the sofa, so the bed's path stays clear.
  - **North**:
    - A 1.0 m window (x 1.80-2.80, sill 0.95 m) with a plain linen roller blind.
    - Under it, a 1.4 × 0.55 m light-oak desk at 0.75 m (x 1.60-3.00): a 0.95 m knee space, then a three-drawer pedestal (0.45 m) at the east end, in line with the wardrobe's north end. The chair tucks fully under the desk whenever it is not in use.
    - A bedside alcove (x 0.35-1.50) between the north-west tower and the desk. It is the ottoman's parking spot, and the tower's east face holds a fold-out nightstand.
  - **East**: a 1.4 × 0.6 m floor-to-ceiling wardrobe (x 2.40-3.00, y 0.90-2.30) with two sections behind two sliding fronts, one light oak and one mirror, with steel pulls.
    - South section: long hanging over a shoe pull-out.
    - North section: tee drawers at the bottom, then a fold-down ironing board stored upright above them, then folded stacks on the shelves. The board folds out west at 0.85 m and is 0.80 m long, so it stops 12 cm short of the bed's front edge.
  - **South**: a 0.7 m door at the east end (x 2.15-2.85). Its swing clears the wardrobe by 0.2 m. Three oak peg hooks west of the door (a coat, a tote bag), clear of the front-south post.
  - **Floor**: about 0.9 m between the bed's front edge and the wardrobe. A 0.40 m storage ottoman on castors stands in front of the sofa by day.
- **Palette**:
  - Shell: neutral white walls and ceiling (about sRGB 242, 241, 238), not the bedroom project's warm white (238, 233, 225), which sits at the edge of the cream zone. Pale grey subfloor at frame 1.
  - Wood: light oak for the floor, the tower shelves, the bed frame and underside, the desk and one wardrobe front. No walnut.
  - One accent: the muted blue-grey sofa and wall pads. Muted blues are 2 of the 7 paint-brand picks for 2027 [fact, trends file; snapshots].
  - Colour comes from filled storage:
    - books in a gradient across the towers (mustard, ochre, sage, slate blue), on screen by 2.5 s in both cuts;
    - plain-coloured board-game boxes;
    - clothes (rust, denim, sage, camel);
    - mustard and pale blue-grey sofa pillows, and a terracotta throw on the white duvet.
  - Target the hits' band: saturation 0.17-0.38, warmth 22-57, brightness 138-178.
- **Light**:
  - Daylight only. A warm, low morning sun comes through the north window at a slant, laying a sunbeam across the bare subfloor inside the frame-1 view, with a soft sky fill.
  - The window never switches to night, so the bedroom project's day-to-night swap is dropped. [measured] 13 of 25 flops sit off the colour band, against 2 of 25 hits. Off the band means a cream or orange cast (warmth above 60), oversaturation (0.45 or more) or darkness (brightness under 125).
  - The bed's four downlights blink as a "the bed is moving" signal: in the long-cut tease at 14.4 s, and at 26.4 s just before the descent.
  - Lights-on, 15 s cut: at 12.0 s a passing cloud dims the sun by no more than 15%, with brightness kept at 138 or more. Then the LED strip in the ceiling rail, the tower shelf LEDs, the nightstand lamp and the desk lamp come on, staggered 12.20-12.45 s.
  - Lights-on, long cut: the downlights are the main beat. They come on as the bed starts back up (44.6 s), and their pools slide up the sofa as it rises. Then the rail LED, the shelf LEDs and the lamps come on at 47.5-47.8 s.
  - Keep the sky light on and the exposure as in the bedroom project (-0.38).

### Frame 1 and the hook

At 0.0 s, the empty room is seen from the south-east corner at 1.5 m, looking north-west at the bare west wall and the ceiling. A sunbeam lies across the grey subfloor, and the camera eases into a push-in. By 0.3 s the first light-oak planks slap down against the far wall. They land far to near and are all down by 0.65 s. At 0.60 s an obviously made double bed (white duvet, terracotta throw, pillows) rises off the new floor against the west wall. It docks on the ceiling with an upside-down clunk at 1.20 s, and the camera tilts up after it. The premise is clear in second 1, and the suspense is when the bed comes down.

### 15 s cut

- 0.0-3.0 s · S1 south-east corner (frame-1 view):
  - Planks land 0.30-0.65 s. The bed rises 0.60-1.20 s and docks.
  - The ceiling rail runs out round the bed at 1.25-1.40 s. The four posts drop in whole at 1.40-1.85 s, with crank socket and button pad already fitted, and four bright tinks as the floor plates land.
  - Both towers slide in whole at 1.85-2.10 s, and the motor box clicks into the north-west tower top at 2.15 s.
  - Books cascade onto the shelves at 2.15-2.45 s, so colour is on screen.
  - The sofa box drops at 2.55 s, the wall pads slap on at 2.65-2.75 s, and the cushions land at 2.85 s.
  - Camera: an eased-in push-in north-west (about 0.3 m), tilting up after the bed (pitch 6° to 14°).
- 3.3-7.0 s · S2 wardrobe and desk (from under the front of the parked bed, looking east):
  - The wardrobe carcass slides out of the east wall at 3.40 s, and long garments swish onto the rail at 3.70-4.00 s.
  - The tee drawer opens at 4.15 s, fills at 4.30-4.55 s and shuts at 4.75 s. The mirror front slides across at 4.95 s.
  - The desk top slides out of the north wall at 5.25 s and the pedestal drops at 5.45 s. Its top drawer opens, fills (notebooks, charger) and shuts, 5.60-6.20 s.
  - The chair rolls in and tucks under at 6.35 s. The laptop and lamp land at 6.55 and 6.70 s.
  - Camera: a slow slide north-east (about 0.4 m), panning left from the wardrobe to the desk (yaw 82° to 38°).
- 7.3-10.7 s · S3 the ride (a three-quarter front view from the south-east):
  - The stand-in pops in by the front-north post and presses its button at 7.65 s. By 7.90 s it has stepped back about 0.4 m to the south, clear of the bed. The bed's downlights come on at 7.85 s.
  - **Wow**: the bed descends 7.95-10.15 s (2.2 s, eased in and out, motor hum) and lands with the deepest thud of the video at 10.15 s. The straps unclip at 10.25 s and the pillows bounce once at 10.35 s.
  - Camera: starts at 1.75 m and lowers about 0.5 m with the bed, following its eased curve. A slow sideways drift runs through the whole station, so the camera never locks off before or after the move. The bed, the sofa and the closing gap stay in frame together, and the front-north post stays near the right edge.
- 11.0-15.0 s · S4 wide:
  - It starts low near the bed. A 0.7 m drawer in the middle of the bed's front rail slides out (pyjamas) at 11.15 s and shuts at 11.45 s. The nightstand flips out of the north-west tower at 11.55 s, and a lamp, phone and mug land on it at 11.75-12.05 s.
  - The stand-in sits on the bed edge, just north of the drawer.
  - The cloud dims the sun at 12.0 s, and the lights come on 12.20-12.45 s.
  - Camera: a slow drift back (pull back, rise, tilt up) to the exact frame-1 pose by 15.0 s, easing out over the last 0.4 s. S1 eases in over its first 0.4 s, so the loop seam is near-still for under 1 s.
  - The loop restarts on the empty room, and the bed rising at 0.6 s echoes the bed you just saw. It is an echo, not a seamless match.

### Long cut (40-50 s)

49.5 s in 8 zones and 15 camera stations, each 2-4 s long, every one gliding, joined by 0.3 s swings. The wow lands at 30.0 s (61%), and the bed goes back up at 44.7-47.0 s, so the wow plays twice. No end card.

- **0.0-6.6 s · Hook and towers**
  - L1 (0.0-3.0, frame-1 view, push-in, tilt after the bed): planks land, the bed rises at 0.60-1.20, the rail runs out at 1.25-1.40, the posts drop in whole at 1.40-1.85, the towers slide in whole at 1.9-2.2, the motor box lands at 2.4, and the first shelf of books lands at 2.5.
  - L2 (3.3-6.6, low slide south to north along the bay, facing west): books land shelf by shelf in the gradient, 3.4-5.6. One cubby in the south-west tower is left empty. The crank socket clicks onto the front-south post at 5.9, and the button pad clicks onto the front-north post at 6.2.
- **6.9-13.4 s · Sofa bay**
  - L3 (6.9-10.2, orbit left to right): the seat box lands at 7.0, the wall pads at 7.3-7.5 and the cushions at 7.8. Both gas-strut lids lift at 8.2, board games stack in at 8.4-8.9, throws at 9.0-9.3, and the lids shut at 9.6.
  - L4 (10.5-13.4, push-in at seat height): the mustard and pale blue-grey pillows land at 10.6 and 10.8. The ottoman rolls in at 11.0; its lid lifts on slippers and a throw at 11.4 and shuts at 12.3. The rug lands at 12.6 and a plant on a tower at 13.0.
- **13.7-16.6 s · The mechanism**
  - L5 (13.7-16.6, crane up beside the bed's front edge): the four downlights blink twice at 14.4. The camera rises past the crank socket and a belt slot in the front post to the ceiling rail. It then glides along the mattress side, a strap buckle and the safety-sensor strip.
- **16.9-23.4 s · Window desk**
  - L6 (16.9-20.1, slide west to east, facing the window): the desk top lands at 17.0 and the pedestal at 17.3. Three drawers open, fill and shut in a cascade, 17.6-19.0. The laptop, lamp and mug land at 19.2-19.7, and the chair at 19.9.
  - L7 (20.4-23.4, slow orbit behind the chair): the stand-in walks in from the door at 20.4-21.3 and sits at the desk at 21.4. The laptop opens at 21.7 and the roller blind rolls up at 22.3.
- **23.7-31.0 s · The descent**
  - L8 (23.7-26.8, from the north-east corner above the desk, slow push south-west): the stand-in stands at 23.8 and pushes the chair under the desk at 24.1. The ottoman rolls into the bedside alcove at 24.5, and the sofa pillows hop into the empty cubby at 24.9 and 25.1. The stand-in walks to the front-north post, presses the button at 25.8, and by 26.1 has stepped back east, clear of the bed and the ottoman's path. The downlights come on at 26.4.
  - L9 (27.1-31.0, the ride: the same three-quarter front view from the south-east, lowering about 0.5 m with a slow sideways drift): **wow**. The bed descends 27.6-30.0 (2.4 s, eased, with a hum) and lands at 30.0 with the deepest thud. The straps unclip at 30.1 and the pillows bounce at 30.2.
- **31.3-37.5 s · Bed-down storage**
  - L10 (31.3-34.4, low slide along the bed's front rail): the bed drawer slides out at 31.5. Pyjamas, a book and an eye mask drop in at 31.7-32.2, and it shuts at 32.5. The stand-in walks over and sits on the bed edge at 32.7-33.5.
  - L11 (34.7-37.5, orbit round the head end): the nightstand flips out of the north-west tower at 34.9. The lamp, phone and mug land at 35.2-35.6, and the phone screen glows at 35.9.
- **37.8-44.0 s · Wardrobe**
  - L12 (37.8-40.9, facing east, slide south to north): the carcass slides out of the wall at 37.9 and long garments go on at 38.3-38.8. The tee drawer opens, fills and shuts at 39.0-39.8. Folded stacks land at 40.0-40.5, and the mirror front slides across the hanging section at 40.7.
  - L13 (41.2-44.0, three-quarter push-in on the north section): the stand-in walks over at 41.2-42.0. It folds the ironing board down at 42.2 (its leg swings down) and folds it back up at 42.8. The mirror front slides back, and the shoe pull-out glides out at 43.1 and back in at 43.6.
- **44.3-49.5 s · Up again and lights on**
  - L14 (44.3-47.2, from the north-east corner, higher than L8, slow tilt up with a slow pull-in): the stand-in presses the post button at 44.4 and steps back by 44.6. The downlights come on at 44.6. The bed rises at 44.7-47.0 (2.3 s, the same easing as the descent, with a hum) and docks softly with a low clunk and no overshoot.
  - L15 (47.5-49.5, a 2.0 s drifting wide): the rail LED, shelf LEDs, desk lamp and nightstand lamp come on at 47.5-47.8. The camera drifts back to the frame-1 pose, easing out over its last 0.4 s.

### Storage moments (10, numbered)

1. **Book towers** (build): two floor-to-ceiling towers fill shelf by shelf with a book gradient. One cubby in the south-west tower stays empty until the sofa pillows hop into it just before the descent.
2. **Sofa seat** (reveal): two lids lift on gas struts over board games and folded throws, then shut.
3. **Ottoman coffee table** (reveal, then a move): its lid lifts on slippers and a spare throw. Before the descent it rolls into the bedside alcove, clearing the bed's landing zone.
4. **Desk pedestal** (build): three drawers open in a cascade, fill with notebooks, cables and a charger, and shut.
5. **Bed drawer** (reveal): a 0.7 m drawer in the bed's front rail that only opens once the bed is down, holding pyjamas, a book and an eye mask.
6. **Fold-out nightstand** (build): a flap drops out of the north-west tower's face at mattress height, and a lamp, phone and mug land on it.
7. **Wardrobe hanging section** (build): coats and dresses swish onto the rail.
8. **Wardrobe drawers** (build): a drawer of tees opens, fills and shuts, and folded stacks land on the shelves above.
9. **Fold-down ironing board** (reveal, in use): the stand-in folds it down to waist height, then folds it away.
10. **Mirror front** (reveal): it slides across, and the shoe pull-out under the hanging section glides out with shoes and back.

### The person moment

- **The figure**: the adult stand-in from pipeline/figure.py (about 1.68 m, a faceless mannequin). Start with fig_adult and switch to fig_sage if it fades into the white walls.
- **15 s cut**:
  - It pops in by the front-north post at 7.3 s and presses the button at 1.1 m with a forward right-arm reach (sh_r about 60, elbow_r about 10). With the shoulder at about 1.40 m and a 0.6 m arm, that puts the hand at about 1.10 m.
  - It steps back about 0.4 m to the south, at least 0.35 m east of the bed's front edge, and looks up (neck -24) while the bed comes down.
  - In S4 it sits on the bed edge (Figure.sit_pose(0.68)).
- **Long cut adds**:
  - it walks in and works at the desk (walk, then the sit_desk pose), then pushes the chair in;
  - it sits on the bed edge after the bed drawer closes;
  - it folds the ironing board down and up;
  - then it sends the bed back up and stays clear, looking up.
- **Safety**: it is never under or on the bed while the bed moves. The bed only moves when the figure is at least 0.35 m east of the bed's front edge (x 1.83 or more) and out of the ottoman's path.

### Sound

- **Landing sounds**: soft ticks for planks, and a quick riffle for each shelf of books. A short metal slide for the ceiling rail, and four bright steel tinks for the post plates. Thuds for the sofa, desk, wardrobe and towers. A gas-strut hiss for the seat lids. A slide and a thud on every drawer. Swishes for garments and the roller blind.
- **The mechanism**:
  - a deep upside-down clunk when the bed docks (1.20 s), and a softer one on the long-cut dock (47.0 s);
  - a low motor hum that swells and fades with the eased descent and the rise;
  - the deepest thud of the video on landing.
  - Dip the music about 4 dB under the hum so it reads.
- **Two playful effects**:
  - a cartoon "boing" when the pillows bounce (15 s: 10.35 s; long cut: 30.2 s);
  - a "bip-bip" when the downlights blink in the tease (long cut: 14.4 s).
  - [measured] Commenters single out cartoon sound effects (214 likes).
- **Music**: original, at 118 BPM.
  - [measured, weak evidence] 12 of 25 hits sit at about 111-125 BPM, against 5 of 25 flops.
  - [measured] There is no need to cut on the beat: 23% of the hits' scene changes land on a strong sound, against 24% by chance.

### Risks and fixes

- **It looks fake, AI-made or unsafe.**
  - Show the mechanism from 1.25 s: the ceiling rail, 65 mm posts with floor and ceiling plates, belts hidden in the posts, the crank socket at hand height, an unlabelled button, and a thin safety-sensor strip on the bed's front rail.
  - Use the same easing both ways, with a soft dock and no bounce on the bed itself: 2.2 s down in the 15 s cut, 2.4 s down and 2.3 s up in the long cut. Stop the bed 2 cm above the cushions, and keep the stand-in clear.
  - Pin the power-cut reply: "a self-locking drive holds it where it is, and a hand crank at hand height on the front post lowers it". This is a design answer, not research.
  - Keep our own look: round steel posts and an oak frame, with nothing modelled on a commercial ceiling-bed product.
- **It is not new to the genre.** Ceiling beds are sold commercially [general knowledge], and the trends file lists an undated 3D Short, "Motorized loft bed in a 6 m² drum room" (no view counts).
  - Never claim invention. Sell what is ours: a sofa, a desk and 10 storage moments in 9 m², the pillows hopping into the empty cubby, and the figure using the bed.
  - Keep the household angle in the caption.
- **It reads as a living room (0.70) or a flip-bed gimmick.** [measured, videos.csv] "Small Bedroom? Flip the Bed, Double the Space!" ran 0.43. The 6 titles with fold, flip or multi run a median of 0.88.
  - The made bed is on the ceiling from 1.2 s, so it reads as a bedroom from the start.
  - The caption says "bedroom" with its size, and never "fold" or "flip".
  - The wow lands at 61% of the long cut.
- **Still moments, or a grey, cool colour.**
  - The camera drifts through the whole ride, and small pieces land around every long move. Books are on screen by 2.5 s.
  - Run `python3 research/hh_metrics.py <video>` on both cuts. Hold to motion 7.5+, longest still 1.8 s or less, camera movement 1.8+, warmth 22-57 and saturation 0.17+.
  - With all 9 targets, only 6 of 29 hits pass (the median hit misses 1, the median flop 5.5). Leaving out length, 9 of 29 pass (the median flop misses 5). So treat the other targets as direction.
  - The long cut (49.5 s) sits above hh_metrics' 34-46 s length target. It is still inside the top 12's 34-53 s and the owner's 40-50 s format.
  - The kitchen's near-neutral whites measured warmth 14.5 and saturation 0.11, and a blue-grey accent pulls cooler still. So the sun is warm from frame 1, and the warmth comes from the light oak, the mustard books and pillows and the terracotta throw, not from the walls, which stay neutral white (242, 241, 238) like the rest of the slate.
- **It repeats the 11 m² bedroom project.** That project has a north bed nook, an east window desk with sheers, the door at the west end of the south wall, a three-panel oak-mirror-oak wardrobe with brass pulls and a pull-down rail, and ceiling downlights.
  - Here the plan is rotated: bed bay west, desk and window north, wardrobe east, door in the south-east corner.
  - The pull-down rail becomes an ironing board, the sheers a roller blind, and the colour-blocked books a gradient. The pillows are new (mustard and pale blue-grey), the wardrobe has two fronts with steel pulls, and the downlights ride on the bed.
  - New camera paths and a new frame-1 corner. Post it as a separate video, never as a near-copy.
- **Lights-on is lost in daylight.** In the 15 s cut the bed's downlights face the sofa once it is down, so a passing cloud and the ceiling-rail LED carry the beat. In the long cut the downlights are the main beat as the bed rises.
- **The posting slot clashes with near-duplicates.** Trends idea #6 (a guest-ready study where a wall bed drops, posted 1-12 Dec) and next_videos #4 (a studio panel that folds down into a bed) deliver the same beat.
  - Post the 15 s cut on Sat 19 Dec and the long cut on Wed 23 Dec, at least 5 days after #3's long cut. Trends idea #6 (the guest-ready study) is not on this slate, so there is no clash.
- **Props could carry text or logos.**
  - A laptop with no logo. The laptop and phone screens show a plain gradient glow with no interface.
  - Blank book spines and covers, plain board-game boxes and plain clothes.
  - No labels, numbers, brand plate or warning stickers on the motor box, button pad or bed: a plain, unlabelled button with no icons.
- **The render budget is optimistic.** The README's 35 s per frame was measured on the kitchen at 18 samples with --nobevel. Books, garments, a figure and motion blur will be slower.
  - Effort is medium-high.
  - Render the 15 s cut first. Pass hh_metrics.py (camera 1.8+, longest still 1.8 s or less, warmth 22+) before starting the long cut.
- **Review fixes adapted, not taken word for word.**
  - The books run mustard to slate blue, not pure blue to sage. A cool gradient plus the blue-grey sofa would pull warmth under 22.
  - The blue-grey pillow is paler than the sofa so it does not vanish against it.
  - The ottoman parks in the bedside alcove, not under the desk. That keeps a 0.95 m knee space, and the chair never meets the wardrobe.
  - The frame-1 rise stays fast (0.6 s). It is a build move made before the posts exist, while the motor moves are the slow, matched ones.

### Build notes

- **Reuse from rooms/bedroom/project.py**:
  - `materials()` subsets: oak, birch, floor, books `bk_*` (add ochre and slate blue), clothes `cl_*`, pillows (add mustard and pale blue-grey), led, bulb, `dl_emis`. Drop `sheer`.
  - Helpers: `disc`, `garment`, `stack`, `plant`, `cloth`, `pillow`.
  - The shell pattern, resized to 3.0 × 3.0 × 2.85 m, with the door at the east end of the south wall, the window on the north wall, the day backdrop only and no ceiling downlights.
  - `build_desk`: drawer, laptop lid, lamp and chair, re-placed on the north wall, with a roller blind instead of sheers.
  - `build_wardrobe`: rails, the `wr_drB` drawer, the mirror panel and shoes, cut to 1.4 m with two fronts and steel pulls. Drop `wr_pull`.
  - Timeline helpers `door_z` (for the ironing board, its leg and the nightstand flap), `appear` and `drawer_fn`. The `base` argument of `drawer_fn` lets the bed drawer ride on the moving bed.
  - The `lights()` pattern that reads `mats4`, as the desk lamp does, so the four downlights travel with the bed.
  - The `BLENDER` dict, minus the night backdrop and the sheer setting.
- **Reuse from rooms/kitchen/project.py**: the pull-out pantry slide, for the shoe pull-out.
- **Reuse from pipeline**: `camkit.hold_camera`, and figure.py (`walk`, the `sit_desk` pose, `Figure.sit_pose`, plus a new forward-reach pose for the button).
- **New pieces**:
  - The bed: one piece `bed` with the oak frame, oak underside, four downlight discs, corner sleeves, the safety-sensor strip, mattress, a duvet from `cloth()`, the terracotta throw and two straps. Child pieces `bed_pillows` (for the bounce) and `bed_drawer` are composed on its transform.
  - One custom track, `bed_z(t)`: rise from the floor 0.60-1.20 s, park, an eased descent, and an eased rise with the same curve and no overshoot.
  - Four 65 mm steel posts (`tube`, each dropped in whole) with floor and ceiling plates, the ceiling rail with its LED strip, the motor box, the crank socket and the button pad.
  - The sofa: seat box, two gas-strut lids, `rbox` cushions and three 6 cm wall pads.
  - Two book towers with an empty cubby, the nightstand flap, the ottoman on castors, the ironing board with a swing-down leg, the roller blind and the peg hooks.
  - A custom camera segment for the ride: a lowering that follows `bed_z` with `smooth()`, plus a constant slow sideways drift, because camkit glides at constant speed.
  - A sun-dimmer track for the cloud at 12.0 s.
  - In music.py, new `hum` and `boing` sound kinds, and `music.json` set to `{"bpm": 118}`.
- **Two timelines** (15 s and 49.5 s) share `build()`.
  - In the long cut, keep the east wall mostly out of shot until L12 (check L6, L7 and L9).
  - Probe with `ROOM_REST=1` first, starting with the ride camera at the start, middle and end of the descent.
  - Starting 15 s poses to probe, as `P(x, y, z, yaw, pitch)` (yaw east of north):
    - S1: `P(2.60, 0.35, 1.50, -50, 6)` → `P(2.40, 0.55, 1.46, -52, 14)`
    - S2: `P(1.15, 0.95, 1.30, 82, -6)` → `P(1.35, 1.30, 1.26, 38, -7)`
    - S3: `P(2.55, 0.35, 1.75, -40, 8)` → `P(2.40, 0.55, 1.25, -40, -4)`, plus the sideways drift
    - S4: `P(2.10, 1.20, 1.10, -55, -8)` → the S1 start pose
- **Effort: medium-high.** The mechanism is one rigid move. The work is the new shell, re-placing the reused furniture, 15 long-cut stations, five figure actions and two timelines.
- **Render time** [inference from the README's figure of about 35 s per frame on 4 cores at 720x1280, measured on the kitchen at 18 samples with --nobevel]:
  - The camera never stops, so no frames are copied.
  - The long cut is 1,238 frames (at least about 12 h), and the 15 s cut is 375 frames (at least about 3.6 h). Expect more.
  - Preview with the code renderer first.
- **Hard to render, and avoided**:
  - the duvet is the static `cloth()` drape, with no simulation;
  - a mug, not a glass of water;
  - the faceless figure, so no hair or face;
  - the straps are thin boxes;
  - no crowds.
- **Posting**: the 15 s cut on Sat 19 Dec and the long cut on Wed 23 Dec, at least 5 days after #3's long cut (moved from 8-14 Dec). Trends idea #6 (the guest-ready study) is not on this slate, so there is no clash. It is evergreen, so the date can slip.

## 6. Pull One Book

- **At a glance**:
  - Candidate C13. Judges' mean 5.8; reviewer score 6, given before this revision.
  - Plan ready, not started.
  - Posting: the 15 s cut on Wed 6 Jan, the long cut on Mon 11 Jan.
- **Why it ranks here**:
  - A kids' loft room, the 2026 pattern [measured], where a person uses the cleverest feature.
  - The hidden den pays off in storage, not a secret [inference].
  - Two hidden-room flops were warm and still [measured], so the den's light and colour must be right.

### Pitch

A 3.4 × 2.7 m kids' room builds around a loft bed and a wall of colourful books, with one coral book sticking out. A basket tips and toys spill across the floor. A child tugs the coral book, and a section of the bookcase, still full of books, unlatches. The child pushes it back and slides it aside, like a pocket door, onto a bright den under the loft. Then the mess flies in: blocks into a bench chest, everything else into cubbies. The floor is clear and the child climbs in.

### Post caption (in the post, never on screen)

"9 m², 3.4 × 2.7 m. Pull one book and the floor is clear: loft bed, desk, wardrobe and a den for the mess. Would your kid ever come out, and would you let them? Save it for the January reset."

- Leave out "secret", "hidden" and "magic".
- Pin a comment with:
  - the top-view plan with its dimensions and the slide path;
  - the mechanics: book latch, no spring, floor runners and a top track, shelf lips, finger guard, magnetic catch, a pull on the back face, no lock;
  - "Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app."
- **Timing**: post 6-15 Jan, inside the new-year reset window (27 Dec-20 Jan in the trends file), and at least 5 weeks after #4 Advent Stair.

### Why it should work

- [measured] **Room type.**
  - Kids' rooms run at 1.35× the channel norm (48 Shorts), and titles naming a size at 1.34× (15 Shorts).
  - The best 2026 upload is a kids' room (853,468 views, Aug 2026). Second is a bedroom (689,706). Third is a small kids' room with a loft (547,264).
  - All 4 "recent_top" 2026 videos in the sample (290K-547K) are bedrooms or kids' rooms that report.md codes as raised or loft beds; the CSV frame notes confirm 3 of the 4.
  - [fact, platform-reported] Xiaohongshu's 2026 home trends name three things this room builds: kids' toe-kick step drawers, storage at a 1 m child's eye height, and a "mess-tolerant zone".
- [measured] **A door that opens, used by a person, is proven.**
  - The 8 m² kids' room (5,673,214 views): a child draws on a chalkboard door, then it opens.
  - The 12.8M loft: a hidden door opens onto the closet at 9-11 s. Separately, commenters retell a mum vanishing into the wall at the start of the video.
  - "Tiny but Magical — A 6㎡ Bedroom with a Secret Space!" ran at 4.83× normal (396K).
  - A 3D person uses a feature in 4 of the top 5. Three sampled flops also show a person, so a person alone does not separate hits from flops.
- [measured] **A whole room, and a camera that never stops.**
  - 28 of 29 hits build a whole room, against 12 of 30 flops.
  - The hit's camera moved more in 21 of 25 pairs: motion 8.4 against 5.1 per 0.2 s, longest still 1.4 s against 2.6 s.
  - Short works only if it never stops: the 24 s two-beds room (991,555 views) had the highest motion of all 59 (15.5).
- [measured] **Hidden rooms flop when they are warm and still**: 60,853 views (warmth 110.7, longest still 3.2 s) and 56,848 (warmth 68.4, longest still 4.2 s).
- [measured] **But our own renders run too cool**: warmth 18.3 and 14.5, saturation 0.11, against the hits' 22-57 and 0.17-0.38.
  - [inference] So the den gets warm-neutral light from inside, every station glides, and warm books are on screen by 1.5 s. Aim for warmth 30-40.
- [inference] **The payoff is storage, not a secret.** The top 5 have 6-12 storage reveals. Here the hidden den's job is to swallow the floor mess, which turns the reveal into the hits' own language and earns the January slot.
- **Sends, rewatches and comments.**
  - [fact] Awe drives sharing (Berger & Milkman, 2012).
  - [fact] YouTube counts every replay as a view (since 31 Mar 2025).
  - [snapshot, n=1] One AI concept video of four chairs tucking into cabinetry drew 288 comments on 5,717 likes (5.0%), the highest comment rate among genre rows with 1,000+ likes. Weak evidence.
  - [inference, trends file] Originals with real dimensions and believable mechanics stand out from AI-looking template builds. Hence the pinned plan and the visible latch, runners and track.
  - [inference] The tidy-up is too fast to follow in one view (which toy went where?), and the loop restarts on the empty room.

### The room

- **Size and shape**: 9.2 m², 3.4 m (x, east) × 2.7 m (y, north), 2.7 m ceiling. The frame-1 camera looks west down the 3.4 m axis from high over the east door.
  - Loft, book wall and den are on the west wall and the door is on the east wall, so the room doesn't share #9 Count the Beds' layout (door in the west wall, sleeping zone along the east wall). An earlier draft had it the other way round; every x below is the mirrored value (x → 3.4 − x).
  - **West wall (short): loft and book wall.**
    - A full-length birch loft deck, x 0-1.20, y 0-2.70, top at 1.50 m, underside 1.45 m.
    - On the deck: a 0.9 × 1.9 m mattress (y 0.05-1.95) and a 0.75 m landing at the north end.
    - A muted coral guard rail runs along the deck's east edge from y 0 to 2.30. The 0.40 m gap at y 2.30-2.70 is the way up.
    - Under the deck is a book wall, 2.10 m wide, 1.45 m high and 0.25 m deep, front face at x 1.20:
      - a fixed south run, 0.70 m (y 0-0.70), with the space behind it (x 0.65-0.90) kept clear as the sliding section's parking pocket;
      - a 0.85 m sliding section (y 0.70-1.55) on floor runners and a top track. It pushes 0.3 m back (to x 0.65-0.90), then slides 0.7 m south behind the fixed south run, like a pocket door, leaving a 0.70 m opening (y 0.85-1.55);
      - a fixed north run, 0.55 m (y 1.55-2.10).
      - 0.70 + 0.85 + 0.55 = 2.10 m.
    - **The den** is behind the book wall: about 0.95 × 2.6 m inside (x 0-0.95) and 1.45 m high. It holds:
      - a 1.8 m bench toy chest along the west wall, 0.40 m high, with two lift-up lids;
      - six shallow cubbies above the bench;
      - a 0.10 m floor pad (x 0.40-0.95, y 0.90-2.00), north of the parked section;
      - a pouf at the north end;
      - a soft light panel across its ceiling.
  - **North wall (long): the book stair and the wardrobe.**
    - The stair is 0.60 m wide (y 2.10-2.70), with six 0.25 m rises and five 0.24 m treads, rising west from x 2.40 to the landing.
    - Each of its five risers is an open cubby of spine-out books: the books are the stairs. No drawers.
    - A birch handrail on posts runs up the open (south) side and meets the loft rail's end post.
    - Wardrobe tower: 0.50 × 0.60 × 2.2 m in the north-east corner (x 2.90-3.40). It has a coral door and a 0.10 m toe-kick step drawer in its plinth that pulls 0.35 m.
    - That leaves a 0.50 m clear landing in front of the bottom step (x 2.40-2.90).
  - **South wall (long)**:
    - a 0.9 m window (x 1.45-2.35);
    - a 1.0 × 0.55 m desk under it (x 1.40-2.40), top 0.62 m;
    - a drawer, a chair tucked into the east half, and a three-tier rolling cart under the west end.
  - **East wall (short)**:
    - the door (0.8 m, y 0.90-1.70), opening outward;
    - hooks and a backpack at y 0.2-0.8, with a print of three abstract shapes above them;
    - a plant in the south-east corner.
  - **Free floor**: about 1.7 × 1.5 m (x 1.20-2.90, y 0.55-2.10), with a plain rug at x 2.05-2.85. The section never enters the room: it moves back into the den and slides behind the fixed south run.
- **Palette**:
  - Shell: neutral white, about sRGB 242/241/238. [inference] Not the bedroom project's warm white (238/233/225), which leans toward the cream zone.
