  - Wood: pale birch for every built-in, light oak floor.
  - One accent: muted coral on the loft rail, the wardrobe door and the clue book. This keeps clear of #1 Tape Line's sage and muted blue and #4 Advent Stair's forest green.
  - Colour comes from filled storage:
    - the book wall in a warm-leaning muted rainbow (terracotta, mustard, rust, sage, slate), filling at least a quarter of the frame in S1;
    - the clue book sits in a short run of slate and grey-blue spines on the sliding section's top shelf, so it pops;
    - books in the stair cubbies, small clothes in the wardrobe, toys on the floor and then in the den.
  - Avoid: cream cast, walnut, saturated walls, busy patterns.
  - Targets [measured, the hits' ranges]: saturation 0.17-0.38, warmth 22-57 (aim 30-40), brightness 138-178.
- **Light**:
  - **Day**:
    - The sun, at about 5000 K, comes through the south window onto the floor and the stair's side face.
    - A soft sky fill and a doorway fill light the book wall evenly.
  - **Den**: a soft area-light panel inside, across the den ceiling (3500-4000 K), snaps on mid-slide, so light spills out of the gap. A hidden fill light sits behind the camera for the inside-den station.
  - **End**:
    - The window turns to a soft blue-hour sky.
    - The room lights cascade on: 4 downlights (3500-4000 K), then the loft reading lamp and the desk lamp (3000 K).
    - Exposure stays fixed, so the frame never dims.

### Frame 1 and the hook

- **0.0 s**:
  - The empty white room, seen high from just inside the east wall, above the door, looking west down the room at the bare west wall.
  - Starting pose: about (3.30, 1.30, 2.30), yaw 270° (looking west), pitch about -25°. Probe it with ROOM_REST: the book wall should fill the middle of the frame.
  - It is a high, straight-on view, unlike #9 Count the Beds' low push-in through a doorway (posted 7 days earlier) and #7 Not One Handle's high diagonal from the south-east corner (posted 3 days later).
  - A bright sun patch lies on the grey subfloor, and the camera is already pushing in.
- **By 0.3 s**: the first light-oak planks slap down far to near (ticks), and the den's side panels shoot up.
- **By 1.5 s**:
  - the deck slams on (0.8 s);
  - the floor runners and top track land with a clank (0.95 s);
  - the shelves slide in (1.0 s);
  - about 200 plain-spined books and a few baskets riffle in, one shelf section at a time (1.05-1.4 s);
  - the coral book lands last (1.45 s) with its own "tock": 5 cm proud and slightly tilted. Check in the probe that it sits near frame centre.
- **The no-text question**: why does that one book stick out?

### 15 s cut

- **0.0-3.0 s · S1 doorway to book wall**
  - Lands: the planks, panels, deck, runners and track, shelves, books, the clue book (1.45 s); the coral rail slides on (2.0 s); the mattress drops (2.4 s); duvet and pillow (2.7 s).
  - Camera: a steady push-in west, easing down. Pose (3.30, 1.30, 2.30, yaw 270°) → (2.75, 1.30, 2.10, yaw 270°).
- **3.3-7.0 s · S2 book stair, wardrobe, mess**
  - Stair:
    - the six step boxes stack up (3.4-3.8 s, with rising ticks);
    - books riffle into the five riser cubbies, bottom to top (3.9-4.4 s);
    - the handrail lands with a clank (4.55 s).
  - Wardrobe: the tower lands (4.8 s); the coral door pops open (5.1 s); small clothes slide onto the rail and folded stacks drop (5.2-5.8 s); the door slams (6.1 s).
  - Mess: a toy basket lands by the stair foot and tips, and toys bounce across the floor at x 2.4-2.8, y 1.45-2.0 (6.35-6.9 s).
  - Camera: a slow orbit at 1.0 m, pitch -12°, from (2.70, 0.95), yaw 330° (the stair) to (2.30, 1.00), yaw 15° (the wardrobe and the toys).
- **7.3-10.7 s · S3 the pull** (**wow** at 8.4-10.45 s)
  - The child pops in at (1.90, 1.78) (7.3 s), steps to the bookcase (7.35-7.9 s) and reaches up (7.9 s).
  - The coral book tilts with a click (8.05 s), and the section unlatches.
  - The child pushes the loaded section 0.3 m back on its floor runners (8.15-8.6 s), then pushes it sideways, and it rolls 0.7 m south behind the fixed south run like a pocket door (8.6-9.9 s). The den panel snaps on mid-slide (9.1 s).
  - The bench lid lifts (9.95 s); the floor toys fly in arcs through the gap into the chest and cubbies (10.0-10.45 s); the lid drops (10.55 s).
  - Camera: a low, medium-wide push-in from the doorway side, about 1.7 m from the child. Pose (3.25, 1.52, 1.00, yaw 256°) → (3.10, 1.49, 0.95, yaw 256°), pitch -3°.
- **11.0-15.0 s · S4 desk, lights, wide**
  - The desk lands (11.0 s); its drawer pops open, takes crayons and a blank ruler stick, and shuts (11.15-11.6 s); the chair tucks in (11.65 s).
  - The rug unrolls (11.75-11.95 s); the roller blind rolls up (12.0 s).
  - Lights on at 12.2 s.
  - The child walks into the den and sits on the pad (12.4-13.3 s), then waves (13.6-14.2 s).
  - Camera: from high in the north-east, (3.05, 1.75, 1.50, yaw 240°, pitch -12°), a slow crane up and pull-back into the exact frame-1 pose by 15.0 s, so the video loops. The path stays inside the room. The child is visible through the gap.

### Long cut (40-50 s)

45.5 s (1,138 frames), inside hh_metrics' 34-46 s range.

- **Structure**:
  - seven zones, one every 3.1-7.5 s;
  - 13 stations plus a 2.0 s final wide, so a new angle every 2.0-3.3 s;
  - 0.3 s swings between stations, and the camera glides inside every station;
  - the click at 27.6 s (61%), the wow at 28.0-30.6 s (62-67%).
- **0.0-6.8 s · Z1 loft and book wall**
  - A1 (0.0-3.3 s): the S1 push-in, ending with the rail and mattress.
  - A2 (3.6-6.8 s): high over the rail at 1.95 m, sliding south from (1.55, 2.30) to (1.55, 0.90), looking down.
    - The duvet, pillows and a generic plush land, then a clip-on reading lamp on the rail.
    - The 0.40 m way up reads clearly.
- **7.1-13.9 s · Z2 north wall**
  - B1 (7.1-10.3 s): low (0.7 m) from the south-east, sliding west along the stair. The steps stack, books fill the riser cubbies (storage 2), and the handrail clanks on.
  - B2 (10.6-13.9 s): orbiting right round the wardrobe from mid-room, (2.15, 1.15) → (2.55, 1.05).
    - The tower lands. The toe-kick drawer slides out, shoes drop in, and it slides back (storage 4).
    - The door pops open; clothes, stacks and two baskets go in; the door slams (storage 3).
- **14.2-20.5 s · Z3 window desk**
  - W1 (14.2-17.2 s): pushing in south toward the window, (1.90, 1.75) → (1.90, 1.25).
    - The desk lands. Its drawer opens, fills and shuts (storage 5).
    - A crayon jar and a plant land.
  - W2 (17.5-20.5 s): low from the east.
    - The chair lands and tucks in, then the lamp.
    - The rolling cart slides out, fills and slides back (storage 6).
    - The roller blind rolls up.
- **20.8-27.2 s · Z4 finish and mess**
  - F1 (20.8-24.0 s): a mid-room orbit toward the east wall. The rug unrolls; the hooks, backpack, print and corner plant land.
  - M (24.3-27.2 s): a high wide from the south-east corner, rising from (3.10, 0.30, 1.85) to (3.00, 0.40, 1.95), yaw 322-318°.
    - The toy basket lands and tips, and toys bounce across the floor (24.6-25.4 s).
    - The child walks in from the doorway (25.6-27.2 s) and reaches up (27.2 s).
- **27.5-34.3 s · Z5 the pull and the tidy-up**
  - D1 (27.5-30.8 s): the S3 camera path.
    - The click (27.6 s); the section unlatches, and the child pushes it 0.3 m back (27.7-28.1 s).
    - It rolls 0.7 m south behind the fixed south run (28.1-29.5 s), and the den panel snaps on (28.7 s) (storage 7).
    - The bench lid lifts (29.6 s); the floor toys fly through the gap into the chest and cubbies (29.8-30.6 s).
  - D2 (31.1-34.3 s): pushing in toward the gap, (2.10, 1.60, 0.95) → (1.55, 1.40, 0.90).
    - The lid drops (31.2 s) (storage 8).
    - The child walks through the gap and sits on the pad (31.5-32.5 s).
    - Cushions drop round the child (32.8-33.2 s), and the child points at the cubbies (33.4 s).
- **34.6-37.7 s · Z6 inside the den**
  - E (34.6-37.7 s): inside, low, (0.35, 0.45, 0.80) → (0.40, 0.70, 0.85), yaw 20° → 30°. The parked section now sits in the den's south end, so re-place E if the probe shows it in the way (see Must-checks).
    - The tossed toys hop into neat order in the cubbies (plush, balls, picture books spine-out), each on a rising pluck (34.9-36.4 s) (storage 9).
    - The child hugs a plush and leans back on the cushions (36.6-37.3 s).
- **38.0-45.5 s · Z7 lights and finish**
  - L (38.0-40.9 s): a pull-back out of the den into a high south-east view, (2.00, 1.55, 1.30) → (2.85, 0.75, 1.80). The sky turns blue-hour, and the lights cascade on from 38.6 s.
  - F3 (41.2-43.2 s): a low orbit left toward the den. The child leans into the gap and waves (41.6-42.4 s).
  - **43.5-45.5 s · final wide**: a 2 s drift from about (2.95, 1.30, 2.05) into the frame-1 pose. No end card.

### Storage moments

1. **Book wall** (build): the shelves fill around the clue book, 1.05-1.45 s.
2. **Book stair** (build): books riffle into the five riser cubbies, bottom to top.
3. **Wardrobe tower** (reveal): the coral door pops open; small garments, folded stacks and two baskets go in; the door slams.
4. **Toe-kick step drawer** (reveal, long cut): it slides 0.35 m out of the wardrobe plinth, shoes drop in, and it slides back.
5. **Desk drawer** (reveal): it opens, takes pencils, crayons and a blank ruler stick, and shuts.
6. **Rolling cart** (reveal, long cut): it slides out from under the desk, art supplies drop into its three tiers, and it slides back.
7. **The bookcase section** (reveal): click, pushed 0.3 m back, then slid 0.7 m aside by hand, like a pocket door, onto the lit den.
8. **Bench toy chest** (reveal): the lid lifts, the floor's blocks fly in, and the lid drops. This is part of the wow.
9. **Den cubbies** (build): balls, plush and picture books fly in. In the long cut they then hop into order.

The 15 s cut uses moments 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 8 and 9.

### The person moment

- **The figure**: the child stand-in from `pipeline/figure.py` ('child', about 1.15 m, faceless), in the `fig_blue` material so it reads against the birch, the coral and the warm books.
- **Long cut**:
  - walks in from the doorway (`fg.walk`, 25.6-27.2 s) to (1.45, 1.57), facing west;
  - reaches up with `lerp_pose('stand', 'reach_up', 0.75)`: [computed from figure.py] the fingertip is at about 1.21 m, 0.27 m ahead of the pelvis;
    - the clue book stands on the section's top shelf at 1.00-1.22 m, near its north edge (y about 1.43);
  - tilts the book (27.6 s), then steps up to the wall line and pushes the section 0.3 m back with a forward reach (`sh_r` about 100°: the hand is about 0.39 m ahead, at about 1.03 m [computed]) (27.7-28.1 s);
  - gives it a sideways push from its north edge, and it rolls 0.7 m south into the pocket (28.1-29.5 s), with the hand keyframed to the edge every 0.25 s until the child lets go. Check the reach in a probe still;
  - watches the toys fly;
  - walks through the gap and sits on the pad at (0.65, 1.30) (`sit_pose(0.10)`, 31.5-32.5 s);
  - points at the cubbies, hugs a plush, and waves from the gap (41.6-42.4 s).
- **15 s cut**: pops in at 7.3 s, does the same push and slide (8.05-9.9 s), walks in and sits (12.4-13.3 s), and waves (13.6-14.2 s).
- **What it shows**: the cleverest feature, one book that opens a tidy-up den, is used by the person it was made for. The section opens slowly, by hand, with no spring.

### Sound

- **Landings**:
  - 'thud' for the deck, mattress, stair boxes, wardrobe, desk and bench lid;
  - 'tick' for planks, books (a fast riffle during each sweep), toys and crayons;
  - 'swish' for every drawer and door;
  - 'chime' for the lights coming on;
  - a short, bright 'thud' for the runners, track and handrail clank.
- **Two playful effects**:
  1. The tug: a crisp latch click (a high 'tick'), then a slow wooden creak with a soft runner roll as the section slides (8.05-9.9 s in the 15 s cut, 27.6-29.5 s in the long cut).
  2. The tidy-up: each toy flies in on a rising plucked note, so the mess becomes a quick upward run that ends on the lid's thud (9.95-10.55 s and 29.6-31.2 s). The long cut's cubby sort reuses the run, softer.
  - [measured] Commenters notice playful sound: "the Scooby-Doo sound effects" drew 214 likes.
- **Music**: an original cue at 120 BPM.
  - [measured, weak] Hits had a median of 121 BPM against 100 for flops.
  - Drop the drums for one beat before the tug, so the click lands in a hush.
  - [measured] No need to cut on the beat: 23% of the hits' scene changes land near a strong sound, against 24% by chance.

### Risks and fixes

- **The den goes dark, orange or grainy**, like the two hidden-room flops (warmth 110.7 and 68.4).
  - Fix: a soft area-light panel inside the den (3500-4000 K), a hidden fill behind the inside-den camera, no warm bulbs in the den, and more samples or denoising on those stations.
  - Check stills at 34.0 s (den open, child sitting) and 36.0 s (inside): brightness 138-178, warmth 57 or under, no visible noise.
- **The whole video comes out too cool and grey**, which is this pipeline's usual failure (warmth 18.3 and 14.5, saturation 0.11).
  - Fix: sun at about 5000 K, interior LEDs at 3500-4000 K, lamps at 3000 K; birch and light oak; warm books filling at least a quarter of S1.
  - Measure stills at 2 s, 9 s and 14 s (blue hour) of the 15 s cut before the full render. Aim for warmth 30-40.
- **It repeats the slate.** #4 Advent Stair is a kids' loft with stair drawers and a book-wall den. #1 Tape Line has a loft, stair drawers and a loaded wardrobe that swings on a post. #9 Count the Beds is a 3.4 m-wide kids' room with a west door and a raised sleeping zone on the east.
  - Fix: no stair drawers (the risers are book cubbies), no headboard ledge or torch, a coral accent, a storage payoff instead of a secret, a section that slides aside like a pocket door instead of swinging, and a plan mirrored against #9 with its own frame 1.
  - Check the block-out side by side with the #1, #4 and #9 plans before modelling.
- **Room proportions** (I changed the review's example):
  - The review suggested 2.7 × 3.4 m with the loft on the short wall, which would copy #4 Advent Stair's shape (2.6 × 3.1 m, loft on its short north wall).
  - I kept the short-wall loft but turned the room: the camera looks down the 3.4 m axis at the book wall, and the stair runs along a long wall.
- **Title words** [measured]: titles with "secret", "hidden" or "magic" run at 0.82 (11 Shorts). A Sep 2026 kids' room titled "…It even has a secret space!" got 17K views (era-adjusted 0.459; date estimated).
  - Fix: keep those words out of the caption and hashtags, and lead with "9 m²" and "Pull one book and the floor is clear".
- **A "fake or dangerous" pile-on.**
  - Fix:
    - No spring. The book releases a latch, and the child pushes the section back and slides it aside by hand over about 1.8 s.
    - 2 cm shelf lips, a finger-guard strip on the section's leading edge, and floor runners and a top track you can see, which land early with a clank.
    - Keep the loft rail, the 0.40 m way up and the stair handrail.
  - The section never moves into the room: it goes 0.3 m back into the den, then slides behind the fixed south run. Nothing sweeps the floor, the desk or the child's path.
  - Pin the top-view plan with the slide path, then let the debate run.
- **Step drawer placement** (I changed the review's fix): the review offered a pull-out step drawer at the stair foot, but #4 Advent Stair's long cut already has a pull-out bottom step. So the Xiaohongshu toe-kick step drawer goes in the wardrobe plinth, and the stair foot keeps a plain 0.50 m landing.
- **No close-and-reopen from inside, in either cut** (I changed the original):
  - The pinned plan says it opens from inside (a back-face pull, a magnetic catch, no lock).
- **Cliché, or a single feature in disguise** [measured, dates estimated]: kids' rooms ran at 0.71 in 2026 H2 (9 Shorts).
  - Fix: the den is one of seven zones and nine storage moments; the reveal comes at 60% and pays off in storage.
  - Post 6-15 Jan, at least 5 weeks after #4 Advent Stair. [fact, secondary, indicative] "Declutter" searches spike in early January.
- **Readable marks on props.**
  - Fix:
    - The ruler is a blank stick, and every book is spine-out with a plain cover.
    - The print is three abstract shapes.
    - Blocks carry no letters or numbers; the plush is generic; there are no logos or labels.
  - Check a still at 4× zoom for anything that looks like a letter.

### Build notes

- **Set-up**: one project file (for example `rooms/book_den/project.py`) with a CUT switch for 15 or 45.5.
  - Shared `materials()`, `build()` and `lights()`; two HOLDS lists and two timelines.
  - Preview and render the 15 s cut first.
- **Reuse from `rooms/bedroom/project.py`**:
  - the birch and oak materials, plus the linen, mattress and pillow materials. Swap the warm-white wall for neutral white;
  - `garment()`, `stack()`, `plant()` and `pillow()`;
  - the books loop and the `from_camera` sweep;
  - `drawer_fn()` (with `base=`) for the desk drawer and the toe-kick drawer; `door_z()` for the wardrobe pop and slam; the bed `lift()` hinge for the bench lid.
    - These three are closures nested inside that project's `timeline()`, so copy them into the new file; they cannot be imported;
  - the light slots (`rect_light`, `spot_rect`, `sphere_light`, the sun spot), the `bd_day`/`bd_night` backdrop swap and the BLENDER dict.
- **Reuse from `rooms/kitchen/project.py`**: the pull-out pantry pattern (the carcass stays and the contents fly in once it is out), for the cart and the toe-kick drawer.
- **Shared helpers**: `camkit.hold_camera` for the 14 long-cut holds (13 stations plus the final wide) and the 4 short-cut holds; `figure.py` for the child.
- **New pieces**:
  - the deck and coral rail;
  - the book stair, with its five riser cubbies and handrail;
  - the bookcase:
    - the fixed runs, with the parking pocket behind the south run;
    - the 0.85 m section on floor runners and a top track, on an L-shaped path (0.3 m back, then 0.7 m south);
    - the finger guard, shelf lips and latch;
  - the books grouped by shelf section and by cubby, with the clue book as its own piece;
  - about 30 plain toys, animated in about 6 flight groups;
  - the den: pad, cushions, the bench chest and its two lids, six cubbies and the ceiling light panel;
  - the wardrobe tower and its toe-kick drawer;
  - the desk, drawer, cart, chair and lamp;
  - the south window, roller blind, rug, hooks, backpack, print and plants;
  - two new sound kinds in `music.py`: 'creak' and a rising pluck run (built from the existing `pluck()` and `whoosh()`).
- **Must-checks**:
  - The section's books and the clue book use the section's transform (section(t) @ their own landing). Otherwise they float when it slides.
  - In top view, the section moves only inside the wall line and the den (x 0.65-1.20, y 0-1.55): it never enters the room, so the desk, the rug and the toys (which land at x 2.4-2.8, y 1.45-2.0) are untouched.
  - Parked, it sits at x 0.65-0.90, y 0-0.85, clear of the den pad (from y 0.90) and the bench.
  - The clear opening is 0.70 m (y 0.85-1.55). That is enough for the child, but not for a camera to follow the child through.
  - Cameras:
    - S3 and D1 stay about 2 m from the book wall while the section moves;
    - the swings into and out of the den (34.3-34.6 s and 37.7-38.0 s) pass through the 0.70 m opening, at least 0.1 m from either edge, and must clear the parked section; E as written starts beside it, so move E north if the probe shows a clash;
    - probe with ROOM_REST, plus frames at those two swing midpoints.
  - Inside the den, keep the camera below 1.35 m so it never clips the deck.
  - No lettering anywhere.
  - Give it its own camera path, music track and accent, new against `rooms/bedroom`, `rooms/kitchen`, #1 Tape Line, #4 Advent Stair and #9 Count the Beds.
- **Effort: medium.**
  - 1,138 frames, and the long cut takes about 3 times the 15 s cut's render time (report section 10).
  - On top of that come the figure's walk, reach, edge-tracking push and sit, the inside-den shot, about 260 books and the toy flights.
  - If the render budget runs short, cut E and stretch D2.
- **Hard to render, and how to avoid it**:
  - **Fabric**: no cloth draping; use `pillow()` and rounded boxes.
  - **Hands**: no close-ups. The book tilts as the hand arrives, and the tug is shot medium-wide from about 1.7 m.
  - **Den noise**: use the panel light and the fill, plus more samples or denoising on D2 and E.
  - **Piece count**: group the books by section and cubby, and the toys by flight group.

## 7. Not One Handle in Sight

- **At a glance**:
  - Candidate C11. Judges' mean 5.8; reviewer score 6, given before this revision.
  - Plan ready, not started.
  - Posting: the long cut first, on Sat 9 Jan; the 15 s cut on Thu 14 Jan.
- **Why it ranks here**:
  - A whole 9.6 m² bedroom with a walk-in, the beat behind a 2.05M Short (29.41× normal) [measured].
  - It is not a loft, and "someone who hates clutter" is a weaker premise than a family [inference]. So it is the January test of the flush-wall idea.

### Pitch

A calm 3.0 × 3.2 m bedroom of flush white walls with no handles anywhere opens ten things, one after another. One of them is a walk-in closet behind a wall panel: a person walks in wearing blue and comes back out through the mirror wearing teal. Then everything shuts in one rolling wave, seen from straight above.

### Post caption (in the post, never on screen)

- Long cut: "For someone who can't stand visible clutter: a 9.6 m² bedroom (3.0 × 3.2 m, about 10 × 10.5 ft). No wardrobe against the wall, a walk-in behind it, and not one handle in sight. Count the things that open. Push-latch everything: genius or a fingerprint nightmare? Save this for your reno, or send it to the tidiest person you know."
- 15 s cut: the same, minus "Count the things that open". The 15 s cut doesn't show every opening, so it asks only the push-latch question.
- Keep "secret", "hidden" and "magic" out of the caption.
- Pin a comment with:
  - the plan with dimensions;
  - "Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app.";
  - the trade-offs: "About 1.6 m of rail on two levels, plus shoe and fold shelves. That's less than a 1.2 m double-hung wardrobe (2.4 m), but the walkway doubles as a dressing room and a route out to the door." "Door A and the bed drawers both open into the 0.8 m aisle, one at a time." "Phone across the aisle: no scrolling in bed."
- After a day, reply with the answer to the count: 10 things that open (bedside panel, 2 toe-kick drawers, 2 bed drawers, bench lid, desk, rack, door A, mirror).

### Why it should work

- [measured] **A whole bedroom with a size in the title.**
  - Bedrooms run at 1.51 times the channel's normal (47 Shorts), and titles naming a size at 1.34.
  - 28 of 29 hits build a whole room, against 12 of 30 flops.
  - This builds all of a 9.6 m² room, not one wardrobe.
- [measured] **A walk-in closet in a small bedroom has already won.**
  - "Be Bold — Even the Smallest Bedroom Can Fit a Walk-In Closet!" got 2,052,773 views, 29.41 times normal: 34 s, camera movement 4.23, saturation 0.20, warmth 28.9.
  - The 5 titles that say "walk-in" have a median of 1.565 (a small sample).
  - The 12.8M loft (12,761,012 views, 323 comments) opens a door in the wall onto its closet at 9-11 s. Commenters describe a mum vanishing into the wall.
  - Our figure stages that beat our own way: in through a panel, out through the mirror.
- [measured] **Many reveals, a person, and something to argue about.**
  - The top 5 carry 6-12 storage reveals, and a person uses a feature in 4 of the 5. This plan has 10 things that open, plus a figure.
  - One AI concept of four chairs tucking into cabinetry drew 288 comments on 5,717 likes (5.0%). That is a single one-piece video, and a 31-like clip in the same sample shows 9.7% (3 comments), so treat it as weak.
  - A commenter under the hits wrote "the more cabinets, the smaller the room" (Vietnamese, 10 likes). Flush, handle-free walls answer that and start the push-latch argument.
- [measured] **It never stops moving.**
  - Hits change 8.4 per 0.2 s against 5.1 for flops, and their longest still is 1.4 s against 2.6 s. The hit's camera moved more in 21 of 25 pairs.
  - Here every station glides, and the ending is an overhead open-and-shut wave followed by a 2 s descent, not a still.
  - The short Shorts that did well never stopped moving. The 24 s two-beds room (991,555 views) had camera movement 11.88, the highest of any hit. The 15 s cut follows the same rule.
- [fact + inference] **Timing and loop.**
  - Searches for "declutter" spike in early January (Google Trends, via secondary sources).
  - Post 6-15 Jan 2027, at least 7 days from the wardrobe-reset video (trends #7, window 27 Dec-15 Jan) and from next_videos #2 (the walk-in-plus-loft idea).
  - Since 31 Mar 2025, YouTube counts every replay as a view [fact]. The overhead wave and the drawer that pops back open give people a reason to rewatch [inference].

### The room

- **Size and shape**: a 9.6 m² rectangle, 3.0 m east-west × 3.2 m north-south, with a 2.7 m ceiling.
  - It is not a 3 × 3 m square, so it doesn't share #5 Bed on the Ceiling's shell. The extra 0.2 m goes to the entry corner, now 1.0 × 1.2 m: the walk-in block sits at y 1.2-3.2, and the bed's headboard becomes a 0.2 m-deep shelf at the north wall, so the bed, bench and desk keep their places. The y coordinates below include the shift.
  - There are no handles anywhere: the room door is flush with an edge pull and the window is a fixed pane, so the caption stays literally true.
- **West: the walk-in block.**
  - 1.0 × 2.0 m (2.0 m², about 0.94 × 1.94 m inside), standing on a 12 cm platform with a light-oak edge.
  - It is faced with flush white push-latch panels, with visible 20 mm shadow gaps.
  - **The aisle face** (along the 0.8 m aisle), from north to south:
    - a bedside panel (0.65 m) with a 0.5 m flip-down ledge over a lit recess 12 cm deep;
    - door A (0.7 × 2.4 m, the widest panel), hinged on its north edge and swinging out into the aisle. The ledge is narrower than its panel, so door A clears it at 90°;
    - one fixed panel.
    - Two toe-kick drawers with light-oak fronts sit in the platform's front edge.
  - **The south face** (toward the entry corner):
    - a 0.28 m fixed panel;
    - a 0.7 m full-length mirror panel on a pivot set 0.1 m in from its east edge. The long leaf swings into the closet and the short leaf swings only 0.1 m into the entry corner, clear of the room door's 0.8 m swing. That leaves about 0.55 m clear to walk through. It is the closet's second door, and its back holds shelves no deeper than 8 cm.
  - **Inside**:
    - double rails across the north end, 0.55 m deep and about 0.82 m long each (they stop short of the bedside recess), so about 1.6 m of hanging;
    - 0.3 m birch shelves on the west wall from the mirror end to y 2.6, stopping short of the rails: shoes low, folded clothes in the middle, bins on top;
    - a 0.64 m walkway;
    - an LED strip;
    - a closet ceiling that can be hidden from the camera only.
- **North**: the head of a 1.2 × 2.0 m bed on a 0.45 m drawer platform with a light-oak edge, standing against the east wall.
  - Two push-latch drawers pull out of the platform's aisle side.
  - A white headboard shelf 0.2 m deep, with one light-oak ledge above it holding books, a plant and an abstract print.
- **East**: a 1.2 m window over the long side of the bed, for morning sun.
- **South**:
  - The 0.8 m room door, in the 1.0 × 1.2 m entry corner. The wall above it stays plain.
  - A flush panel that folds down into a 1.2 × 0.45 m desk with a lit recess.
  - Above the desk, an upper cabinet whose pull-down rack swings out and down to about 1.3 m.
  - The light-oak bench at the foot of the bed (1.2 × 0.4 × 0.45 m) is the desk seat. Its top is flush with the bed platform, and its lid is hinged on the desk side so it clears the mattress.
  - A tall plant stands at the aisle's south end, beside the desk.
- **Palette**:
  - **Shell**: neutral white panels (not warm white), pale-grey plaster and a white ceiling. Before the planks land, the bare slab is mid grey so they contrast.
  - **Wood**: light oak on the floor, the bed platform, the bench, the closet platform's edge and the toe-kick fronts; birch inside the closet. No whitewashed-oak fallback, because our renders have only ever run cool and grey.
  - **Accent**: olive bedding, the one accent. The aisle runner is plain jute.
  - **Where the colour comes from**:
    - books on the ledges and in the desk recess;
    - clothes in teal, plum, mustard, sky blue, charcoal and white, none of them from the 11 m² bedroom's set;
    - shoes;
    - three vacuum bags (teal, plum, grey), a sky-blue suitcase, one blue storage box and plants.
  - **Targets**: saturation 0.17-0.38, warmth 22-57, brightness 138-178.
  - No walnut and no cream.
- **Light**:
  - Morning daylight comes through the east window, with bright fill from the sky. The sun throws a patch across the aisle onto the panels.
  - Keep the sun patch and the shadow gaps in frame: they give the motion measure edges to track.
  - Set the exposure for brightness 138-178.
  - **Lights on** (12.2 s in the 15 s cut, 41.2 s in the long cut):
    - An LED strip in the gap along the panel tops lights up around the room, in the same direction as the ripple.
    - The ledge and desk recesses are already lit and stay open.
    - Use 3000-3500 K LEDs. The daylight stays, so the room gets brighter, never dusky.

### Frame 1 and the hook

- **0.0 s**: the empty shell from the high south-east corner, about 2.1 m up, looking north-west and down. Two blank pale walls, a mid-grey slab and a patch of morning sun. The camera is already pushing in.
- **By 0.3 s**:
  - light-oak floor planks slap down from far to near (the first tick at about 0.2 s);
  - the closet's platform, with its oak edge, slides out of the west wall;
  - its first flush panel shoots up from the floor with a thock.
- **0.3-1.4 s**: panels land every 0.15 s, some in view and some off it. These include the desk panel and the mirror.
- **1.4-1.9 s**: door A, just landed, springs open on a packed rail of colourful clothes, then slams shut. That says "these walls are doors" without a single word.

### 15 s cut

- Every item in the long cut's finished room also lands here. The tall plant lands off-screen during S3, and the rest sits inside closed storage.
- **0.0-3.0 s · S1 frame-1 corner**
  - What lands:
    - floor planks, the platform and the panel domino;
    - the teaser door (1.4-1.9);
    - the bed platform (1.45), the mattress (1.65), the olive duvet (1.8), pillows (1.9), the bench (2.05) and the jute runner (2.2);
    - then the oak ledge with books and a plant (2.4-2.9).
  - Colour flashes on screen at 1.4 s and stays from 1.8 s.
  - Camera: a push-in toward the panel wall at about 0.3 m/s, drifting slightly left.
- **3.3-7.0 s · S2 aisle and bed**
  - What lands:
    - the bedside panel flips down into a ledge (3.5), and a lamp, books and a phone land in its lit recess (3.8-4.4);
    - the two bed drawers slide out into the aisle (4.7, 4.85), and a suitcase, a rolled duvet and three vacuum bags drop in (5.1-5.9);
    - the drawers shut with thuds (6.3, 6.45).
  - Camera: about 2.0 m up at the aisle's south end. It slides north while turning from north-north-west (the ledge) to north-east and down (the drawers).
- **7.3-11.0 s · S3 walk-in** (the wow at 8.0-10.6 s)
  - **7.3-9.4 · the door pass**
    - Door A swings open (7.4-7.7) and the closet light snaps on.
    - The camera glides through the doorway (8.0-8.4).
    - The bottom rail fills one garment at a time (8.5-9.3).
    - Camera: pushes in west along y = 2.2 at 1.6 m, passes through the centre of the doorway, then turns right (north) to the rails.
  - **Hard cut at 9.4.**
  - **9.4-11.0 · the dollhouse view**
    - The closet ceiling and the whole aisle face are hidden from the camera only.
    - The camera rises and backs east over the aisle. It shows the whole lit 2 m² room behind the "flat" wall.
    - Boxes slide onto the top shelf (9.6-10.1) and shoes drop onto the low shelf (10.1-10.6).
    - The figure, already inside, reaches up for a jacket. She is hidden during the door pass, so the camera never passes close to her.
    - Door A shuts unseen before 11.0.
  - **Hard cut at 11.0.**
- **11.0-15.0 s · S4 overhead and descent**
  - **Open wave (11.0-11.3)**: the toe-kick drawers, bed drawers, bench lid and door A spring open, and the desk folds down.
  - **Ripple (11.4-12.2)**: those 6 shut in one wave around the room. The ledge and the desk stay open and lit, so the loop's resting stretch keeps its colour.
  - **Lights on (12.2)**: the LED strip runs round the room after the wave.
  - **12.5**: one toe-kick drawer pops back open.
  - **13.2**: a throw drops onto the bed.
  - **14.0**: the drawer clicks shut.
  - Camera:
    - 11.0-13.0: straight down from the ceiling centre at (1.5, 1.6, 2.6), turning about 16°. It sees the aisle, the bed, the bench and the desk.
    - 13.0-15.0: a 2 s descent that eases into the exact frame-1 pose, so the video loops.
  - From 11.3 s these frames are identical to the long cut's 40.3-44.0 s. The first 0.3 s differ only because the desk opens here and is already open in the long cut.

### Long cut (40-50 s)

44.0 s in 7 zones.

- Zones last about 6-7 s, and the finale 4 s.
- The camera angle changes every 2.0-3.8 s.
- There is no end card.
- **Zone 1 · shell and aisle (0.0-6.2 s)**
  - 0.0-3.0 · frame-1 corner, push-in: identical to S1 of the 15 s cut (render it once).
  - 3.3-6.2 · aisle, low slide north: the skirting lands. The two oak-fronted toe-kick drawers slide out of the platform, fill with slippers, socks and a charger tray, and shut (4.0-5.4).
- **Zone 2 · bed (6.5-13.2 s)**
  - 6.5-9.2 · bed head, push-in: the bedside panel flips down (6.8), and a lamp, books and a phone land in the lit recess (7.1-8.1). The ledge stays open to the end.
  - 9.5-13.2 · high over the bed foot looking up the aisle, orbiting toward the window:
    - the bed drawers slide out (9.9, 10.1);
    - a suitcase, a rolled duvet and three vacuum bags drop in (10.5-11.9);
    - the drawers shut (12.4, 12.6).
- **Zone 3 · desk corner (13.5-19.6 s)**
  - 13.5-16.2 · bed foot, sliding east while looking south: the bench lid lifts (13.9), shoes and two throws drop in (14.2-15.2), and the lid shuts (15.7).
  - 16.5-19.6 · desk, push-in then low:
    - the panel folds down on its stays (16.8-17.2) and the recess lights up;
    - a laptop slides into its slot, and notebooks, a pen pot and a small plant land (17.4-18.8).
    - The desk stays open to the end.
- **Zone 4 · upper rack and approach (19.9-26.6 s)**
  - 19.9-22.6 · from the bench, looking up at the desk wall: the pull-down rack swings out and down (20.2), three boxes and a row of books land on it (20.5-21.5), and it swings back up (22.0).
  - 22.9-26.6 · aisle, push-in west on door A:
    - the tall plant lands at the aisle's south end (23.3);
    - a latch tick (25.4);
    - door A swings open through its full arc into the aisle, and the closet light comes on (25.6-26.0).
- **Zone 5 · walk-in (26.9-33.3 s); the wow at 30.2 s (69%), when the cut-away view shows the whole closet filling**
  - 26.9-30.2 · the camera glides through the centre of the doorway at walking pace and turns right to the rails: the bottom rail fills (27.8-29.0), and boxes slide onto the top shelf (29.2-29.9).
  - **Hard cut at 30.2.**
  - 30.2-33.3 · the dollhouse view, rising and backing east, with the aisle face and closet ceiling hidden from the camera only: shoes drop in pairs and folded stacks drop (30.4-31.8), then the shelves on the back of the mirror panel fill (32.0-32.8).
  - **Hard cut at 33.3.**
- **Zone 6 · the person (33.3-39.7 s)**
  - 33.3-35.6 · aisle: the figure, in denim blue, walks up the aisle, steps up through door A and pulls it shut behind her (35.0).
  - 35.9-39.7 · entry:
    - the mirror panel pivots open (36.2);
    - she steps out in a teal jacket (36.5-37.1);
    - the panel pivots shut (37.4);
    - she turns to the mirror (37.7) and checks the jacket;
    - she walks out of frame toward the room door (38.6-39.4).
- **Zone 7 · finale (40.0-44.0 s)**
  - 40.0-42.0 · overhead, turning:
    - open wave (40.0-40.3): toe-kicks, bed drawers, bench lid and door A;
    - the ripple (40.4-41.2);
    - lights on (41.2);
    - the drawer pops open (41.5).
  - 42.0-44.0 · a 2 s descent that ends on the frame-1 pose: a throw drops onto the bed (42.2), and the drawer clicks shut (43.0).
  - From 40.3 s the frames are identical to the 15 s cut's from 11.3 s.

### Storage moments

Ten moments, each a build or a reveal, growing in size, with a different kind of movement each time.

1. **The bedside panel flips down** into a ledge. Behind it, a 12 cm lit recess holds a lamp, books and a phone on a charging slot.
2. **The toe-kick drawers slide out** of the closet platform: slippers, socks and a charger tray.
3. **The bed drawers slide out** of the 0.45 m platform: a sky-blue suitcase, a rolled duvet and three vacuum bags.
4. **The bench lid lifts**: two pairs of shoes and two folded throws drop in.
5. **The desk folds down** from the south wall. Its recess lights up with a laptop slot, notebooks, a pen pot and a plant.
6. **The pull-down rack swings out and down** from the upper cabinet: three boxes and a row of books land on it, and it swings back up.
7. **Door A swings open** on a lit 1 × 2 m walk-in.
8. **The double rails fill**: the top rail is already packed (the teaser), the bottom rail fills, and boxes slide onto the top shelf.
9. **The shoe and fold shelves fill**: shoes drop in pairs, folded stacks drop, and bins slide in.
10. **The mirror panel pivots**: its back holds shelves 8 cm deep (jars and a jewellery tray), and it is the closet's second door.
- The caption's count is things that open: 10 (bedside panel, 2 toe-kick drawers, 2 bed drawers, bench lid, desk, rack, door A, mirror). The long cut shows each one opening.

### The person moment

- **Long cut**:
  - A faceless adult in denim blue walks up the aisle, steps up onto the closet platform through door A, and pulls the door shut behind her.
  - The music drops to a held pad for a second.
  - The camera swings to the entry. The mirror panel pivots, and she steps out through a clearly wide gap, now in a teal jacket.
  - The panel shuts, she turns to check herself in the mirror, then she leaves toward the room door.
  - It shows the cleverest feature: a walk-through closet that lets you dress on your way out. It also echoes the loft's "mum vanishing into the wall" without copying it.
- **15 s cut**: she is already inside, reaching up for a jacket, when the dollhouse view opens.

### Sound

- **Landing sounds**:
  - floor planks tick;
  - each panel lands with a soft thock plus a latch click;
  - the bed platform and the bench land with thuds;
  - contents tick;
  - door A swishes and lands with a soft thud.
- **Playful effect 1**: a pneumatic sigh as the rack swings down (long cut about 20.2 s; music.py's whoosh played downward). The 15 s cut doesn't show the rack.
- **Playful effect 2**: the push-latch drawer pops back open after the ripple with a "boing", then clicks shut (15 s cut 12.5 / 14.0 s; long cut 41.5 / 43.0 s). It is a hidden gag for commenters, and it plays on the caption's push-latch question.
- **The ripple**:
  - 6 thuds over about 0.8 s at a steady strength of about 0.7. In music.py that gives a low, medium-loud thud for each (about 71 Hz at gain 0.65).
  - A latch-click tick sits on each thud. Its pitch steps up around the room, so phone speakers hear the rise.
  - It ends on a soft final clack.
- **Lights on**: a chime.
- **Music**: original, at 120 BPM (music.py with bpm 120). Sound didn't separate hits from flops [measured], and the tempo lean toward 111-125 BPM is weak evidence, so don't cut on the beat.

### Risks and fixes

- **Title wording** [measured, weak: 11 titles]: titles with "secret", "hidden" or "magic" run at 0.82, against 1.00 without.
  - Keep those words out of the caption and lead with who lives there and "9.6 m²".
  - The best of those 11 was itself a size-plus-bedroom title: "6㎡ Bedroom with a Secret Space", at 4.83.
- **Caption honesty**:
  - The walls are all cupboard doors, so "not one cupboard" would draw "this is literally all cupboards". "Not one handle" and "no wardrobe against the wall" are true.
  - The report warns that "24 m² apartment" earned reach and a pile-on.
  - The "who" (someone who hates visible clutter) is weaker than a family premise. [inference, trends file; about 8 posts on 2 accounts] Family premises got roughly 20-50 times the likes of no-premise posts in the snapshots.
- **Grey or cool cast**:
  - Flush white walls fill much of the runtime. Our kitchen v2 averaged saturation 0.11 and warmth 14.5 even though a still of it looked fine.
  - Fixes:
    - colour on screen by 1.8 s;
    - the ledge and the desk stay open and lit through the ripple and the loop rest;
    - 3000-3500 K LEDs;
    - oak on the platform edge and toe-kick fronts;
    - the olive bedding;
    - clear colours in the contents.
  - Two "extra room / hidden room" flops ran warm instead (110.7 and 68.4) and held stills of 3.2 s and 4.2 s. One is titled "Magically Added an Extra Room".
  - Run hh_metrics on the 15 s preview before any full render: saturation 0.17-0.38, warmth 22-57.
- **Flat panels starve the motion measure**:
  - hh_metrics scores the camera from the quietest tiles, and blank white panels barely change under a slow glide. Kitchen v2 scored 1.06 against a 1.8 target.
  - Fixes:
    - keep the oak floor and some filled storage in every frame;
    - glide at about 0.3 m/s or 8° a second;
    - keep the shadow gaps and the sun patch visible.
- **Camera through a 0.7 m door**:
  - The risk is clipping through the frame and wide-lens stretch.
  - Run every pass along the door's centre line (y = 2.2) at about 1.6 m high, at least 0.25 m from each side of the frame.
  - Leave the closet only by hard cut, so no swing crosses a wall.
  - Check test frames at 8.0, 8.2 and 8.4 s. Fallback: a hard cut straight to the inside.
- **The walk-in is too tight to film from inside**:
  - From the door line the rails are only 0.4-0.7 m away, so the rail-filling shot is a close-up. The dollhouse view, hidden behind hard cuts, is the main reveal.
  - Fallback if per-frame camera visibility fails: drift south to about (0.65, 1.5) and look north, so the rails sit 1.1-1.7 m away. In that case the figure appears in the long cut only.
- **Mirror exit physics**:
  - A centre pivot would leave two gaps of about 0.33 m, and its open leaf would reach the room door's swing.
  - The offset pivot gives about 0.55 m clear and swings only 0.1 m into the entry corner. Back shelves stay at 8 cm or less.
- **Practicality gaps commenters will catch**:
  - The closet has about 1.6 m of rail.
  - Door A leaves about 0.1 m of aisle when open.
  - The ledge sits across the aisle.
  - All three are in the pinned trade-offs. Show door A's full swing in Zone 4.
- **A repeated template**:
  - The 11 m² bedroom already has a lift-up bed, lift-up flaps, a mirror panel and lit niches.
  - Here the bed is a drawer platform and the flaps are a pull-down rack, with new garment and bag colours.
  - Post at least a week from next_videos #2 and the reset video.
- **Not a loft**:
  - All 4 "recent_top" 2026 videos in the sample (290K-547K) are bedrooms or kids' rooms that report.md codes as raised or loft beds; the CSV frame notes confirm 3 of the 4. One is "Be bold! Raise the bed by 90 centimeters in a small bedroom!" (343,222 views).
  - The 0.45 m drawer platform raises the bed and adds storage, but it is not a loft. Next to next_videos #2 (walk-in plus loft), treat this as a deliberate test of the flush-wall premise.
- **Bench step for the flaps: not added.**
  - The flaps are gone (the template fix), and the rack comes down to about 1.3 m, so nothing needs a step.
  - A step drawer would also echo the 19.3M kitchen's beat.
- **Ten reveals that could feel like a list**: grow them in size, change the movement every time, and overlap two at once in places.

### Build notes

- **Reuse from rooms/bedroom/project.py**:
  - the shell builder: walls, window opening, day backdrop, floor bands landing far to near;
  - materials:
    - floor, oak and birch wood, mirror, led;
    - the mid-grey 'subfloor' (150, 147, 142) as the bare slab;
    - the cl_* and vac_bag pattern, with new colours;
  - helpers: garment(), stack(), plant(), cloth(), pillow(), disc();
  - door_z() and drawer_fn() (pop in, open, bounce shut);
  - the wardrobe's rail, garment, folded-stack and shoe code, for the closet;
  - the BLENDER look settings.
  - Not the lift-up base: this bed is a drawer platform.
  - Changes:
    - swap 'wall' (238, 233, 225) for a neutral white;
    - make the LEDs warmer (3000-3500 K);
    - use no sage, brass or fluting.
- **Reuse from rooms/kitchen/project.py**: the door() hinge helper and fill().
- **figure.py**:
  - one adult in mat='cl_denim' (88, 112, 150) for the walk in, a mid tone that reads against white;
  - a second instance in a teal material for the walk out, swapped with visible= while she is inside;
  - fg.walk, and fg.track with step_up and reach_up;
  - floor set to 0.12 m inside the closet;
  - visible= also hides her during the 15 s door pass and after she leaves.
- **camkit.py**:
  - hold_camera for every station. A hold that starts at the previous hold's end time gives a hard cut, used into and out of the dollhouse view.
  - Frame-1 pose, which is also the end pose: (2.85, 0.15, 2.10), yaw -35°, pitch -20°.
  - Overhead: (1.5, 1.6, 2.6), pitch -90°, yaw 0° to 16°.
  - Dollhouse: about (1.4, 2.2, 2.0) to (1.85, 2.15, 2.55), yaw -90°, pitch -35° to -50°.
  - Door passes run along y = 2.2 at 1.6 m.
  - Tune all poses with ROOM_REST test frames.
- **New pieces** (all boxes and rigid movements):
  1. A panel() helper: a flush panel with 20 mm gaps and a hinge type (side, bottom, top or offset pivot) that returns its open and close functions. It builds every flush panel.
  2. The closet block:
     - the 12 cm platform with its oak edge;
     - rails, and birch shelves that stop at y 2.6;
     - the LED strip, and an area light that switches on with door A;
     - a closet ceiling tagged as camera-hideable.
  3. The drawer-platform bed: 0.45 m high, with two push-latch drawers on the aisle side (drawer_fn).
  4. The pull-down rack: a shelf on two parallel arms that swings out and down (a custom animation with two pivots).
  5. A wave() function:
     - it orders the wave pieces by their angle around the room centre;
     - it plays their openings forward (the open wave), then their closings in the same order (the ripple);
     - thud strength stays steady, and the latch ticks rise.
  6. Cove LED pieces that switch on one after another, like the bedroom's LED pieces.
  7. A CUT=15|44 switch, so one project file holds both cuts. The finished room is identical in both.
  8. Camera-only visibility in blender_render.py:
     - it already sets visible_camera for lights; extend that to tagged meshes, keyed per frame (check with a 3-frame test);
     - the code-renderer preview won't hide them, so check the dollhouse view in Blender test frames.
  9. music.py:
     - a per-event tick pitch, because it now picks tick pitch from event order (980 Hz plus 140 Hz steps, cycling every 6);
     - a short "boing".
- **Effort**: medium.
- **Hard to render, and how to avoid it**:
  - There is no water, hair or face in this room.
  - The only draped fabric is the duvet: reuse cloth().
  - Garments stay thin rounded slabs.
  - Keep everything free of text, logos and marks:
    - book spines, the laptop and the print plain;
    - shoes plain (no stripes or swoosh-like marks);
    - the phone screen black;
    - the vacuum bags and suitcase without labels or badges.
  - The mirror adds noise: show it at an angle, with roughness 0.02.
  - **Render time** [inference from README.md's about 35 s per frame on 4 cores at 720x1280; the camera always moves, so the renderer copies no frames]:
    - long cut: 1,100 frames, about 10.7 h;
    - 15 s cut: 375 frames, about 3.6 h;
    - together about 14.3 h.
    - Reuse saves about 1.6 h, bringing it to about 12.7 h: the shared 0-3 s (75 frames, about 0.7 h) and the identical finale stretch (about 93 frames, about 0.9 h).
  - Render the 15 s cut first and check it with hh_metrics. Then render only the long cut's 3.0-40.3 s and copy in the shared frames.

## 8. Dinner for Eight

- **At a glance**:
  - Candidate C6. Judges' mean 6.0; reviewer score 6, given before this revision.
  - Plan ready, not started.
  - Posting: the 15 s cut on Tue 8 Dec, the long cut on Sat 12 Dec.
- **Why it ranks here**:
  - It is the slate's kitchen slot. The channel's biggest Short is a kitchen (19.3M) [measured], and eight plates in a ring is a premise anyone can count [inference].
  - 2026 kitchens peaked at 129K views [measured], so this is the December hosting post, not a growth bet.

### Pitch

Eight coloured plates land in a ring on the floor of an empty kitchen. The kitchen builds around them. Then its slim island stretches into a table for eight in three clacks, and eight folding chairs, each seat matching a plate, hop out of an oak wall that looked like plain cabinetry.

### Post caption (in the post, never on screen)

"Christmas dinner for eight in an 11.5 m² kitchen (3.2 × 3.6 m). No dining room. Would you host eight in here? And who gets the seat by the oven? Send this to whoever's hosting this year."

- If it posts after about 12 Dec, drop "Christmas" from the first line.
- First comment (pinned): the measured plan (below), its trade-offs, plus "Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app."

### Why it should work

- [measured] The channel's biggest Short is a kitchen: 19,269,469 views, 53 s.
  - Its late beats are a 3D woman on a pull-out step drawer (29-32 s; the second comment calls it "genius") and a hidden table that slides out as a chair lands (39-51 s).
  - This plan uses different mechanisms: a pull-down dish lift instead of a step drawer, and a table that unfolds out of the island with eight chairs from a wall.
  - Kitchens run at 1.00 era-adjusted, and 7 are in the era-adjusted top 30.
- [measured, my check of videos.csv; dates mostly estimated] Kitchens held steady in 2026, but with a low ceiling.
  - 1.03 over 26 Shorts for the year; 1.13 in 2026 H2 (n=11), against 0.71 for kids' rooms (n=9) and 0.97 for bedrooms (n=4).
  - Across all of 2026, bedrooms (1.37, n=15) and kids' rooms (1.32, n=23) still led.
  - The best 2026 kitchen reached 129K views, against 853K for a kids' room and 689K for a bedroom. The 2026 H2 kitchen median is 35K views.
  - [inference] So this is the slate's December hosting slot, not a growth bet.
- [measured] It builds a whole room and keeps moving.
  - 28 of 29 hits build or transform a whole room, against 12 of 30 flops. This one transforms the room twice: empty to kitchen, then kitchen to dinner for eight.
  - Hits move 8.4 per 0.2 s against 5.1, and their longest still is 1.4 s against 2.6 s.
  - Short only works if it never stops: the 24 s two-beds room (991,555 views) had the most motion of all 59 (15.5).
- [measured, confounded by account size] Premise videos have a median of 307,650 likes against 634 for plain showcases. Eight plates in a ring in an empty 11.5 m² room is a premise anyone reads without text.
  - [measured, n=1] Among genre rows with 1,000+ likes, the highest comment rate (5.0%: 288 comments on 5,717 likes) went to a hidden-seating concept, four chairs that tuck away to look like cabinetry (tagged "kitchen (hidden seating)", not a whole kitchen). That was an AI concept. [inference] Ours wins by being buildable: real dimensions and physical landings.
- [fact, via search snapshots; indicative] Christmas content on social runs from 1 Nov (NewEngen, 19 Nov 2026), and #cozychristmas is up 170%+ (TikTok newsroom).
  - [measured] 0 of 298 Haven Haus titles are holiday titles, so the slot is open.
  - [inference] The styling stays winter-neutral, so it can still run as a family-dinner video if it slips.

### The room

- Size and shape: 11.5 m², a 3.2 × 3.6 m rectangle, with a 2.6 m ceiling.
  - **West wall (long)** holds the whole kitchen: fronts 0.60 m deep, worktop 0.62 m deep. From south (door) to north (window):
    - a 0.45 m tall pull-out pantry;
    - a 0.6 m fridge column, with a vent grille in the plinth and a gap above (this answers the "no room to vent" complaint, 167 likes). The two tall units stand together by the door;
    - a 0.6 m sink unit with pull-out bins;
    - a 0.45 m slim dishwasher;
    - a 0.6 m "dinner column": a pegged plate drawer over a pan drawer; above the worktop a lift-up tumbler cabinet, and a top cabinet with a pull-down dish lift holding the platter;
    - a 0.7 m oven and hob with a slim hood, with the dinner column's 0.6 m of worktop as its landing space;
    - a 0.2 m spice pull-out.
    - Upper cabinets run to the ceiling. The splashback is clay.
  - **East wall (long)**: flush light-oak panels 0.15 m deep that read as plain cabinetry.
    - The middle 2.3 m (y 0.65-2.95 m) hides eight folding chairs, hung flat in a 4 × 2 grid behind two bi-fold doors (four leaves of about 0.575 m).
    - When open, the folded leaves stand at y 0.65 and 2.95 m, about 0.25 m clear of the east-end chairs.
    - The south 0.65 m is a broom cupboard.
    - The north 0.65 m is open shelving, 0.25 m deep, for cookbooks.
  - **North wall (short)**: a 1.2 m window centred on the table, sill at 0.95 m, snow outside.
  - **South wall (short)**: a 0.85 m doorway on the same centre line, with a pocket door, so no door swings into the room.
  - **Middle**: a slim island with a 1.2 × 0.85 m oak top at dining height (0.76 m) on a 0.14 m steel-cored spine. A folded leaf hides under the top. Extended, it becomes a 2.0 × 0.85 m table.
  - **Measured plan (pin it)**:
    - Table at x 1.45-2.30 m, y 0.80-2.80 m.
    - Three seats a side at 0.67 m spacing, plus one at each end.
    - Clearances: 0.83 m from the table edge to the worktop (0.85 m to the base fronts), 0.75 m to the chair wall, 0.80 m at each end.
    - Knee room: about 0.35 m each side of the spine, a little under the usual 0.40 m.
    - With the island closed, the cooking aisle is 0.83 m: one cook at a time.
    - Say the trade-offs openly: "Seats eight, seated. Cook first, then sit: once everyone is in, the oven and dishwasher stay shut and nobody passes the host. The seat by the door gets up for latecomers."
- Palette:
  - Shell: clean white walls and ceiling (not cream); matte white upper cabinets and tall columns; pale-grey worktop; floor of pale white-oak planks.
  - Wood: light oak base fronts, island and table top, chair frames and the whole chair wall (the biggest surface in the wow shots).
  - One accent: a muted clay splashback, kept to a strip behind the run.
  - Colour comes from what fills the storage:
    - eight plates, eight tumblers and eight chair seats in eight muted glazes (sage, celery, dusty blue, slate, clay, ochre, plum-grey, butter), so each chair matches a plate;
    - bowls, mugs and tea jars in the uppers; pantry jars (lentils, pasta, rice, beans, tea) and coloured spice lids;
    - cookbooks; one red and one sage enamel pot;
    - oranges and green apples; a small potted fir with a few red berries.
  - No readable text on any prop: plain colour-block book spines, unlabelled jars, a blank oven display, no appliance badges, plain knobs.
  - Avoid walnut, warm-white or cream fronts, red-and-gold, and patterns. The runner is plain linen.
  - Targets: saturation 0.17-0.38 and warmth 22-57. Our kitchen v2, with a similar white shell, measured 0.11 and 14.5.
- Light:
  - Daylight: soft, bright snow light through the north window, low warm sun on the snow outside, and a warm fill (about 3,500-4,000 K) from the doorway. Aim for brightness of about 150-165 (the band is 138-178).
  - Lights-on (12.2 s in the short cut, 42.2 s in the long cut): a white dome pendant over the table, LED strips under the upper cabinets and in the open shelves.
  - Outside, the snow turns to a warm low-sun dusk while the room stays bright (not a cool blue dusk). Grade the pendant so warmth stays at 57 or under.

### Frame 1 and the hook

- **0.0 s**: the empty kitchen seen high from the north-east corner (about 2.0 m up, pitched down about 25°), looking back toward the doorway, the camera already gliding. A bare pale-grey concrete floor (screed), white walls, warm light spilling in through the doorway. Nothing else.
  - This is a new axis for us: kitchen v2 opened from the doorway looking at a centred window.
- **0.30 s**: a sage plate drops onto the bare floor with a ceramic tick, at the place where the north-west seat will be.
- **0.4-1.0 s**: seven more plates follow, one every 0.1 s, each a different glaze, each with the same ceramic clink, panned left to right as the ring goes round. They land in a ring that traces where the table will stand: three a side and one at each end. By 1.0 s, eight colours sit in an empty room, easy to count: eight people, no table.
- The floor, the run and the island then build around the ring. The island drops in whole in the ring's centre, and the plates hop up into a stack on its top.

### 15 s cut

- 0.0-3.0 s · S1 north-east corner:
  - the plate ring, eight plates (0.3-1.0 s);
  - light-oak planks slap down from far to near; each plate hops 1-2 cm with a tick as its plank lands under it (0.6-1.4 s);
  - base carcasses slide out of the west wall, and the pantry and fridge columns slide in whole by the door (1.3-2.0 s);
  - the island drops in whole in the ring's centre, and the plates hop up into a stack on it with ticks (2.0-2.5 s);
  - the worktop drops, and the plain oak panel wall slides out on the left (2.6-2.95 s).
  - Camera: high, slow push-in toward the doorway.
- 3.3-7.0 s · S2 the run:
  - the plate drawer slides out, the stack hops across the aisle onto its pegs with eight settling ticks (3.35-3.9 s), and the drawer shuts (4.0 s);
  - the upper cabinets drop, and the hob, hood and sink land with thuds alongside them (4.1-4.6 s);
  - the tall pantry slides out and fills shelf by shelf with colourful jars (4.7-6.2 s), then slides back (6.5 s);
  - the clay splashback ripples on (6.6-6.9 s).
  - Camera: high on the east side of the island, 2.0-2.4 m from the fronts, sliding south and turning left from the dinner column to the pantry.
- 7.3-10.7 s · S3 over the island:
  - clack 1: the top splits and the halves glide apart (7.5 s);
  - clack 2: a folded leaf rises and opens flat (7.9 s);
  - clack 3: four legs fold down (8.2 s);
  - the plate drawer opens (8.25 s), the plates hop out onto the eight places, clockwise, with the same panned clinks (8.3-8.8 s), and it shuts (8.9 s), before any chair lands in front of it;
  - the oak wall's bi-fold doors open on eight folded chairs in a 4 × 2 grid (8.4 s);
  - the chairs hop out one every 0.2 s (8.6-10.0 s), east side first and west side last, unfold, and land at the place whose plate matches their seat, in eight woody thuds; all have landed by 10.3 s;
  - the doors fold shut on the empty wall (10.5 s).
  - **wow** at about 7.5-10.3 s.
  - Camera: high three-quarter view from the south-west corner, slow orbit to the right.
- 11.0-15.0 s · S4 wide:
  - the runner rolls down the table centre, stopping short of the end places (11.0-11.3 s);
  - the lift-up opens and the tumblers drop beside the plates, clockwise, with ticks (11.3-11.9 s);
  - lights on at about 12.2 s;
  - fruit, bread and the potted fir land in the centre, and the host pops in and sits at the window-end seat, by the hob (12.3-12.8 s);
  - eight napkins flop onto the plates with soft ticks (13.0-13.6 s);
  - red berries pop onto the fir and the host lifts a tumbler (13.8-14.3 s).
  - Camera: one drift from high above the east-side chairs, north and round to the frame-1 corner pose, at 0.3 m/s or faster, so the loop cuts from the feast to the empty room.

### Long cut (40-50 s)

46.0 s.

- Structure: 7 zones of 6.1-6.6 s, 14 camera angles of 2.0-3.8 s joined by 0.3 s swings, and the wow at 60-68% of the runtime.
- 0.0-6.3 s · Zone 1, shell:
  - Angle 1 (0.0-3.0), north-east corner, high push-in: the plate ring (0.3-1.0); planks from far to near, each plate hopping as its plank lands (0.9-2.6).
  - Angle 2 (3.3-6.3), high on the east side, sliding north along the run: base carcasses slide out south to north (3.4-4.2); worktop (4.4); the upper cabinets drop, and two doors flip open on coloured bowls, mugs and tea jars, then shut (4.6-5.4); the island drops in whole in the ring's centre and the plates hop up into a stack (5.5-6.2).
- 6.6-12.9 s · Zone 2, pantry end:
  - Angle 3 (6.6-9.6), high on the east side, sliding toward the south-west corner: the plate drawer swallows the stack (6.7-7.6); the pantry and fridge columns slide in whole by the door (7.8-8.4); the fridge's plinth grille clicks in under its vent gap (8.6-9.0).
  - Angle 4 (9.9-12.9), slow push-in: the pantry slides out (10.0), jars land shelf by shelf (10.2-12.0), and it slides back (12.5).
- 13.2-19.5 s · Zone 3, sink and hob:
  - Angle 5 (13.2-16.2), high on the east side, sliding north: three bins pull out (13.3-14.0) and go back (14.6); sink and tap (14.9-15.3); the splashback ripple (15.3-16.1).
  - Angle 6 (16.5-19.5), push-in: oven and hob (16.6); the hood slides down (16.9); the spice pull-out fills (17.2-18.0); the pan drawer opens full of pots and shuts (18.2-18.8); the lift-up opens on eight tumblers and closes (19.0-19.4).
- 19.8-26.4 s · Zone 4, the host and the plain wall:
  - Angle 7 (19.8-23.1), slow orbit around the dinner column: the dish-lift moment (see below).
  - Angle 8 (23.4-26.4), slide along the east wall: the flush oak panels slide out, plain (24.0); cookbooks fill the north shelves in a colour ripple (24.6-25.6); a small plant (25.9). This sets up the wall as "just a wall".
- 26.7-33.0 s · Zone 5, the **wow**:
  - Angle 9 (26.7-29.1), high orbit over the island: clack 1 at 27.6 s (60%), clack 2 at 28.2 s, clack 3 at 28.8 s; the plate drawer opens and the plates hop onto their places, clockwise (28.9-29.4), and it shuts (29.5).
  - Angle 10 (29.4-33.0), high slide toward the chair wall: the doors open (29.4); the chairs hop out (29.6-31.0), east side first, and have all landed by 31.3 (68%); the doors shut (31.6); a push toward the full table.
- 33.3-39.6 s · Zone 6, the setting cascade:
  - Angle 11 (33.3-36.3), slow crane down the table from the window end: the runner (33.4-34.0); eight napkins flop onto the plates, clockwise (34.2-35.4); the pendant drops on its cord and swings once (35.6-36.1).
  - Angle 12 (36.6-39.6), low at table height from the east, sliding north: tumblers from the lift-up (36.7-38.0); bread board, fruit bowl and potted fir (38.1-39.4).
- 39.9-46.0 s · Zone 7, finale:
  - Angle 13 (39.9-43.7), three-quarter view from the south-east corner, push-in: the host pops back in, serves and sits (39.9-42.0); lights on at 42.2; berries pop onto the fir and the host lifts a tumbler (42.6-43.4).
  - Angle 14 (44.0-46.0), a 2 s drifting wide to the frame-1 corner pose, at 0.3 m/s or faster.
    - A last berry and a tumbler clink land at 44.8 s, then the loop cuts to the empty room.
    - No end card.

### Storage moments

Ten moments; numbers 1, 2, 6, 8 and 9 are also in the 15 s cut.

- [fact, Houzz via secondary sources; indicative] Renovators add pull-out bins (64%), pantry cabinets (47%) and dedicated spice storage (41%). All three are in the default set.
1. Plate drawer: the pegged drawer slides out, the stack of eight hops in across the aisle, and it shuts (build). After the clacks it serves the plates to the table (reveal).
2. Tall pull-out pantry: slides out, fills shelf by shelf with jars, and slides back (build).
3. Under-sink pull-out bins: three bins, one in sage, slide out and back (reveal).
4. Spice pull-out beside the hob: fills with coloured-lid jars and slides back (build).
5. Pan drawer under the plates: opens full of enamel pots and shuts (reveal).
6. Lift-up cabinet: opens on eight tumblers in the plate colours, then serves them to the table (reveal).
7. Pull-down dish lift in the top cabinet: the host pulls it down to chest height and lifts off the platter (reveal, in use).
8. Island to table: the top splits, a folded leaf rises and opens, four legs fold down, in three clacks (reveal).
9. Chair wall: the bi-fold doors open on eight chairs in a 4 × 2 grid, the chairs hop out to the places that match their seats, and the doors close on the empty wall (reveal).
10. Uppers and open shelves: doors flip open on bowls, mugs and jars; cookbooks land in a colour ripple (build).
- Alternates if the holiday baking kitchen (trends #9; next_videos #3 and #10) also posts in December: swap 2, 3 and 4 for a slim cutlery-and-napkin pull-out in the island spine, a linen drawer for the runner, and a tray rack.

### The person moment

- **Who**: one faceless adult stand-in in light cool grey, `fg.figure('host', 'adult')` from pipeline/figure.py. No face, no hair. The eight colour-matched places stand in for the guests.
- **Long cut, 19.8-23.1 s**:
  - pops in at the dinner column (19.9, tick);
  - opens the top cabinet (20.2, swish);
  - `reach_up` to the handle, then `reach_both`, and pulls the dish lift down and out of the cabinet to chest height (20.5-21.3, soft hiss and a clunk);
  - lifts the oval serving platter off it (21.4-21.9);
  - pushes the lift back up (22.0-22.5, clunk) and sets the platter on the worktop beside the hob (22.6-23.0, tick);
  - pops out at 23.2 s with a tick, so no frozen figure stands through the wow.
- **Long cut, 39.9-42.2 s**: pops back in at the worktop, walks along the run with the platter, sets it on the table by the fir, and sits at the window-end seat (`sit_pose(0.45)`) as the pendant comes on. Then lifts a tumbler (42.6-43.4).
- **15 s cut**: pops in beside the window-end chair at 12.3 s and sits by 12.8 s, under the new light; lifts a tumbler at 13.8-14.3 s.
- **Evidence** [measured]: a 3D person uses a feature in 4 of the top 5, and the 19.3M kitchen's person-uses-a-feature moment drew the "genius" comment. Ours uses a feature Haven Haus never showed.

### Sound

- **Landing sounds**: thud for carcasses, the island and chairs (eight woody chair thuds at one pitch); tick for jars, spices, tumblers, napkins, the stack hopping into its drawer and each pop of the host; swish for drawers, pull-outs and doors; a chime at lights-on.
  - Plates: one ceramic clink at a single pitch, panned left to right as the ring goes round (0.3-1.0 s), and again as the plates land on the table. No rising count here: #9 Count the Beds keeps the rising-note count, and #4 Advent Stair has its rising scale.
  - music.py mixes in mono today, so the pan needs a small stereo change; without it, keep the single pitch and drop the pan.
- **Playful effects (two)**:
  - a ratchet clack-clack-clack for the three table clacks;
  - a soft hydraulic hiss and clunk for the dish lift.
- **Evidence** [measured]: commenters notice effects ("the Scooby-Doo sound effects", 214 likes).
- **Music**: original, at 120 BPM: brushed shaker, plucked bass and a celesta line.
  - Wintry but not Christmas: no sleigh bells, no carol quotes.
  - Duck the music slightly under the clacks.
  - [measured, weak evidence] Hits lean to 111-125 BPM.

### Risks and fixes

- **"Eight can't fit"**: a fight over the premise, like the 2.3K-like "it's not an apartment" comment under the 24 m² video.
  - Claim "seats eight, seated", and pin the plan with its trade-offs (0.83 m aisle, about 0.35 m knee room, oven and dishwasher shut once seated, the doorway seat).
  - End on a high corner wide where all eight chairs and plates can be counted.
- **Practicality pile-on**:
  - The hob now has 0.6 m of worktop beside it and sits away from the fridge; the fridge keeps its vent grille and top gap.
  - Keep "who gets the seat by the oven?": it is a good-humoured debate prompt.
- **A low ceiling for kitchens, and dining framing at 0.70**:
  - Treat it as the December hosting slot, not a growth bet. Post the 15 s cut first as a test.
  - Build the kitchen storage first: the first 7 s of the 15 s cut are all kitchen. The caption says "kitchen", and the table only appears at the wow.
- **Deadline**: post 8-12 Dec, after #4 Advent Stair; this overlaps #4 and #9 Count the Beds for the same weeks.
  - Never post it the same week as the baking kitchen (pencilled for 8-14 Dec in trends_nov2026.md); if both are made, use the storage alternates.
  - Render the 15 s cut first.
  - If it slips past about 12 Dec, swap the fir for a herb pot, drop "Christmas" from the caption, and render it fresh as an evergreen family dinner. Never post a re-cut: the 19 s re-cut of the 19.3M kitchen got 24K views and no comments.
- **Too close to the 19.3M kitchen, kitchen v2 and the baking kitchen**:
  - A pull-down dish lift replaces the step-drawer reach; frame 1 moves to the high north-east corner; the storage alternates cover a December baking kitchen.
  - I chose the dish lift over the reviewer's other option, a child on the toe-kick step reaching the tumbler lift-up: a 1.15 m child cannot reach an upper cabinet over a 0.62 m worktop, which would start the wrong practicality debate. It also saves the step drawer and a custom foot-hook pose.
- **Grey, cool or AI-looking render**:
  - Against grey and cool: the plate ring at 0.3 s, bowls and jars by 4.6 s in the long cut (pantry jars by 4.7 s in the 15 s cut), light-oak chair wall and fronts, chair seats in the eight glazes, warm doorway fill and warm low sun on the snow.
  - Against an AI look:
    - every item lands with a sound;
    - plates and tumblers leave their own cabinets, cross the 0.83 m aisle and drop from straight above;
    - each chair hops in a visible arc (about 2 m for the west side) and lands with weight; nothing assembles itself off screen.
  - Run hh_metrics.py on low-res station previews before the final render, and on both cuts after. Treat it as a guide, not a gate: with all 9 targets only 6 of 29 hits pass; the median hit misses 1 and the median flop misses 5.5. The 19.3M kitchen itself misses 3 (camera 1.75, first movement 1.2 s, length 53 s).
- **Dead tails**: late small landings run to 14.3 s and 44.8 s, and the final drifts move at 0.3 m/s or faster. Check that hh_metrics reports the longest still at 1.8 s or under.
- **Collisions**: the plates leave the drawer before any chair lands in front of it; the folded bi-fold leaves stand clear of the east-end chairs; the chairs land by 10.3 s and the doors shut at 10.5 s. Check each in a probe still.

### Build notes

- **Reuse from rooms/kitchen** (code only; not its layout, the 2.10 × 3.90 m L-kitchen):
  - the pull-out pantry pieces and their `pull()` and `fill()` timeline functions;
  - the drawer-with-contents pattern (`L1_dr`, `drop(..., parent=drawer)`);
  - the `door()` hinge helper;
  - the `mug()` and `bowl()` lathe helpers, for tumblers and bowls;
  - the fixed light-slot pattern.
- **Reuse from rooms/bedroom**:
  - FPS 25 and the camkit HOLDS pattern;
  - `drawer_fn`, `door_z`, `lid()` (for the lift-up);
  - `chair()` landing with wobble;
  - `stack()` for cookbooks and napkins;
  - `plant()`, adapted into the fir: lathe cones plus red sphere berries.
- **pipeline/figure.py**:
  - poses `stand`, `reach_up`, `reach_both`, then `walk()` and `sit_pose`;
  - `pop=19.9` for the entrance and `visible` off from 23.2 to 39.9 s, with tick events at both. `pop` scales a figure in only once, so use a second instance with `pop=39.9` if the return looks abrupt;
  - the platter follows `Figure.transforms(...)['host_farm_r']`.
- **pipeline/camkit.py**: `hold_camera` with 4 holds for the 15 s cut and 14 for the long cut. The frame-1 pose is also the last hold's end pose.
- **New pieces**:
  - the butterfly-leaf island: two halves that slide 0.40 m each way, a two-part hinged leaf, four fold-down legs, and a 0.14 m steel-cored spine;
  - the folding chair: three rigid parts (back with rear legs, seat, front legs), with one unfold-and-hop function reused 8 times with time offsets and a seat colour per glaze;
  - two bi-fold doors (four leaves);
  - the pull-down dish lift: two parallel arms and a basket that stays level (a custom verb);
  - 8 plates, 8 tumblers and 8 chair seats in 8 glaze materials;
  - an oval platter; napkins;
  - the runner: a thin box growing along y, plus a rolling cylinder;
  - the fridge vent grille; the pendant; a snowy exterior with low warm sun.
- **Effort**: medium-high: three new mechanisms (butterfly island, an eight-times folding chair, bi-folds), the dish lift, a full figure routine, and about 15 h of rendering in weeks shared with #4 Advent Stair and #9 Count the Beds.
  - Build only the 15 s cut's mechanisms first and reuse them for the long cut.
  - Render at 720x1280.
- **Hard to render, and how to avoid it**:
  - Glass: use opaque ceramic tumblers.
  - Food: spheres and ellipsoids only; no roast.
  - Cloth: a flat runner and folded napkins.
  - Snow: a bright exterior with a few static snowy branches; falling flakes only if they don't shimmer.
  - People: one faceless figure. No seated guests: eight figures would be a crowd.
- **Render time** [inference from README.md, about 35 s per frame on 4 cores at 720x1280]: the camera never stops, so no frames are copied.
  - 15 s cut: 375 frames, about 3.6 h.
  - Long cut: 1,150 frames, about 11.2 h.
- **Probe stills before rendering** (`ROOM_REST=1`): check that
  - the frame-1 and final corner pose shows all eight chairs and plates, and that the pendant and the seated host hide none of them;
  - the corner pose clears the open shelves;
  - the folded bi-fold leaves clear the east-end chairs;
  - the stack hop lands cleanly on the pegs;
  - the plate drawer is shut before any west chair lands;
  - the dish lift clears the hood and the host's head;
  - nothing readable shows on any prop.
- **Originality**:
  - The 19.3M kitchen is warm white, walnut and green tile with a checkered floor; it opens on a wall break and uses a step drawer.
  - Ours is white, light oak and clay, opens on an empty room seen high from the corner, and uses its own mechanisms (butterfly island, colour-matched chair wall, dinner column with a dish lift).
  - No on-screen text and no end card.

## 9. Count the Beds

- **At a glance**:
  - Candidate C7. Judges' mean 5.8; reviewer score 6, given before this revision.
  - Plan ready, not started.
  - Posting: the holiday 15 s cut on Fri 11 Dec, the evergreen long cut on Wed 30 Dec.
- **Why it ranks here**:
  - A kids' room with a raised bed, a family premise, and a double that rolls toward the lens [inference].
  - The evidence for the framing is weak. In 2026, "two kids" titles ran 0.70 (n=8); the channel's only two guest-room titles (est. Aug 2025 and Jan 2026) ran 0.70 and 0.83 [measured]. The holiday hook also expires.

### Pitch

A whole room for two kids, with a raised bed, builds on a podium. The guests' bags skid in through the open door at 0.3 s. Then more beds come out of the podium. Each sleeper's reading light clicks on with a note one step higher, until 10 m² sleeps five and everyone can still reach the door without climbing over a bed.

### Post caption (in the post, never on screen)

"Everyone says skip the podium in a small room. This one holds 2 more beds. 10 m² (3.4 × 3.0 m, about 11 × 10 ft), 2 kids + 3 guests for the holidays. Would you give grandpa the pull-out or the top bunk? Send this to whoever's hosting the family."

- Evergreen long cut: the same, but replace "for the holidays" with "when the family stays over".
- Pinned comment: the night plan with dimensions; "Every sleeper reaches the door without crossing a bed."; "Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app."

### Why it should work

- **Best category, with a raised bed, but cold in 2026** [measured]:
  - Over all time, kids' rooms run at 1.35 times normal, "two kids, one room" titles at 1.36 and titles that name a size at 1.34.
  - Every 2026 hit was a bedroom or kids' room with a raised or loft bed.
  - The 8 m² kids' room (5,673,214 views) and the 24 m² flat (5,513,915) both open by building a steel frame.
  - Counter-figure: in 2026, "two kids" titles ran at only 0.70 (n=8, era-adjusted, mostly estimated dates). 4 of the 5 such titles since Aug 2026 got 17,000-19,000 views. So the room alone won't carry it; the mechanism and the premise have to.
  - Outside Haven Haus, HomeCraft's "Two kids, one room" post shows 22.5K likes, against 260-924 for its single-bedroom posts [fact; search snapshot, indicative].
- **A bed rolling toward the lens should make big motion** [inference, from measured facts]:
  - The 24 s "Two Beds in One Small Room with Smart Wardrobe & Pull-Out Storage" (991,555 views) had the highest motion of all 59 Shorts: 15.5, against medians of 8.4 for hits and 5.1 for flops [measured].
  - Its title says "Pull-Out Storage", and no roll-out bed is recorded, so the link to a roll-out bed is an inference.
  - Its camera score (11.88) is 2nd of 59, behind a 6,836-view dimension guide (12.1). Camera motion helps, but it doesn't make a hit on its own [measured].
  - The hit's camera moved more than its flop's in 21 of 25 pairs [measured].
- **A premise you can see and hear**:
  - Premise videos have a median of 307,650 likes against 634 for plain showcases, though account size confounds this [measured].
  - "1 Billion Kids Bedroom Design" has 395.6K likes and 7,581 comments [snapshot, indicative].
  - YouTube counts every replay as a view [fact].
  - Five reading lights click on, each with a rising note, so muted viewers can count by eye and others by ear. Both may replay to check [inference].
- **It answers the debate before it starts**:
  - The top comments attack practicality: a fridge with no room to vent (167 likes), "how do you make your bed?" (32) [measured].
  - The double pops up to 50 cm, a normal sitting height, which answers "grandpa's knees". A steel frame with no post over the hollow answers "what holds the bunk up?". The night step answers "how do kids get down?" [inference].
  - Pinterest and TikTok now let users turn AI content down [fact, trends file]; originals with real dimensions and believable mechanics should stand out [inference, trends file].
- **Timely and easy to send**:
  - Christmas content on social runs from 1 Nov [fact, NewEngen, 19 Nov 2026]. #cozychristmas is up 170%+ [fact, TikTok newsroom, platform-reported].
  - Sellers push wall beds and guest-room conversions for holiday hosting [fact, weak evidence].
  - Sends drive reach to non-followers (Mosseri) [fact].
  - "The relatives are coming" is a "this is us" family premise that people send on [inference].

### The room

- **Size and shape**: 10.2 m², 3.4 m west-east × 3.0 m north-south, with a 2.7 m ceiling.
  - Coordinates: x runs east from the west wall (0-3.4); y runs north from the south wall (0-3.0).
  - **North wall**: a window 1.0 m wide (x 0.6-1.6, sill at 1.0 m), above where the double parks at night. Snow-capped rooftops outside in the holiday cut only.
  - **East side**: a 0.45 m podium, 1.5 m deep (x 1.9-3.4), running the full 3.0 m. That is 4.5 m², 44% of the floor.
    - On it, from y 1.05 to 2.95, foot ends to the south: the low bed (80 × 190 cm) at x 2.6-3.4, and the place for the futon (70 cm wide) at x 1.9-2.6.
