    - Inside, under both: a 1.25 × 1.9 m hollow that holds the 120 × 190 cm double on castors. A steel sub-frame in the deck spans the hollow, and an LED strip lights it.
    - **Raised bed**, above the low bed: a pale-grey steel deck 0.85 × 2.5 m with its top at 1.6 m (x 2.55-3.4, y 0.5-3.0).
      - It holds an 80 × 190 cm mattress plus a 0.6 m porch at its foot, where the stair arrives.
      - It is held by a steel ledger on the east wall, a bracket on the north wall and one corner post at about (2.5, 0.5). The post stands on the solid south end of the podium and doubles as a handhold. No post stands over the hollow.
      - About 0.9 m of headroom over each kids' mattress.
    - **Landing** (y 0.55-1.05, the full podium width): both kids' foot ends and the futon's open onto it.
      - A 0.8 × 0.5 m lid in its east half hides the folded futon.
      - A pull-out step drawer in its front face gives a 0.22 m half-step down at night.
      - The porch above leaves about 1.05 m clear over the landing's east half.
    - **Stair**, along the south wall (y 0-0.5): four blocks climbing west to east, with steps at 0.68, 0.91, 1.14 and 1.37 m (0.23 m rises). Each riser holds a toy drawer, and a cubby for two nesting stools sits under the first step.
  - **West wall**:
    - A 0.75 m pocket door at the south end (y 0.1-0.85, with the pocket at y 0.85-1.6), so nothing swings or slides into the room.
    - A fold-down desk for two in a 0.12 m wall niche (y 1.7-3.0, 1.3 m wide), with slim pigeonholes. Folded, it sits flush with the wall.
    - Top cabinets at 2.2-2.7 m, clear of the door head: linen over the door, the kids' wardrobe with a pull-down rail in the middle, and the guest cabinet over the desk.
  - **Night plan, every bed out**:
    - The double rolls 1.27 m west to x 0.7-1.9, y 1.05-2.95, with its head to the north wall.
    - A 0.7 m corridor runs along the west wall beside it, and nothing sticks out of the wall.
    - The entry zone (x 0-1.9, y 0-1.05) stays clear, apart from the night step at its east edge (0.35 × 0.45 m).
    - The futon and the low bed lie side by side on the podium.
    - Everyone reaches the door without climbing over a bed:
      - the raised-bed kid by the porch, the stair and the landing;
      - the low-bed kid by sliding out of the foot end, under the porch, onto the landing;
      - the cousin straight off the futon's foot end onto the landing;
      - all three then use the night step and the entry zone;
      - the grandparents step off the double's foot into the entry zone.
    - Plan key (1 character = 0.1 m across, 0.25 m down; the gap in the west wall is the pocket door):
      - `.` corridor, `D` double, `C` futon, `B` low bed under the raised bed;
      - `_` landing (folded futon under its east half, the porch above);
      - `T` night step pulled out, `o` the raised bed's corner post;
      - `1`-`4` stair steps (toy drawers in the risers, stool cubby under step 1).
    ```
         window (north wall, x 0.6-1.6)
    +----------------------------------+
    |.......DDDDDDDDDDDDCCCCCCCBBBBBBBB|
    |.......DDDDDDDDDDDDCCCCCCCBBBBBBBB|
    |.......DDDDDDDDDDDDCCCCCCCBBBBBBBB|
    |.......DDDDDDDDDDDDCCCCCCCBBBBBBBB|
    |.......DDDDDDDDDDDDCCCCCCCBBBBBBBB|
    |.......DDDDDDDDDDDDCCCCCCCBBBBBBBB|
    |.......DDDDDDDDDDDDCCCCCCCBBBBBBBB|
    |.......DDDDDDDDDDDDCCCCCCCBBBBBBBB|
    |               TTTT_______________|
                    TTTT______o________|
                        111122223333444|
                        111122223333444|
    +----------------------------------+
    ```
- **Palette**:
  - Shell: neutral white walls and ceiling. Not the bedroom project's "warm white", which sits at the edge of the cream zone.
  - Wood: light oak for the floor, the podium deck and fronts, and the desk flap.
  - Steel: pale grey, painted rather than metallic.
  - Accent: slate, the only one, including one slate front (the top stair drawer). Oat counts as a neutral. This is a new accent, unlike #1 Tape Line's sage and blue.
  - Bedding, one colour per bed: slate for the raised bed and oat for the low bed. The futon is white with a slate pillow; the double has a white sheet and an oat throw.
  - Clear colour from storage, on screen early:
    - the slate-blue suitcase at 0.3 s;
    - clear (not muted) book spines;
    - coloured toy bins;
    - the kids' clear-coloured clothes on the pull-down rail.
  - Leave out: walnut, cream cast, pink, tartan, saturated walls, and any tree, string lights or ribbons.
  - Stay inside the hits' band: saturation 0.17-0.38, warmth 22-57 [measured].
- **Light**:
  - Daylight: soft winter daylight through the north window plus a large, bright ceiling fill, so the white shell reads clean. Target brightness is 138-178.
  - Five reading lights, one per sleeper (two for the double), click on during the build as each bed is made.
  - Lights on near the end:
    - the downlights stay at full strength;
    - the window turns a warm peach dusk (not blue, which would cool the frame);
    - the five reading lights swell into five warm pools.
  - Exposure stays bright throughout: the dusk final frames must still measure brightness 138 or more.

### Frame 1 and the hook

- **0.0 s**: the empty white room seen from the open doorway, low (about 1.1 m), with the camera already gliding in over the threshold. In the holiday cut, snowy rooftops show in the window at the left edge. An original two-note doorbell sounds.
- **0.3 s**: a slate-blue cabin suitcase skids in past the lens and thuds onto the bare floor. It is the first colour on screen.
- **By 0.7 s**: an oat suitcase and a pale-grey backpack with a rolled oat sleeping bag follow.
- The question needs no text: who is moving in, and where will they all sleep? The premise is a picture, so muted viewers get it too; the doorbell is only a bonus.
- Why a low dolly through the door: The Advent Stair opens from the high doorway corner on a snowy window. Coming in at guest height with the bags makes a different frame 1.

### 15 s cut

- 0.0-3.0 s · S1 doorway dolly (frame-1 pose):
  - 0.0: the doorbell.
  - 0.3-0.7: the three bags skid in and land, the slate-blue suitcase first.
  - 0.95, 1.1 and 1.25: the podium slaps down in three sections, north to south.
  - 1.4: the low bed's mattress.
  - 1.5: the steel ledger clanks onto the east wall; 1.6: the corner post bolts down.
  - 1.72: the raised deck with its porch; 1.85: its mattress.
  - 2.0, 2.12, 2.24 and 2.36: the four stair blocks drop, rising west to east.
  - 2.5: both duvets drop together (slate up top, oat below).
  - 2.65: the raised bed's reading light clicks on (note 1), and its pillow pops at 2.72.
  - 2.82: the low bed's light (note 2); its pillow pops at 2.9.
  - Camera: starts in the doorway, about 1.1 m high, looking north-east. It glides 0.6 m in along the south side of the entry zone, turning about 20° right so the stair is in frame when it lands.
- 3.3-7.0 s · S2 west storage wall:
  - 3.35: the top cabinet run slides in.
  - 3.5-4.2: the linen doors open, folded duvets and pillows stack in by colour, and the doors shut.
  - 4.3-5.1: the wardrobe doors open and the pull-down rail swings down, hung with clear-coloured kids' clothes. It lifts back and the doors shut.
  - 4.9: the desk flap drops; 5.1-5.9: its pigeonholes fill with books and pencil pots in a ripple.
  - 6.2-6.8: the guest cabinet opens, the two suitcases and the backpack fly up into it, and it shuts.
  - 6.9: the desk flap folds up flush.
  - Camera: in the east end of the entry zone, facing the west wall. It slides 0.3 m north while panning about 40° right, from the linen cabinet to the desk and the guest cabinet.
- 7.3-10.7 s · S3 the podium at night:
  - 7.3-7.9, bed 3: the landing lid lifts, the folded futon rises and slides onto its place, and the lid shuts.
  - 7.9-8.4: the futon unfolds north in three clacks.
  - 8.5: the futon's reading light clicks on (note 3); its slate pillow pops at 8.6.
  - **Wow, about 8.7-10.0 s**:
    - a latch clicks;
    - the double rolls 1.27 m out of the podium toward the lens with a rumble;
    - its four legs pop down and it rises from 33 to 50 cm (clack-clack).
  - 10.05: a folded oat throw flies onto the double and opens on a two-panel hinge.
  - 10.2 and 10.35: its two reading lights click on (notes 4 and 5), with the pillows following at 10.25 and 10.4.
  - 10.5: the night step slides out at the landing edge.
  - Camera: low in the entry zone near the door, facing north-east. It pushes in as the double rolls diagonally toward the lens, with the futon unfolding behind it on the podium.
- 11.0-15.0 s · S4 final wide:
  - 11.0-11.3: the camera swings back out through the doorway.
  - 11.4 and 11.55: the two grandparents pop in, seated on the double's foot end and facing the door; 11.7: a cane leans against the bed's corner (tick).
  - Lights on from 12.2 s:
    - 12.2-12.6: the window turns a warm peach dusk, with the downlights at full strength;
    - 12.6-13.4: the five reading lights swell in bed order, with a soft echo of the five notes.
  - Camera: glides in from 0.25 m outside the doorway to the frame-1 pose by 15.0. It moves inward, the same way S1 moves, so the loop reads as one continuous move. The doorbell and the first bag cover the cut back to the empty room.

### Long cut (40-50 s)

46 s, evergreen version: no snow in the window, and the evergreen caption. Seven zones of 6-7 s, each with two gliding angles (a new angle about every 3 s, 0.3 s swings). The wow lands at 28-31 s (61-67% of the runtime). No end card. No gap between beats is longer than about 1.6 s.

- **0.0-6.5 · Arrival and both kids' beds**. Angles: the doorway dolly, then a low slide north along the podium face.
  - As S1, but slower, plus the podium face panels, the porch guard rail, the stair blocks dropping step by step, and the window sill.
  - Notes 1 and 2 at 4.4 and 4.9, each with its kid's reading light; the pillows follow.
- **6.5-13.5 · Kids' storage on the podium**. Angles: a push-in from the entry zone on the stair, then a crane up beside the porch.
  - 6.8-8.4: the four riser drawers ripple open, fill with coloured toy bins and shut, the slate top front last.
  - 8.8-9.8: the book ledge on the porch rail fills with clear-coloured spines.
  - 10.2-11.0: a slim shelf under the deck at the low bed's head fills with a torch, a water bottle and a plain plush toy.
  - 11.6-12.8: the landing lid lifts to show the folded futon, then shuts with a soft knock (a tease for bed 3).
- **13.5-20.5 · West storage wall**. Angles: a slide north with a pan right, then a slow push-in on the guest cabinet.
  - 13.7-14.8: the linen cabinet fills.
  - 15.0-16.2: the wardrobe's rail swings down with clear-coloured clothes and lifts back.
  - 16.4-18.4: the desk flap drops, books fill the pigeonholes, and the stools land.
  - 18.8-20.0: the guest cabinet opens empty and holds 0.6 s while the camera keeps moving, then shuts.
- **20.5-27.0 · The guests' turn**. Angles: high over the stair looking north-west, then low on the landing looking north along the podium.
  - 20.6: an optional second doorbell.
  - 20.8-21.6: the bags, with the sleeping bag, fly into the guest cabinet.
  - 22.2: the desk flap folds up; 22.5-23.0: the stools nest and slide into the cubby under the first step.
  - 23.2-24.0, cutaway: a deck panel in the futon's place hinges up about 60°, and the LED strip lights the hollow. It shows the steel sub-frame and the stored double with a bare sheet, then the panel drops back.
  - 24.3-25.8: the futon (lid, rise, slide, then the flaps unfold).
  - 26.1: the futon's light (note 3); its pillow follows.
- **27.0-33.5 · Wow**. Angles: low in the entry zone facing north-east, pushing in as the double rolls toward the lens; then a high orbit from above the window, circling left toward the door wall.
  - 27.6: the latch clicks.
  - 28.0-29.6: the double rolls out in one continuous move.
  - 29.9-30.4: the legs pop down and it rises to 50 cm.
  - 30.8 and 31.0: notes 4 and 5, with the double's reading lights; the pillows follow.
  - 31.4: the linen doors reopen on camera.
  - 31.6-32.4: two folded duvets fly to the foot ends of the futon and the double, and each opens on a two-panel hinge.
  - 32.6: the oat throw lands on the double and opens; 32.8: the linen doors shut.
- **33.5-40.0 · The person moment**. Angles: a side view along the double's foot from the corridor, at 1 m height, sliding slowly; then a high diagonal from the doorway corner.
  - 34.0 and 34.2: the grandparents pop in on the foot end; 34.5: the cane leans against the bed's corner.
  - 35.0: one stool slides back out of the cubby beside grandpa; 35.3-36.3: he reaches as his mug lands on it.
  - 36.8-38.4: the night step slides out at the landing edge. Its lid flips to show slippers inside, then shuts.
- **40.0-46.0 · Lights and loop**. Angles: a slow rising orbit in the entry zone that ends near the door; then a swing out through the doorway (43.7-44.0) and the glide back in.
  - 40.4-41.0: the window turns a warm peach dusk; the downlights stay at full strength.
  - 41.2-42.4: the five reading lights swell in bed order (raised bed, low bed, futon, double ×2), with a soft echo of the notes.
  - 44.0-46.0: the glide into the frame-1 pose, moving the same way as the opening.

### Storage moments (6-12, numbered)

1. Stair toy drawers: the four riser drawers ripple open, fill with coloured toy bins, and shut (long cut).
2. Book ledge on the porch rail and a slim shelf under the deck fill up (long cut).
3. Linen cabinet over the door: duvets and pillows stack in by colour.
4. Kids' wardrobe in the top cabinets: the pull-down rail swings down, hung with clear-coloured clothes, and lifts back.
5. Fold-down desk for two: the flap drops, the slim pigeonholes fill, and later it folds up flush.
6. Guest cabinet: swallows the two suitcases and the backpack with its sleeping bag. In the long cut it opens empty first, as a tease.
7. Stool cubby under the first step: the stools nest in at night, then one slides back out as grandpa's night table (long cut).
8. Cutaway: a deck panel hinges up about 60° and an LED strip lights the stored double (long cut).
9. Bed 3: the landing lid lifts, and the folded futon rises, slides out and unfolds flat.
10. Bed 4, the wow: the double rolls out of the podium, pops up to 50 cm and gets its throw.
11. The linen doors reopen and two folded duvets fly to the futon and the double, opening on a hinge (long cut).
12. Night step: slides out of the landing's front face. In the long cut its lid shows the slippers inside.

The 15 s cut uses 3, 4, 5, 6, 9, 10 and 12.

### The person moment

- **Who**: two faceless grandparent figures from `pipeline/figure.py`, both adults: one light grey (`fig_adult`), one muted blue (`fig_blue`).
  - Their spines bend about 12° for a slight stoop, and a walking cane (a bent tube) leans against the bed.
  - No white figure: it would vanish against the white sheet and walls.
- **When**: they pop in seated on the double's foot end, facing the door, right after its lights and pillows: 11.4 and 11.55 in the 15 s cut, 34.0 and 34.2 in the long cut.
- **Why the foot end**: it is the only edge of the double that the doorway frame (frame 1 and the final wide) shows clearly. Their knees also land in the clear entry zone, so the corridor stays empty.
- **How it proves the feature**: `Figure.sit_pose(0.50)` puts their feet flat on the floor with their knees bent close to a right angle. In the long cut, the side view from the corridor shows at a glance that the pull-out sits at chair height.
- **Long cut extra**: a stool slides out as grandpa's night table, and he reaches (a pose track from sit to reach and back, about 1 s) as his mug lands on it. A mug reads at phone size; glasses would not.
- **Kept out**: no stand-to-sit move, no lying poses, and no kid figures. Four figures would be too much animation, and the kids are implied by their beds and lights.

### Sound

- **Landings**:
  - soft thuds for the podium sections, mattresses and suitcases;
  - metallic clanks for the ledger and the corner post (a tick layered on a light thud);
  - ticks for books, toys and clothes;
  - a swish on every opening door or drawer and a thud when it shuts;
  - a long low rumble as the double rolls, then two dry clacks as its legs lock.
- **Playful effects**:
  - An original two-note doorbell at 0.0 s, plus the optional second ring at 20.6 s in the long cut.
  - The count: five soft, marimba-like plucks rising C5, D5, E5, G5, A5, in key with the track. Each one plays as a sleeper's reading light clicks on: two kids, the cousin, then two on the double. A quieter echo plays during the lights-on swell.
- **Music**: original, at 120 BPM from `pipeline/music.py`.
  - Turn off the track's own pluck arpeggio (`"pluck_bars": []`). It plays in bars 0-1 (0-4 s at 120 BPM) and would mask notes 1 and 2.
  - No holiday layer: the holiday cues come from the snow in the window and the caption. No licensed or recognisable Christmas songs.

### Risks and fixes

- **Deadline**: the holiday hook expires after the holidays.
  - Post the holiday 15 s cut 10-12 Dec, at least 7 days after #4 The Advent Stair actually posts.
  - Render only the holiday 15 s cut and the evergreen long cut. The long cut can post in January.
  - Build it after The Advent Stair is locked. If behind schedule, drop moments 2 and 7.
- **"Fake" or "fire hazard" replies**:
  - Use real sizes (80, 70 and 120 × 190 cm) and a steel frame with no post over the hollow.
  - Show the lit cutaway (long cut), a 0.7 m corridor, the night step, and a route to the door for every sleeper without crossing a bed.
  - Pin the night plan with dimensions, plus "Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app."
- **Cold topic and weak framings** [measured]:
  - The evidence:
    - "Two kids" titles ran at 0.70 in 2026 (n=8), and 4 of the 5 since Aug 2026 got 17,000-19,000 views.
    - Kids' rooms fell to 0.71 in 2026 H2 (n=9; dates estimated).
    - Guest-room titles ran 0.70 and 0.83. Titles with fold, flip, transform or multi-function wording ran 0.81 (n=15).
    - Two 2026 two-kids rooms flopped at about 17.5K. One (17,521) spent its first 12 s on one bed; the other (17,591) was pink and opened on old furniture.
    - Podiums are what the genre argues against. Haven Haus's "Even a Tiny Bedroom Doesn't Need a Tatami—Don't Rush This Choice" ran at 17.5 times normal (about 1.0M views). HomeCraft posted "Stop choosing tatami for a small bedroom, here's what actually happens." on 12 Jun 2026 [fact, caption snapshot].
  - Fixes:
    - let the mechanism carry the video;
    - open the caption with the anti-podium line and "10 m²";
    - keep "guest room" and "transform" out of the caption;
    - build both kids' beds by 3 s;
    - keep the shell neutral.
- **Caption question (a fix only partly applied)**: the review suggested "How many can sleep in 10 m²?" as well as the anti-podium line. The anti-podium line already says 2 kids + 3 guests, so asking how many would read as bait again. Only the open question about grandpa's bed is kept.
- **Too grey or too dark** [measured]:
  - Our Blender kitchen measured saturation 0.11 with a white shell, and the pink two-kids flop measured 0.12. Dark looks marked 5 flops and no hits.
  - Fixes:
    - the slate-blue suitcase at 0.3 s;
    - clear book spines and coloured toy bins;
    - check saturation of 0.2 or more on a `ROOM_REST` still before animating;
    - keep the downlights at full strength;
    - check that the dusk final frames stay at brightness 138 or more with `research/hh_metrics.py`.
- **Repetition** [inference, trends file: never ship the same template twice]:
  - #4 The Advent Stair has a snowy frame 1 from the high doorway corner, a kids' loft with stair drawers, string lights on the loft rail, a small tree, red ribbons and rising pitched ticks.
  - #1 Tape Line, Turning Wall has two kids, sage and blue bedding, a raised bed and five stair drawers.
  - Fixes:
    - frame 1 is a low dolly through the door;
    - no tree, string lights or ribbons;
    - a slate accent on oat neutrals;
    - the stair climbs along the wall with drawers in its risers;
    - the count uses plucks tied to lights (5 notes, not 24);
    - post at least 7 days after The Advent Stair and at least a week apart from Tape Line, Turning Wall.

### Build notes

- **Reuse from `rooms/bedroom/project.py`**:
  - materials (oak, birch, linen and the pillow fabrics, recoloured to oat and slate);
  - `garment()`, `stack()`, `cloth()` and `pillow()`;
  - the timeline helpers `door_z()` and `drawer_fn()`;
  - the lift-up bed hinge (the `LIFT_PIV` pattern), for the cutaway panel, the landing lid, the futon flaps and the step lid;
  - the light cascade and day/night window swap (the `T_NIGHT` pattern);
  - the `BLENDER` settings.
- **Reuse elsewhere**:
  - from `rooms/kitchen/project.py`, the pull-out pantry slide (`PO`/`PB`) as the model for the double's roll-out;
  - `lathe()` for the mug;
  - `pipeline/camkit.py` `hold_camera` for every station, with `vfov=90` for the doorway stations so the double's head and the raised bed share the frame;
  - `pipeline/figure.py` for the two grandparents.
- **New pieces**:
  - the shell: neutral white, a pocket door with a short stretch of hallway outside it (for the glide back in), and the north window with code-built rooftops (boxes with white caps in the holiday cut);
  - the podium in three sections, with the hollow, its steel sub-frame and LED strip. Its front panel is the double's side rail, so it rolls out with the bed and needs no hatch;
  - the double: frame, slats, mattress, castors, and four pop-up legs with a 0.17 m lift;
  - the futon: four rigid flaps of 47.5 cm on three hinges. Folded, it is about 70 × 48 × 32 cm, which fits the 0.41 m-deep box under the 0.8 × 0.5 m lid;
  - the steel raised bed: east-wall ledger, north-wall bracket, one corner post, deck with porch, guard rail, book ledge and under-deck shelf;
  - four stair blocks with riser toy drawers, and the stool cubby;
  - the night step with a flip lid;
  - a three-section top cabinet run, including the pull-down wardrobe rail;
  - the niche desk with slim pigeonholes;
  - two suitcases, a backpack and a rolled sleeping bag;
  - two nesting stools, a mug, a cane and five reading lights.
- **Load path**: the review suggested posts on a solid strip east of the hollow. A north-wall bracket plus one post on the solid south end does the same job, with no post over the hollow, and keeps the low bed's side open.
- **Two small code changes, plus one config change**:
  - Count notes: tick pitch now cycles by event index (`freq=980 + 140 * (i % 6)` in `music.load_cfg`). Let `Timeline.ev` carry an optional note into `frames.json`, and add a `pluck` kind that uses `music.pluck()`, so the five notes rise on cue.
  - Doorbell: add a `bell` kind (two sine bells).
  - Music: the `MUSIC` block in `music.json` replaces the defaults whole. Copy all its keys (`drop_at`, `chords`, `roots`, `riser`), then set `"bpm": 120` and `"pluck_bars": []`.
- **Effort**: medium-high: about 15 new mechanised pieces, 2 figures and 2 new sound kinds.
  - At about 35 s per frame (README, 4 CPU cores; the camera never stops, so no frames are copied), the 15 s cut takes about 3.6 h and the 46 s cut about 11 h.
  - Render only those two versions.
- **Hard to render, and how to avoid it**:
  - Unrolling: none. The futon is rigid flaps, and the sleeping bag stays rolled.
  - Duvets: folded rigid `cloth()` stacks that open on a two-panel hinge. No cabinet-to-bed teleports.
  - A dark hollow: the panel hinges to about 60° and the LED strip lights the inside, so it stays clean at about 18 samples.
  - Falling snow: white rooftops only.
  - Faces and hair: the faceless mannequins.
  - Lying figures: seated only.
  - Many lights: the reading lights are emissive shades plus one or two real lights, to keep Cycles noise down.
  - Water: none; the mug stands in.
- **Order of work**:
  1. Make a top-down `ROOM_REST` still of the night plan with every bed out, the night step out, and the seated figures' knees blocked out. Check every route to the door and every clearance before detailing.
  2. Make a `ROOM_REST` still from the frame-1 pose. Check that all five reading lights are in frame and that saturation is 0.2 or more.
  3. Render the holiday 15 s cut first, then the evergreen long cut.
  4. Check with `hh_metrics.py`: motion 7.5 or more, longest still 1.8 s or less, camera 1.8 or more, and brightness 138-178, including the dusk final frames. Treat the targets as direction, not a gate: only 9 of 29 hits pass every target, and the median hit misses 1 against 5 for the median flop [measured].

## 10. The Kitchen Behind the Wall

- **At a glance**:
  - Candidate C14. Judges' mean 6.0; reviewer score 5 (the lowest of the ten), given before this revision.
  - Plan ready, not started.
  - Posting: the long cut first, on Sat 16 Jan; the 15 s cut on Wed 20 Jan.
- **Why it ranks here**:
  - It adds a kitchen-studio and a new hook, a wall that folds open on frame 1 [inference].
  - It opens on a closed studio, while 22 of 29 hits open on the empty room [measured].
  - 2026 kitchens trailed bedrooms and kids' rooms [measured]. So this is the January kitchen test.

### Pitch

A 14 m² studio holds a storage bed and a plain birch wall. On frame 1 the wall folds away, and a kitchen builds and fills behind it and on the east wall. Then lids drop over the hob and sink, the run becomes a desk, and a person sits down to work. In the 15 s cut the wall folds shut, the studio is calm again, and the loop opens it once more. The long cut ends on the lit, finished room.

### Post caption (in the post, never on screen)

"14 m² studio (4.0 × 3.5 m) for one: four folding panels close off the whole cooking run in a wall 65 cm deep, and two lids turn it into a desk. Closed kitchen or open kitchen? Save this for your studio." Pin a plan with dimensions that shows the duct route to the outside wall, plus "Modelled and rendered from code, no AI video, no design app."

### Why it should work

- [measured] **A proven hook with a new mechanism.** The channel's biggest Short is a kitchen that opens on a wall breaking away (19,269,469 views, 53 s, 220 comments). Of the top 5, four open on the empty room and one on that wall break. Across the 29 hits, 4 open on a finished room, that kitchen among them. Ours opens on the closed studio, and the wall starts folding at 0.10 s.
- [measured] **Kitchens can explode, but they were weak in 2026.**
  - Across the channel, kitchens sit at the era-adjusted median (1.00 over 54 Shorts) yet hold 7 of the era-adjusted top 30. Only bedrooms have more (8).
  - A kitchen built from the empty room reached 5,287,190 views (38 s, camera movement 6.25).
  - 2026, recounted from videos.csv (most dates there are estimated): kitchens 1.03 (n = 26), against kids' rooms 1.32 (n = 23) and bedrooms 1.37 (n = 15). The best 2026 kitchen got 129K views, against 853K for a kids' room and 689K for a bedroom. Every 2026 hit was a bedroom or kids' room with a raised or loft bed.
  - Kitchens ran 0.92 in the first half of 2026 (n = 15) and 1.13 in the second (n = 11). That second figure has only 2 exact dates and one 5.4x outlier, so it doesn't show a recovery.
  - [inference] So the studio bed and the size caption carry part of the bet, and this is a test post, not one of the best slots.
- [measured] **Whole room, moving camera.** 28 of 29 hits build or transform a whole room, against 12 of 30 flops. The hit's camera moved more in 21 of 25 pairs (motion 8.4 vs 5.1; longest still 1.4 s vs 2.6 s). [inference] This plan uses all four walls and keeps the camera gliding in every station.
- [measured] **Size and debate.**
  - Titles that name a size run at 1.34.
  - The 19.3M kitchen's top comment (167 likes) attacks the fridge's ventilation, so we answer that in the design.
  - [snapshot, n=1] One AI concept video of four chairs tucking into cabinetry drew 288 comments on 5,717 likes (5.0%), the highest comment rate among genre rows with 1,000+ likes. Weak evidence. Premise videos had a median of 307,650 likes against 634 for plain showcases (confounded by account size).
- [fact; indicative search snapshots] **Loops and sends.**
  - YouTube counts every replay as a view.
  - Awe drives sharing (Berger & Milkman, 2012).
  - 4 of the 7 paint-brand 2027 colours found are greens, which suits the sage tile.
  - [inference] Originals with real dimensions and believable mechanics stand out from AI-looking builds.

### The room

- **Size and shape**: 14.0 m², 4.0 m wide (west to east) × 3.5 m deep, 2.7 m ceiling. Axes as in rooms/*: x east, y north, z up. The layout is new (rooms/kitchen is a 2.1 × 3.9 m room).
  - **North wall**:
    - A kitchen enclosure 0.65 m deep (y 2.85-3.50) runs from x 0.84 to the east wall.
    - Four birch-tone bi-fold panels close it (each 0.64 × 2.45 m, 25 mm thick), across x 0.84-3.40.
    - A plain white bulkhead sits above the panels (2.45-2.70 m), with no slot in its face. The hood duct turns east inside it to an outside grille on the east wall.
    - Inside, left to right: a 0.20 m panel pocket (x 0.84-1.04), a slim dishwasher (0.45), the hob (0.60), the sink base (0.75, with a false front on top and a U-shaped drawer below), then a corner door (0.56, x 2.84-3.40) into a blind corner (x 3.40-4.00). Worktop at 0.90 m.
    - **Desk kit**: a 25 mm oak hob lid and sink cover live in a slot under the worktop's front edge. The sink cover runs on two runners and can stay 0.25 m proud as a knee ledge.
    - **Uppers at 1.45-2.45 m**, lined up with the bases (2.36 m in all):
      - a 0.45 m narrow cupboard;
      - the 0.60 m hood, its underside at 1.55 m (65 cm above the hob);
      - a 0.75 m two-door cupboard (1.75-2.45 m) over an oak jar shelf at 1.50 m;
      - a 0.56 m pull-down cupboard;
      - an open corner shelf over the blind corner.
    - The worktop under the pull-down stays clear: lowered, its base sits about 13 cm above it.
    - At the far left, the white 0.80 m bathroom door sits in a 0.84 m recess.
  - **East wall**:
    - The tall return is 0.60 m deep (x 3.40-4.00). From the corner: an 80 mm filler (y 2.77-2.85), the fridge column (y 2.17-2.77), then the larder (y 1.57-2.17) with five inner drawers.
    - Both have birch-tone doors up to 2.45 m, hinged on their south sides, so they open away from the corner and past 110°. The fridge's oak door is fixed to the fridge, so there is only one door to open.
    - Slatted vent grilles sit in the plinth and at the top. They are there from frame 0.
    - The window is at y 0.45-1.45 (sill 0.95 m, head 2.20 m), 12 cm clear of the larder.
  - **South wall**: the entrance door (x 1.50-2.40) is the camera's doorway. The rest is plain white.
  - **West wall, the bed**:
    - A storage bed: platform 1.40 × 2.00 m (x 0-1.40, y 0-2.00), top at 0.60 m (normal bed height, not a raised bed), with three deep drawers (fronts 0.62 × 0.45 m) facing east. The 1.35 × 1.90 m mattress has a white duvet, a sage throw and pale-blue pillows. The head is to the north, leaving a 0.85 m path to the bathroom.
    - A 0.65 m counter stool with a footrest stands at the bed's head as a bedside table (x 1.45-1.80, y 1.60-1.95).
    - Long cut only: a 0.30 m deep cupboard over the bed at 1.85-2.45 m, with two lift-up doors and an open book bay.
- **Palette**:
  - **Shell**: neutral white walls and ceiling (not warm white) and a birch-pale plank floor (`birch`, sRGB 226, 204, 170).
  - **The wall** (panels and tall-return doors): birch tone. As a material its red minus blue is 56, close to the 57 limit, so check frame 1.
  - **Inside**:
    - Base fronts in light oak (rooms/bedroom `oak`, 208, 172, 130; red minus blue 78). If warmth at 9.8 s runs above 57, switch them to birch.
    - Uppers, hood and bulkhead in matte white. Worktop in pale grey. Lids in oak. Black only on the hob glass.
  - **One accent**: pale sage tile laid in straight vertical columns (stack bond), inside the niche only. The sage throw on the bed echoes it.
  - **Colour from filled storage, early**:
    - bedding at frame 1, sage tile at 0.3 s, oak fronts at 1.05 s, lemons and green apples at 2.55 s;
    - green, blue and yellow jars and mugs at 4.4 s, plates and blue bowls at 5.0 s, teal and blue enamel pots at 6.1 s;
    - larder jars at 7.65 s (yellow pasta, green split peas, red beans, blue tins);
    - the long cut adds blue, green and yellow bottles, fridge produce, folded clothes and coloured books.
  - **Avoid**: walnut, warm white, terracotta, mustard, orange lentils, a checker floor or busy patterns.
- **Light**:
  - **Daylight**: the east window throws a soft sun patch across the floor (visible in frame 1). Keep the sun neutral or slightly cool, with a bright, neutral sky fill.
  - **Constant hidden fills**: reuse kitchen v2's LDOOR (the doorway light behind the camera) and LBOUNCE (bounce off the east wall). Add a soft area light inside the niche under the bulkhead. They are on in every frame, so they can't break the loop, and Blender keeps them out of the camera's view.
  - **Lights on**: at 11.6 s in the 15 s cut and 41.9 s in the long cut, inside the final wide.
    - An LED strip under the uppers and one in the jar shelf come on first, then the hood light.
    - The long cut adds a wall light over the bed.
    - Keep brightness 138-178 and warmth 22-57.
  - **The end (15 s cut)**: the inside lights switch off as the last panel closes, so the final frame matches frame 1.

### Frame 1 and the hook

- **At 0.0 s**, seen from just behind the entrance doorway with the camera already pushing in, the closed studio:
  - white walls, and the birch floor with its sun patch;
  - the head of the storage bed at frame left (white duvet, sage throw, blue pillows, drawer fronts), with the stool beside it;
  - a flat birch-tone wall of four tall grooved panels with shadow gaps, under a plain white bulkhead;
  - the white bathroom door at the far left.
- **At 0.10 s** the panels start folding left in a quick concertina, swinging out toward the camera.
- **By 0.3 s** two wooden clacks have landed (0.16 and 0.24 s), the right half of an empty white niche is open, and the first column of sage tiles slaps onto its back wall (0.30 s).
- **The loop (15 s cut)**: the final frame is the same picture, so every replay sets off the reveal again.

### 15 s cut

- **0.0-3.0 s · S1 doorway, the birch wall**:
  - The panels fold to the left (clacks at 0.16, 0.24, 0.32 and 0.40 s), and the stack slides into the pocket (0.44-0.62).
  - Sage tiles sweep right to left (0.30-1.00). Oak-fronted base units slide out of the back wall (1.05-1.65).
  - Worktop (1.85), sink (2.15), hob (2.30), a bowl of lemons and green apples over the dishwasher (2.55), tap (2.75).
  - Camera: pushes in about 0.6 m at 0.2 m/s, with 4° of yaw drift to the right. It stays low enough to keep the floor planks and the bed's drawer fronts in frame, so the landings happen about 2.5 m away.
- **3.3-7.0 s · S2 cooking run**:
  - Three white uppers fly in from the camera (3.35-3.75). The hood lands (3.95), and its duct telescopes up into the bulkhead (4.10-4.35).
  - Jars and mugs ripple onto the oak shelf (4.40-4.90).
  - The two-door cupboard opens (4.95), plates and blue bowls stack in (5.00-5.30), and the doors shut (5.40).
  - The corner door opens (5.55). Two shelves glide out of the blind corner (5.65-6.05), and teal and blue enamel pots land on them (6.10-6.40). The shelves glide back (6.50-6.80) and the door shuts (6.90).
  - Camera: slides east along the run at eye height, about 0.7 m at 0.2 m/s, with 3° of yaw drift.
- **7.3-10.7 s · S3 the larder and the desk**:
  - The larder door swings open to about 115° (7.35), so the camera in the south-west sees past it. Five inner drawers cascade out top to bottom (7.45-7.95), and jars and tins drop in (7.65-8.25). The drawers close bottom to top (8.30-8.70) and the door shuts (8.80).
  - **Wow at 8.6-10.4 s, the kitchen becomes a desk**:
    - The hob lid and the sink cover slide out of their slot and drop flush (thuds at 8.65 and 8.85). The sink cover slides 0.25 m forward as a knee ledge (9.00).
    - The stool glides over from the bed (9.00-9.45). A plain laptop with no logo and a lamp land (9.15, 9.30).
    - The figure pops in, seated (9.60). A mug lands (9.85), and the figure turns its head toward the window (10.05-10.40).
  - Camera: a slow orbit to the left from the room's south-west quarter, about 12°, keeping the larder and the desk in frame.
  - The figure is hidden from 10.75 s, during the swing.
- **11.0-15.0 s · S4 wide**:
  - The stool glides back to the bed (11.05-11.45), the sink cover slides back (11.15), and a herb pot lands on the corner shelf (11.30).
  - **Lights on at 11.6 s** (the LED strips), then the hood light (12.0). The lit room holds for about 2.1 s.
  - The panel stack slides out of the pocket (13.75-13.90) and unfolds left to right (13.90-14.40, four falling clacks). The inside lights go off as the last panel closes. The closed studio drifts on for 0.6 s.
  - Camera: the S1 line, starting 0.8 m further back so it drifts in through the doorway at 0.2 m/s. It reaches the frame-1 pose and speed at 15.0 s, so the loop has no jump.

### Long cut (46.0 s = 23 bars at 120 BPM)

- **Shape**: 7 zones and 13 angles.
  - Z1-Z6 run 6.0-7.4 s each, and Z7 runs 4.65 s.
  - a1-a12 run 2.6-3.7 s each, plus the 4.65 s final wide (a13).
- **Joins**: 0.3 s swings between angles. Use a straight cut, a zero-length gap in camkit, for the two turns of more than 120°: a10 ends and a11 starts at 34.95 s, and a12 ends and a13 starts at 41.35 s. The hits cut between zones.
- **Z1 · The wall opens and the run builds (0.0-7.4 s)**
  - **a1, 0.0-3.4**: the S1 build, slower: panels at 0.10-0.62, tiles at 0.30-1.30, base units at 1.35-2.05, then worktop, sink, hob and fruit by 2.95.
  - **a2, 3.7-7.4**, low slide east:
    - A U-shaped drawer pulls out around the sink trap. Blue, green and yellow bottles fill both arms, and it closes (3.85-4.85).
    - The dishwasher door drops, its racks glide out with plates and ceramic tumblers, the racks glide in and the door shuts (5.30-6.95).
- **Z2 · Uppers, hood and duct (7.7-14.6 s)**
  - **a3, 7.7-10.9**, push-in on the hob end: uppers fly in from the camera (7.75-8.15), the hood lands (8.35) and its duct rises into the bulkhead (8.55-8.85). Jars and mugs land on the oak shelf (9.10-9.80). A kettle and a tea tin follow on the worktop by the dishwasher (10.1, 10.4).
  - **a4, 11.2-14.6**, slide east: the two-door cupboard opens, plates and blue bowls stack in, and the doors shut (11.35-12.20). Utensils land on an oak rail on the tile (12.50-13.10). Oils and a plant land on the open corner shelf (13.40-14.20).
- **Z3 · The corner and the fridge (14.9-21.9 s)**
  - **a5, 14.9-18.2**, slow orbit to the left: the corner shelves glide out, teal and blue enamel pots and pans land, the shelves glide back and the door shuts (15.05-17.80).
  - **a6, 18.5-21.9**, push-in on the tall return:
    - The fridge door swings fully open to about 110° (18.60-18.95).
    - Its shelves fill with green apples, lemons, blue yoghurt pots, red and green peppers and milk bottles (19.10-20.30). The door bins fill (20.40-20.80), and the door shuts (21.10).
    - The push-in ends low, on the slatted plinth grille.
- **Z4 · The larder (22.2-28.2 s)**
  - **a7, 22.2-25.3**, low angle rising: the door opens to about 115° (22.35), five inner drawers cascade out (22.50-23.10), and jars and tins fill them drawer by drawer (23.30-24.80).
  - **a8, 25.6-28.2**, high angle, slow slide: flour and oats land (25.8-26.4), the drawers close bottom to top (26.8-27.5) and the door shuts (27.75).
- **Z5 · Wow: the kitchen becomes a desk (28.5-34.95 s)**
  - **a9, 28.5-31.9**, slow orbit around the desk. **Wow at 29.1-30.9 s** (63-67% of the runtime):
    - The hob lid and sink cover drop flush (29.10, 29.35), and the sink cover slides 0.25 m forward (29.55).
    - The stool glides over from the bed (29.60-30.05). The laptop and lamp land (29.80, 30.00).
    - The figure pops in, seated (30.30), and a mug lands (30.90).
  - **a10, 32.2-34.95**, close on the corner upper, with the figure in the foreground:
    - The pull-down cupboard glides 0.42 m down and 0.12 m forward with two shelves of bowls and mugs (32.35-32.80, a soft "pfff").
    - The figure looks up at it (32.5-32.9).
    - It springs back up (33.60-34.00, a small "boing").
- **Z6 · The bed wall (34.95-41.35 s)**
  - **a11, 34.95-38.2**, straight cut to a view from beside the closed larder, looking west-south-west at the bed, with a slow push-in:
    - The three bed drawers slide out (35.25-35.60). Folded clothes in blue, sage, white and yellow drop in (35.70-36.60), and the drawers close in a ripple (36.80-37.30).
    - The over-bed cupboard lands on the west wall (37.60).
  - **a12, 38.5-41.35**, slide south along the bed, looking south-west: the cupboard's two lift-up doors open on spare bedding and close (38.60-39.40), coloured books fill the open bay (39.50-40.30), and a plant lands (40.60).
- **Z7 · The lit wide (41.35-46.0 s)**
  - **a13**: straight cut to a view on the S1 line about 0.9 m behind the frame-1 pose, drifting forward at about 0.2 m/s so it reaches the frame-1 pose at 46.0 s.
  - **Lights on at 41.9 s** (the LED strips), then the hood light (42.4) and the wall light over the bed (42.8).
  - Late beats, so the tail never sits still: the figure lifts its mug at 44.0 s, and a herb pot lands on the corner shelf at 44.8 s.
  - The lit, finished studio stays on screen to the end, with the figure still at the desk. There is no vanish and no end card. The loop jumps back to the closed studio, as any build's loop does.

### Storage moments

1. **The wall itself** (both cuts): four panels fold into a stack and slide into a 20 cm pocket. In the 15 s cut they come back out and close.
2. **U-shaped sink drawer** (long cut): it wraps around the trap, and bottles fill both arms.
3. **Dishwasher** (long cut): the door drops, and the racks glide out full and back in.
4. **Upper doors** (both cuts): they open on plates and blue bowls, then shut.
5. **Corner shelves** (both cuts): two shelves glide out of the blind corner with enamel pots, then glide back.
6. **Fridge** (long cut): the one oak-fronted door swings fully open and the shelves fill. Vent grilles sit in the plinth and at the top.
7. **Larder** (both cuts): five inner drawers cascade out, fill with colourful jars and tins, and close in a ripple.
8. **Kitchen to desk** (both cuts): lids drop over the hob and sink, the sink cover slides out as a knee ledge, and the stool glides over.
9. **Pull-down cupboard** (long cut): its two shelves glide down to about 13 cm above the worktop and spring back up.
10. **Bed drawers** (long cut): three deep drawers fill with folded clothes.
11. **Over-bed cupboard** (long cut): its lift-up doors open on spare bedding, and books fill the open bay.
- That makes 5 reveals in the 15 s cut and 11 in the long cut. The oak jar shelf fills in both cuts, but it is a fill, not a reveal.

### The person moment

- **The figure**: one faceless adult stand-in, sitting at the sink end of the new desk.
  - Build it with `cook = fg.figure('cook', 'adult', pose='sit_desk', at=(2.45, 2.33, 0), seat=0.65)` and pose it with `cook.sit_pose(0.65, base='sit_desk')`.
  - At a 0.65 m seat, the sit_pose maths puts the feet on the floor. Check this in a probe.
  - It is light grey (`fig_adult`), so it reads against the sage tile and the oak.
- **Clearances**, using figure.py's body (about ±0.115 m deep, ±0.16 m wide, ±0.23 m with the arms):
  - the knees stop about 0.12 m short of the base fronts, under the 0.25 m ledge;
  - the chest is about 0.1 m behind the ledge's edge;
  - the hands hover about 8 cm above the laptop (lower the shoulders a few degrees if they float);
  - it is 0.7 m from the fridge column, and the larder is shut before it appears.
- **15 s cut**: it pops in seated at 9.6 s, turns its head toward the window, and is hidden from 10.75 s during the swing.
- **Long cut**: it pops in at 30.3 s, looks up at the pull-down (a neck keyframe with `track`) and is still at the desk in the final wide.
- **Why this feature**: cook, work and sleep in 14 m² is the studio's whole point, and the 19.3M kitchen never turned its run into a desk. It doesn't use that video's step drawer or have a person reach a top cupboard. It needs no arm solve: the figure only sits.

### Sound

- **Landings**: every piece gets a sound.
  - `thud`: panels, carcasses, worktop, lids, stool, bed drawers.
  - `tick`: tiles, jars, fruit, bowls, books.
  - `swish`: doors, drawers, the corner shelves, the sink cover.
  - `chime`: the lights.
- **Playful effects**:
  - The wall opens on four rising wooden clacks (0.16-0.40 s) and, in the 15 s cut, closes on four falling ones (13.90-14.40 s), so they bookend the loop. Make them as higher-pitched thuds (`thud` events with a raised `f0` override), which needs no new code.
  - The long cut's pull-down comes down with a soft "pfff" (a `swish`) and springs back up with a small cartoon "boing" (34.0 s). The boing needs one new sound kind in music.py, which has only thud, tick, swish and chime. Commenters notice playful effects: the "Scooby-Doo" comment has 214 likes.
- **Music**: original, at 120 BPM (set `{"bpm": 120}` in music.json).
  - The long cut is exactly 23 bars.
  - The 15 s cut is 30 beats (7 bars plus 2 beats), so the loop restarts on a downbeat at 0.0 s. The first clack follows at 0.16 s.
  - Cuts don't need to hit the beat: 23% of the hits' scene changes land on a strong sound, against 24% by chance.

### Risks and fixes

- **It could read as a remake** of the 19.3M kitchen.
  - Fix: no step drawer, appliance flap, slide-out table, bottle cupboard or person reaching a top cupboard. The wow is a beat that video never had (the run becomes a desk). The wall folds; it doesn't crack.
  - Birch, a sage stack-bond tile and a birch floor: no walnut, no checker floor.
- **The category is weak right now**: 2026 kitchens ran 1.03, against 1.32 for kids' rooms and 1.37 for bedrooms, and none passed 129K.
  - "Whole small home" titles are a gamble too: 5 Shorts at 0.70, though they include the 12.8M loft and the 5.5M flat. That flat's title also drew the channel's biggest title fight (a 2.3K-like comment).
  - Fix: lead the caption with the true size and a claim the video proves. Post it as a kitchen test, not in a best slot. No dining zone, sofa or TV (living or dining 0.70).
- **Frame 1 is the closed studio, not an empty room.** 22 of 29 hits open on the empty room and 4 on a finished room, the 19.3M kitchen among them.
  - Kept because the seamless vanish loop needs the bed in both the first and last frames. The room still moves at 0.10 s, and the kitchen side is empty behind the panels.
- **Grey, or too warm**: our kitchen renders measured saturation 0.11 and warmth 14.5, against targets of 0.17-0.38 and 22-57. A big birch wall and warm contents can tip the other way.
  - Fix: a neutral sun, a birch floor, and blue, teal and green contents. Check saturation and warmth on probe stills at 0.0 s and 9.8 s first.
  - Then run `hh_metrics.py` on both cuts. Treat a miss as direction, not a gate: only 9 of 29 hits pass every target (6 of 29 with the length target). The median hit misses 1, the median flop 5 (5.5 with length).
- **Dark or grainy recesses** (the larder, the blind corner, under the uppers).
  - Fix: the constant hidden fills plus the niche fill. Probe 0.0, 8.0, 9.8 and 12.8 s at final settings (18 samples) before rendering everything.
- **Practicality pile-ons**:
  - "Where does the steam go?": the duct rises into the bulkhead and, in the pinned plan, turns east to an outside grille. There is no slot in the bulkhead's face.
  - "Where do the panels go?": the pocket is shown.
  - The fridge: an 80 mm filler, a hinge away from the corner, grilles from frame 0, and the door opening fully in the long cut.
  - "No oven?": the caption doesn't claim a full kitchen.
  - Sitting at a counter: the 0.25 m knee ledge.
- **A broken loop (15 s cut)**.
  - Fix: the figure is hidden at 10.75 s, the stool is back by 11.45 s and the sink cover by 11.15 s. Everything else that changed sits behind the panels.
  - The tall return and its grilles exist from frame 0, the inside lights go off as the last panel closes, and the fills never change. Diff frame 0 against the last frame.
- **The camera movement is unproven.**
  - Fix: glide at about 0.2 m/s with 3-5° of yaw drift. Keep wood grain, floor planks and the bed's drawer fronts in frame, and push S1 closer.
  - Run `hh_metrics.py` on a 720p preview of the first 7 s before the final render.
- **Fixes not taken**: none was rejected. Two were overtaken by the new wow, because the person no longer uses the pull-down: the hand-on-bar arm solve, and the standing spot at (2.95, 2.45).

### Build notes

- **Reuse from `rooms/kitchen/project.py`**:
  - the material set (jar and jar_fill, tin, mug_sage, bowl_blue, plate, lemon, apple, pasta, beans, rice, oil, bottle_b and bottle_g, enamel, basin, hob);
  - the floor-tile row loop, turned to slap tile columns onto the back wall;
  - the slide and drop carcass build, the `door()` hinge and the `drawer()` overshoot;
  - the pantry `fill()` pattern, for contents that ride the larder drawers and fridge shelves;
  - the sink, tap, hob and hood models, the lights-on pattern, and the LDOOR and LBOUNCE lights (made constant).
- **Reuse from `rooms/bedroom/project.py`**:
  - `P()` stations, used with `pipeline/camkit.py`'s `hold_camera` at 25 fps;
  - roomkit's `wood_mats` with the bedroom's `oak` and `birch` settings;
  - the `pull()` motion of `wr_pull` for the pull-down, retimed to 0.42 m down and 0.12 m forward;
  - `drawer_fn` (larder and bed drawers), `door_z`, `stack()` (plates, folded clothes, books), `plant()`, `cloth()` and `pillow()` (the bed) and `appear()` (staggered lights).
- **Also use**: `pipeline/figure.py` (`figure` with `seat`, `Figure.sit_pose`, `track` for the head turns, `animate` with `pop` and `visible`).
- **New pieces**:
  - **The folding wall**: `fold(u)` makes a zig-zag. Hinge k sits at `x0 + k·w·cos θ`, and odd hinges move `w·sin θ` out into the room. Panels rotate ±θ in turn, and the free end rides the top track. Then the stack moves 0.64 m north into the pocket. Play it backwards to close.
  - **Mechanisms**: two kidney shelves on a two-step path; the U-drawer (three boxes around a tube trap); the larder's five staggered inner drawers; the dishwasher door and racks; the hob lid and sink cover on runners.
  - **Fixtures**: the integrated fridge with its 80 mm filler and slatted grilles; the telescoping duct (`grow` on z); the storage bed with three drawers; the over-bed cupboard and book bay; the counter stool; the LED strips, hood light and bed wall light.
  - **Sound**: one new "boing" kind in music.py.
- **Effort**: medium-high. It is still all boxes, lathes and existing animation verbs, with no arm solve. One `project.py` can choose the 15 s or 46 s cut with an environment variable. If time is tight, drop the dishwasher and the over-bed cupboard beats first.
- **Hard to render, and how to avoid it**:
  - No running water.
  - No steam: show the duct instead.
  - No clear glass: frosted jars with coloured fill, solid fridge shelves, ceramic tumblers.
  - Simple lathe fruit and box packets. A laptop made of plain boxes, with no logo.
  - A mannequin figure with no face or hair and no walk: it pops in already seated.
  - Offset each panel by its thickness so the fold doesn't intersect.
- **Render and check**:
  - Probe each station with `ROOM_REST=1`, then probe the busy moments listed above. Render the 15 s cut first, and get it through `hh_metrics.py` before starting the long cut.
  - The README gives about 35 s per frame on 4 cores (720x1280). At that rate the 15 s cut (375 frames) takes about 3.6 h and the long cut (1,150 frames) about 11.2 h: about 15 h of CPU in all, before the upscale to 1080x1920. The camera never stops, so few frames can be copied.
- **Slot**: January, as a kitchen test post. It isn't seasonal, so it can move, and it shouldn't take one of the best slots.

## Runners-up

- **The Floor Steps Up (C10, judges' mean 5.4)**
  - The idea: the back of an empty 9 m² bedroom lifts 1 m into a bed platform with a drawer stair, and a made-up trundle rolls out of the desk front for a friend.
  - Not chosen:
    - Its finished room repeats #2's 1 m storage platform with drawer stairs.
    - Its hidden guest bed clashes with #9. Its own plan says to keep only one such video, and #9 scored higher with the judges.
  - It is a good January backup if #9 is dropped.
- **The Five-Metre Room (C16, judges' mean 5.2)**
  - The idea: a 10.5 m² room with a 5 m ceiling. A loft deck drops on oak rods, and a 5 m book wall fills while the camera drops like a lift.
  - Not chosen:
    - It is high effort: hundreds of books, ten drawer rigs and about 1,225 Cycles frames in the long cut.
    - Whole-small-home titles run 0.70 (5 Shorts) [measured], and its own plan calls it boom or bust.
  - Keep it for a stretch slot, built in stages.
- **Scored but not developed**
  - C3 (judges' mean 6.2) is a close cousin of #1, and the slate can carry only one split-kids room.
  - C8 (judges' mean 5.2).
